# Pixel World — Development Plan

**Priority: build the world’s visuals and zoom journey first.** The app should open directly on a beautiful pixel Earth, then reveal a continent, a country, and a detailed capital as the player zooms. The latest GlobeQuest concept image supplied on 10 November 2026 is the visual reference.

The previous Phase 3 navigation/visit implementation has been removed: parchment panels, location pickers, settings, visit markers, saved visits, the global capital catalog, and its storage dependency. Those features return after the map experience is convincing. The globe and earlier foundation remain.

**Current work: Portugal → Lisbon, Spain → Madrid, France → Paris, Germany → Berlin, Netherlands → Amsterdam, Belgium → Brussels, United Kingdom → London and Ireland → Dublin are running in the app.** Zoom levels:

- **Continent board:** night sea, puzzle-piece countries, flora, fauna and mountains.
- **Country tier (Portugal):** a castle at Lisbon, church towns, houses, trees and boats at its real cities.
- **Capital:** an isometric Lisbon scene that grows out of Lisbon's location and can be panned.

Deep zoom is allowed only where detail exists.

**Priority from 20 November 2026: gameplay before more capitals.** 44 European capital scenes exist, but nothing to do in them yet. New capitals pause until the Phase 5 gameplay slice (country sheet → capital scene → "find it" → collectible card → saved progress) has been built on three capitals and playtested on phones. The product direction is in [PROJECT_VISION.md](./PROJECT_VISION.md).

## The experience we are building

| Scale | What the player should see | How it should feel |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Earth | Recognizable continents, blue oceans, green/arid/icy land, readable coasts, clouds, stepped lighting, a pixel atmosphere, and stars | Open the app into the full-screen universe; drag to spin and pinch toward a place |
| Continent | The continent fills the view; countries, islands, coasts, rivers, terrain, and major settlements become readable | Follow the same geographic focus as the globe fills the screen and curvature becomes less visible |
| Country | A recognizable country map with richer terrain, roads/settlements where data supports them, and a clear capital | Continue zooming into that geography; finer information arrives in the same palette and pixel treatment |
| Capital | A geographically grounded scene with streets, building footprints, water, parks, recognizable landmarks, and appropriate city scenery | Arrive in a place worth exploring, with a useful maximum zoom and a smooth return to the country |

The pilot route follows the reference: **Earth → Europe → Portugal → Lisbon**. It replaced Malta → Valletta on 10 November 2026; Malta's design mockups remain in `docs/art/phase3/`.

**Decision (10 November 2026): one capital per country, as an illustrated scene.** Each country shows a few buildings at its real cities at country zoom. Its capital is an isometric diorama in the reference's style and angle. The diorama arranges the capital's real landmarks in their real relative positions, compressed to fit one scene: it is an illustration, not a street-accurate map. This replaces the earlier plan for OSM-derived street and building footprints at capital scale.

The reference’s quest lists, avatar, XP, badges, statistics, and parchment cards describe the eventual game. They are later layers over the map. Stop geographic drill-down at capital cities for this iteration.

## Visual rules

1. **World first.** The map and universe fill the screen. Keep the opening free of large panels, pickers, travel forms, and game dashboards while building the scenery.
2. **One art direction.** Keep hard pixel edges, a coordinated ocean/land palette, coherent outlines, stepped lighting, and a consistent scale for decorative elements. The city must belong to the same world as the planet.
3. **Zoom reveals actual detail.** Increasing the size of the current globe texture does not create rivers, streets, buildings, or landmarks. Add the geographic layers each scale needs.
4. **Follow the place.** Preserve the point under the gesture, geographic alignment, orientation, and camera continuity. Use a gradual change of scale/detail; avoid a jump to a disconnected city picture or a differently styled atlas.
5. **Keep coarse coverage while loading.** Reveal finer detail when ready, retain the current view during loading, and bound memory. Do not blank or remount the map between scales.
6. **Separate scenery and source geometry.** Real geography anchors shorelines, roads, and landmarks. Decorative clouds/trees/color variation are illustrative; do not invent named places from noise.
7. **Keep coverage honest.** A capital overview is not a detailed city scene. Define coverage and useful zoom limits until a pack exists.
8. **Build only what is used.** Keep assets small, record sources/licenses, and measure compressed and decoded costs. Removed atlas/demo/sprite images stay removed.

## Phases and order

| Phase | Focus | Result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | App foundation | Expo app, routing, shared styling, and checks | Complete |
| 2 | Earth and universe | An attractive, responsive opening planet | Core implemented; visual refinement and platform validation active |
| 3 | Continent/country visuals and continuous zoom | Richer geography appears while zooming into the same world | Implemented for Europe |
| 4 | Detailed capital visuals | Recognizable capital scenes, one per country | 44 European capitals built; paused until the Phase 5 playtest |
| 4b | Living world | The world moves on its own: space and planet first, then board, country tier and capitals | Space, planet, board, country tier and capital water/gulls/cloud shadows done; capital smoke, flags and people need P5-01 |
| 5 | Gameplay slice | "Find it" loop, collectibles, discovery states, and saved progress on three capitals; playtest gate | Next |
| 6 | UI kit and game feel | Pixel UI components, scene animation, sound, real day/night, accessibility | UI kit v1 built early (24 November 2026, P6-01/03/04) so Phase 5 screens use it; sound, day/night and accessibility pass planned |
| 7 | World progress, Quests and Passport | Three tabs, clouds over undiscovered countries, daily place, travel track, stamps, My World | Planned |
| 8 | Resume capitals and release validation | New capitals ship with gameplay; platforms, performance, and offline use verified | Planned |
| 9 | After the first release | More challenge types, field guide, postcards, seasonal events | Backlog |

**Visual milestone before Phase 5:** demonstrate Earth → Europe → Malta → Valletta and back, with actual detail increasing at each scale. Minimal focus/back controls needed to demonstrate this belong to the visual work; visits, searchable global pickers, progress panels, and rewards do not.

## Current implementation

- Expo SDK 58, Expo Router, Skia 2.14.0, Gesture Handler, and Reanimated.
- Full-screen Earth and universe with drag, pinch, wheel, keyboard, and Home reset. The opening camera faces the Atlantic/Europe/Africa rather than an arbitrary destination screen.
- The Earth detail pass adds a brighter atmosphere and stepped outer glow, coastal shallows/sandy edges, forest-like green variation, arid colors, icy polar texture, restrained ocean highlights, and layered clouds with shadows. These surface treatments are illustrative; the geographic masks still anchor the real coastlines.
- Space contains a cratered moon, a satellite with solar-panel grids/body/dish/antenna, varied blue/white/gold stars, and faint background color. Objects move to suitable positions for portrait/landscape and sit behind the growing planet. The scenery uses shader code, with no new image files, dependencies, or decoded textures.
- One 4096×2048 Natural Earth land/border/lake texture: **222,387 PNG bytes**, approximately **32 MiB decoded**, excluding GPU/transient overhead. Country names are display labels only.
- The same sphere renderer stays active through zoom 0–5 (1×–32×). From zoom 1.5 to 2.5, one shader blends the daytime globe into the continent board.
  - Terrain pixels are 3 points on phones at every zoom (`CELLS_ACROSS`).
  - Sea bands come from a coast-distance field and are converted to screen pixels at each zoom.
  - Country tones and borders come from the board texture's country index.
  - Raised coasts come from the neighbouring screen pixels.
  - 705 icons draw in one Skia `Atlas`, projected every frame and fading in from zoom 2.0.
    - Mountains sit on Natural Earth ranges, at least 550 km apart.
    - Each continent's flora and landforms (16 icons) and wildlife (29 species, 3–5 animals each, about 133 in all) come from the curated habitats and sourced animal anchors in `scripts/geography/scenery.json`, version 2. Every species has a checked source link. The old unused scenery layer (`ContinentScenery`, its small sprites and data) has been removed.
  - Coverage is global but continent-scale only. Beyond about zoom 4, coasts and borders turn blocky until country packs exist.
  - **Country tier decluttered (20 November 2026).** Covered countries show one sprite per place instead of castle/church clusters among houses: 958 country sprites became 654 sites. The builder gives each a zoom step (5.0–6.2) from which it is clear of every icon already shown, so neighbouring small countries no longer pile up and detail grows while zooming in. Covered capitals' landmarks are drawn a few icon pixels aside; 13 continent animals and plants make way for capitals and landmarks; 45 towns and decorations that only fit after 6.2 are left out. 609 country icons are drawn, all capitals and landmarks among them, in one batched draw per step. Rules: [ART_CONTRACT §3a](docs/art/ART_CONTRACT.md#3a-country-and-capital-tiers-decided-10-november-2026). Not yet checked on physical devices.
  - **Sea depth (20 November 2026).** Beyond the coastal ring, the sea's tone follows Natural Earth bathymetry (200/1000/2000/4000 m, pinned and checksummed with the other sources): shelves lighter, basins and the abyss darker; shallow coasts turn teal in temperate seas and turquoise in the tropics. A 1024×512 grey `depth.png` (50 KB, about 2 MiB decoded) feeds the shader. The shaders' random function no longer uses `sin()`, which drew glints and grass specks as dense streaks at country zoom on real GPUs.
  - **Regional building families (20 November 2026).** Towns are drawn in six families instead of one whitewash set: Central (ochre plaster, half-timber, copper onion towers), Mediterranean (whitewash, terracotta, campanili), Atlantic (stone, brick, slate, Gothic spires), Nordic (falu red, white churches with black spires), Orthodox (pastel walls, blue domes) and Ottoman (plaster over stone, mosques with minarets in Turkey and Albania, alternating with churches in Bosnia). Every capital is a walled town crowned with a gold star. Placement now measures overlap over the full reachable zoom range (to 7.0).
- The generator rebuilds the globe, display labels, manifest, and public-domain attribution from three pinned Natural Earth inputs. Outputs replace completed files atomically so Metro can remain running during regeneration.
- No visit tracking, local progress store, capital catalog, modal location picker, or contextual game HUD is currently installed.

[README.md](./README.md) contains run/regeneration/check commands. [TECHNICAL_PLAN.md](./TECHNICAL_PLAN.md) remains an architecture reference; this document controls implementation order.

## Phase 1 — Foundation

- [x] **P1-01 — Configure project checks.** SDK-compatible lint and typecheck commands.
- [x] **P1-02 — Resolve starter typing issues.** Expo declarations and web typing fixes.
- [x] **P1-03 — Establish routing.** Expo Router screens in src/app/; world rendering outside routes.
- [x] **P1-04 — Establish theme foundations.** Shared colors, type, and spacing.
- [x] **P1-05 — Verify the foundation.** Lint/typecheck, available browser checks, and platform bundle exports.

## Phase 2 — Earth presentation and visual foundation

**Result:** opening the app immediately shows an appealing, interactive pixel planet in a full-screen universe.

- [x] **P2-01 — Connect the renderer.** Skia native entry and browser CanvasKit with loading/failure/retry states.
- [x] **P2-02 — Generate the Earth overview.** Pinned Natural Earth masks, display labels, provenance, and license.
- [x] **P2-03 — Implement the globe camera.** Rotation, focal zoom, wrap/tilt bounds, and web input alternatives.
- [x] **P2-04 — Keep the original art through zoom.** Reuse the globe texture; remove obsolete atlas and unused images.
- [ ] **P2-05 — Validate the renderer.** Physical Android/iPhone, Safari, frame behavior, GPU/transient memory, five-minute memory trends, and throttled cold loading. Record unavailable targets.
- [ ] **P2-06 — Define the four-scale art contract.** Capture Earth/continent/country/capital style studies. Specify palette, pixel density, water/coast treatment, terrain, outline weight, lighting, and city-building perspective. Plan the gradual globe-to-regional presentation before generating detail packs.
  - **Continent/country contract, [draft 2](./docs/art/ART_CONTRACT.md) (10 November 2026).** Draft 1, using globe-blue water, dark borders and 2-point pixels, was judged too far from the concept image. Draft 2 adopts the concept's look:
    - a night sea with sparkles and clouds;
    - raised land with lit coasts and green cliff faces;
    - puzzle-piece country tones with pale borders;
    - a few large upright icons (snowy mountains, tree clumps, houses, castles, sailboats), with no rivers, roads or country-name labels;
    - an orange selection glow with a pin and white chip;
    - 3-point art pixels;
    - a parchment header and bottom card, with factual content only.

    Draft 2.1 adds detailed, shaded icons from `scripts/art/sprites.py`: a multi-peak snowy mountain, grassy hill, broadleaf and pine trees, castle, parish church, house, sailboat and pin. They are drawn with 2-point icon pixels over the 3-point terrain. Water bands are still measured in art pixels. Real-data mockups are in `docs/art/phase3/` (Europe from Natural Earth; Malta from OSM). Five open questions await review, including how the daytime globe ocean transitions to the night sea. The capital-scale rules are deferred to Phase 4.
  - **Capital/UI style study (10 November 2026), route `/style-study`.** A 12×11-tile seaside street from [Ninja Adventure](https://pixel-boy.itch.io/ninja-adventure-asset-pack) (CC0), drawn with Skia `Atlas` (8 batched draws, nearest sampling, whole-number scale; 2× on phones), plus a nine-slice panel and Pixelify Sans text. Findings: the warm 16×16 style and terracotta/red-tiled roofs suit the reference; its buildings are one-storey cottages, so multi-storey townhouses and landmarks still need original sprites. Pack grass `#ADBC3A` is yellower than globe grass `#8AC240`, and pack water `#71DDEE` is much lighter than globe shallows `#40A8C4`. **Decision: remap the pack to the globe.** `scripts/art/recolor_style_study.py` swaps 9 grass/foliage and 5 water colours for the globe's land and water ramps (exact-colour table; roofs, paths, flowers, and characters unchanged) and writes `assets/style-study/globe-palette/`. The study page shows the globe palette by default, with a toggle back to the pack colours. Pixelify Sans reads well at 12–22 px and now sets the globe's country labels and loading/error text, loaded by the root layout behind the splash screen; the pack's own font has broken letter spacing in the browser and is rejected for text. The pack's orange nine-slice frame works at 2–3×; the reference's cream parchment would need a recolour. Imported files total 188,319 bytes. The study does not yet define the continent/country scales, and it has not been checked on native devices.
- [x] **P2-07 — Make the first Earth visual pass.** Clear the opening UI; refine opening framing, ocean/coastal colors, atmosphere, stars, clouds, and silhouette while preserving real land shapes and the original pixel treatment. Continue visual iteration as needed.

- [x] **P2-08 — Add reference-inspired Earth and space detail.** Brighter land/ocean/ice texture, readable coasts, cloud bodies/highlights/shadows, atmosphere/glow, varied stars, a cratered moon, and a detailed satellite. Keep the scene interactive, use responsive object placement, hide distant objects naturally behind the zooming sphere, preserve the geography mask, and avoid new image downloads.

**Exit criteria:** Earth reads clearly at phone/tablet/desktop sizes; drag/pinch remain responsive; the shared art rules are concrete enough to guide the next scales. Device evidence remains tracked rather than inferred from a bundle export.

## Phase 3 — Continent/country scenery and visual zoom

**Dependencies:** the globe and P2-06 art rules. This replaces the removed navigation/visit prototype. Previous task IDs P3-01 through P3-06 are retired; the new visual tasks use P3-V IDs.

- [ ] **P3-V01 — Prepare geographic detail for the pilot route.** Bound Europe/Malta inputs; retain source IDs, coastlines/islands, country shapes, and real terrain/water data needed for rendering. Add only the metadata the visual journey uses.
  - *Started (10 November 2026):* inputs are pinned for the design mockups. They are Natural Earth rivers, mountain ranges and populated places (`scripts/art/phase3-sources.lock.json`) and the Geofabrik Malta OSM snapshot `malta-261108`. In it, Malta has 7,711 coastline points versus 38 in Natural Earth 1:10m, so the country scale must use OSM. No app data pack is generated yet.
- [ ] **P3-V02 — Render the continent view.** Show recognizable European geography with readable countries, islands, rivers, and terrain in the established pixel style. Use actual geographic detail and restrained labels; keep the scene full-screen.
  - *In progress (10 November 2026):* the board renders in the globe shader from `assets/world/board.png`, generated by `npm run assets:prepare`, with icons from `scripts/art/build_map_icons.py`. Country-name labels hand over to the board at zoom 1.9. Still to do: selection (pin, glow, chip), the header and card, and checks on native devices.
- [ ] **P3-V03 — Render the country view.** Add Malta’s finer coast/water, land-use/terrain, settlement/major-road structure, and Valletta’s geographic position. Scale decorative scenery deliberately; do not scatter arbitrary named landmarks.
- [ ] **P3-V04 — Connect the zoom visually.** Define camera/detail ranges from geographic bounds rather than the example image’s 10×/5× labels. Preserve the gesture’s focus through a gradual presentation/projection change, reveal detail progressively, and reverse cleanly. Keep one camera coordinate model.
- [ ] **P3-V05 — Verify visual continuity.** Capture the same route at all zoom thresholds and fractional zooms; check coastline alignment, pixel/palette continuity, tiny islands, loading, interrupted gestures, rotation/orientation, portrait/landscape, and bounded asset loading.

**Exit criteria:** zooming toward Europe and Malta reveals visibly richer geography, with no art-style switch, abrupt replacement image, or screen-sized UI blocking the map. A read-only globe enlarged to 16× does not meet this milestone.

## Phase 4 — Capital scenery: Lisbon first, then each country's capital

**Placement audit (21 November 2026): all 44 capitals pass `check_capital.py`.** The 22 capitals built before SITES tables (Lisbon to Budapest) could not have their compass order checked. `scripts/art/capital_sites.py` now gives their 173 landmarks real coordinates: the plan's researched values confirmed by Wikipedia (within 150 m, or 600 m for large sites) or OpenStreetMap, and Wikipedia and OpenStreetMap together for Lisbon, Madrid and Paris, which had no plan table. Fixes: Lisbon's Sé moved from the riverfront to its real place north-east of the Praça do Comércio below the castle hill, with Largo da Sé, Tram 28 passing in front, the castle's slope cleared of houses and pines, and its east side back on screen; Tower Bridge moved north of the London Eye; Bern's Kirchenfeld Bridge slid along the Aare so it lands west of the Historical Museum; St Nicholas moved clear of Prague Castle; Nyhavn's coordinate taken at the canal's midpoint. Dublin's Record Tower (part of the castle) and the Leif Erikson statue in front of Hallgrímskirkja are recorded as intended overlaps. The committed PNGs of Prague, Rome, Copenhagen, Warsaw and Vilnius predated their 17 November model updates; all five were regenerated, so every shipped scene now matches its source.

**Status (20 November 2026): paused after Vaduz.** No new capitals until the Phase 5 playtest (P5-08) confirms what each scene needs for gameplay. Work resumes as P8-01, with every new capital shipping its landmark data and challenges.

**Liechtenstein and Vaduz (20 November 2026).** The 720×520 north-up illustration follows the Alpine Rhine along the Swiss side of the valley, turning westward as it flows north. The Old Rhine Bridge crosses from Sevelen on the west bank to Vaduz on the east bank. The valley floor stays open by the embankments, with gravel bars and fields. The centre gathers around the Städtle pedestrian street and Peter-Kaiser-Platz east of the river, below the wooded bluff. The Red House sits north of the centre in Mitteldorf, with its vineyard to the south. Vaduz Castle sits on the eastern rock spur, above every other landmark. The [Vaduz visitor map](https://www.citytrain.li/application/files/7516/7541/6071/Vaduz_Ortsplan_2022.pdf), [municipal heritage register](https://www.vaduz.li/en/living-environment/living-building/listed-buildings) and [Alpine Rhine centreline](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1524365679) establish the layout. The river is regulated in this reach, and the mapped reach excludes boats.

| Landmark | SITES anchor and first source (lon, lat) | Second coordinate source (lon, lat) | Setting, silhouette and front |

| Red House | **9.52182, 47.14480**, [OSM building](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/351951309) | **9.52171, 47.14482**, [Wikidata](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q2168503), about 9 m | Mitteldorf vineyard slope, north of the centre. Two-storey dark-red west house with north/south stepped gables, one central four-storey tower with rough stone below and plaster/timber above, bent brown pyramid roof and low east winepress barn. The familiar vineyard-facing south elevation is represented on the visible near side, with the red house left of the tower and barn right. [Historic Lexicon](https://www.historisches-lexikon.li/Rotes_Haus) and [geotagged vineyard view](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Vaduz_Rotes_Haus.jpg). |
| Kunstmuseum Liechtenstein | **9.52211, 47.13950**, [OSM museum footprint](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/28712148) | **9.52253, 47.13931**, [Wikipedia](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunstmuseum_Liechtenstein), about 38 m | Below the bluff between Äulestrasse and Städtle. Low 60×25×12 m polished black box of basalt and river-pebble concrete, flat roof and thin recessed bands of glass. The Städtle entrance is on the east side and remains hidden. The dark mass, aggregate sheen and recessed glazing identify the museum. [Museum architecture](https://www.kunstmuseum.li/en/museum/about-us) and [entrance](https://www.kunstmuseum.li/en/your-visit/plan-your-visit). |
| Vaduz Castle | **9.52434, 47.13951**, [Burgenwelt survey and plan](https://www.burgenwelt.org/liechtenstein/vaduz/object.php) | **9.52444, 47.13944**, [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaduz_Castle), about 11 m | Wooded rock spur east of the centre. One square eastern keep under a steep tiled tent, two broad roundels at the north and south ends, west ranges around the court and a southwest gate, with the south roundel prominent from town. Pale masonry and plaster, warm brown-red roof tiles. The town-facing west/southwest front is visible. [Princely Collections plan](https://www.liechtensteincollections.at/en/collections-online/ground-plan-of-schloss-vaduz) and [Historic Lexicon](https://historisches-lexikon.li/Vaduz_%28Schloss%29). |

Ochre clinker-brick High House with one very steep gable and a ridge light, low glazed connector and lower, longer flat-roofed Long House to its east/southeast. The west entrance faces the square and snaps southwest in the model. | Government Building | **9.52274, 47.13711**, [OSM building](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/240862251) | **9.52278, 47.13722**, [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Building,_Vaduz), about 13 m | Peter-Kaiser-Platz south of Landtag. Three-storey cream Renaissance Revival façade, rusticated base, eleven west-facing axes, central projecting entry with a sandstone balcony and arched upper lights, and two roofed corner pavilions. West front snaps southwest toward the square. [Municipal heritage register](https://www.vaduz.li/en/living-environment/living-building/listed-buildings) and [Government reference](https://www.regierung.li/thema/16425/the-government?id=231506). |
| Cathedral of St Florin | **9.52276, 47.13622**, [OSM cathedral footprint](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/28712150) | **9.52270, 47.13620**, [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaduz_Cathedral), about 5 m | South of the government quarter. Long pale grey neo-Gothic three-aisle nave under a dark roof with a polygonal east apse, pointed lights and buttresses. One west-front tower with clocks, louvred bell openings and one tall spire with small corner ornaments. Western portal snaps southwest and apse remains opposite. [Municipal heritage register](https://www.vaduz.li/en/living-environment/living-building/listed-buildings). |
| Old Rhine Bridge | **9.51168, 47.13246**, [OSM bridge centreline](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/6071236) | **9.51194, 47.13250**, [Wikidata](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q436158), about 21 m | Long, narrow timber passage on five intermediate supports with six spans and a single continuous shallow gable. Brown vertical siding and open portals; shorter supports rise from the gravel banks. The southwest approach remains visible, and the Vaduz portal points northeast. [Restoration report](https://www.llv.li/serviceportal2/amtsstellen/amt-fuer-kultur/denkmalpflege/pdf-dateien/fund--und-forschungsberichte/jb-archaeologie-2010-web-pdf1.pdf) and [tourism exterior](https://en.tourismus.li/map/poi/alte-rheinbrucke-e5935511-7115-400a-942f-e7a4911f3b58.html). |

Natural Earth `LIE` supplies **Vaduz** at `9.516670, 47.133724` for the castle and two houses. The 75 km city-spacing rule leaves one city group. Camera coverage and the icon generator share `[9.47, 47.04, 9.64, 47.28]`. The capital scene starts at the [national survey reference on Städtle](https://www.llv.li/serviceportal2/amtsstellen/amt-fuer-tiefbau-und-geoinformation/geodetic_reference_point.pdf), `9.522423, 47.138451`. Two pines at `9.485, 47.165` and `9.485, 47.095` pass the board country filter and lie in the Natural Earth outline. The southern pine uses a 22-art-pixel eastward drawing offset to clear the castle group. The country tier has no offshore boats or separate landmark badge given Liechtenstein's inland setting and compact scale.

The seven isolated 4× studies and the house study were compared with the Lisbon, Madrid and Paris benchmarks. Iteration widened the castle ranges, added dormers and placed their keep above the north and south roundels. The cathedral gained louvred bell openings, an octagonal spire, a south sacristy and abbreviated twin entrance stairs. The Government Building has three arched central windows, a sandstone-baluster balcony, patterned roof tiles and a small crest panel. Its lion, mosaics, ornamental eaves and some façade bays fall short of the photographed detail. The Red House's step gables, broken tower roof and lower winepress barn retain their order. The Landtag roof light and glazed link read clearly, with a compressed Long House. The Kunstmuseum's recessed bands and polished aggregate retain the monolithic silhouette. Its signage and east entrance are outside the visible model. The bridge shows five supports and both abutments, with the fine bracing simplified. The completed 720×520 scene was reviewed beside the three benchmark scenes for geography, relative scale, roof heights and unobstructed landmarks.

**Vaduz verification.** The landmark, capital and map-icon generators completed, and `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py vaduz` reported **OK** for all seven sites with two 1.00 rank correlations, zero overlaps, zero hidden pixels and zero clipping. The five country placements are inside the shared bounds. All 1,658 previous placements, the atlas and the earlier scene PNGs match their original bytes. `npm test` passed 112 tests, and `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` and the Android/iOS Expo export passed. Both platform manifests contain the final Vaduz PNG and icon atlas. Chrome at 1000×780 and 390×844 showed both tiers. Wheel arrival matched direct entry and repeated drags stopped at all four edges. Chrome served the same Vaduz PNG bytes as the generator. The existing port-8081 server served an earlier bundle, so the verified browser run used a fresh port-8106 server. Physical devices and Safari remain unverified.

**Cyprus and Nicosia (20 November 2026).** The north-up 720×520 illustration follows the low Venetian ramparts and dry moat. Paphos Gate opens on the west, Famagusta Gate on the east, and Eleftheria Square links Ledra Street to the modern city across the southern moat. The Cyprus Museum stands outside the west wall and east of the diverted, intermittent Pedieos (Kanlıdere). Only a fringe of the river and its planted banks appears in the illustration. Büyük Han and Selimiye stand inside the north half of the old city, with the mosque east of the han. The routes and eleven bastion points are compressed from the [Visit Cyprus city map](https://www.visitcyprus.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/Lefkosia_Map_2024.pdf), [Venetian fortifications guide](https://www.visitcyprus.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/12/Cyprus_Venice_Cultural_Routes_2011_EN.pdf) and [OSM river](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/880436403). The modern buffer zone and the detailed street grid are outside the illustrated scope.

| Landmark | SITES anchor and first source (lon, lat) | Second coordinate source (lon, lat) | Setting, silhouette, front and model |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Paphos Gate | **33.35673, 35.17373**, [OSM gate footprint](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1086932111) | **33.35667, 35.17385**, [University of Cyprus survey](https://monumentmaterials.ucy.ac.cy/items/show/5), about 14 m | Western rampart, above the former moat. One WSW round arch, no tower, buff sandstone with a low grass-topped battered wall. The model represents the opening on the lit SW face, with masonry courses, dressed voussoirs and a recessed threshold. The portal surround and hillside are compressed. The Wikipedia point about 350 m north misses the mapped gate. |
| Famagusta Gate | **33.37119, 35.17424**, [OSM gate footprint](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/255063675) | **33.37106, 35.174328**, [Visit Cyprus](https://www.visitcyprus.com/discover-cyprus/culture/sites-and-monuments/famagusta-gate-pyli-ammochostou/), about 15 m | Eastern wall, with a long vaulted tunnel and high internal dome beneath the earthen rampart. The city-facing WNW entrance displays a round arch and triangular pediment between grassed shoulders. The model turns that portal toward SW and keeps the external face and internal dome out of view. It includes the two upper openings and ashlar courses. Tunnel depth, carved shields and the window trim fall short of the reference detail. |
| Cyprus Museum | **33.35575, 35.17173**, [OSM museum footprint](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/331964706) | **33.35528, 35.17167**, [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus_Museum), about 43 m | Outside the west wall, southwest of Paphos Gate. Low sandstone wings and clay hips behind one white marble Ionic portico, with four columns and a triangular pediment. The [geotagged front view](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Nicosia_01-2017_img28_Cyprus_Museum.jpg) places the approach WNW. The model locally turns the portico to SW. It retains four columns, steps, low roof and framed wing windows. Ionic capitals and pediment carving are abbreviated. |
| Eleftheria Square | **33.36017, 35.16953**, [OSM pedestrian deck](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1442087052) | **33.3602, 35.1695**, [Archistrail](https://archistrail.com/place/-), about 4 m | Southern wall at the D’Avila bastion and Ledra Street. [Zaha Hadid Architects](https://www.zha.com/projects/architecture/eleftheria-square?disclaimer=true) describes the upper pedestrian bridge, dry-moat park and triangulated garden forms. The model gives the white deck branching arms, tapered supports, rails and triangular planters on the lower paving. It spans toward the south with no principal front, towers or domes. Exact curves, rail profiles and the complete park plan remain simplified. |
| Büyük Han | **33.36257, 35.17626**, [OSM courtyard and mescit](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/686121427) | **33.36250, 35.17630**, [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BCy%C3%BCk_Han), about 8 m | Inland in the northern old city. Four low stone ranges, two levels of pointed arcades, flat roofs with masonry chimneys, and one octagonal domed mescit raised above its ablution fountain. The east entrance stays hidden and the secondary west gate is shown at SW. The model keeps the arcaded court and raised central dome separate, with fewer bays and chimneys than the real building. [UNFICYP building study](https://unficyp.unmissions.org/sites/default/files/bb-06_june_2004.pdf). |
| Selimiye Mosque | **33.36455, 35.17652**, [OSM mosque footprint](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/28958071) | **33.36450, 35.17650**, [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selimiye_Mosque,_Nicosia), about 5 m | Inland east of Büyük Han. Long pale limestone Gothic nave with polygonal eastern apse, flat roof, buttresses, three west portals and exactly two minarets above the old west turrets. The west front snaps SW, with both minarets at that end and the apse opposite. Two stepped gallery collars, conical caps, pointed openings and open buttresses preserve the skyline. Fine portal tracery and the exact number of flank bays are abbreviated. |

At country zoom, Natural Earth `CYP` supplies the city anchors. Nicosia and Paphos are the two groups left by the shared 75 km rule. Seven tree icons use the board’s country index and two boats sit offshore to the south. The independent **Choirokoitia** icon shows roofless circular walls on two hillside terraces. It is anchored at **33.34398, 34.79712** ([mapped excavation](https://history.about-cyprus.com/en/place/choirokoitia/)), about 45 m from the [OSM remains outline centre](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1101925655) at **33.34362, 34.79684**. [UNESCO](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/848/) independently gives **33.34394, 34.79674** (about 42 m from the anchor) and documents the circular dwellings and hillside setting. Camera and country bounds match at `[32.15, 34.48, 34.65, 35.78]`.


**Nicosia visual audit and checks.** Viewed each of the six isolated 4× studies and the house study alongside the eleven requested Lisbon, Madrid and Paris benchmark previews, then compared the finished scenes at the same pixel size. The revised han has flat masonry roofs, the western gate has a complete battered side, and the houses have separate clay hips, chimneys, shutters and timber bays. The gate ornament, museum capitals, square curvature and fine mosque tracery remain abbreviated as recorded above. In the country view the reduced Choirokoitia sprite still sits close to the capital group at zoom 5. All 1,642 earlier placements and 106 earlier sprite frames and their pixels were preserved.

Both asset generators, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py nicosia`, `npm test` (111/111), `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` and `npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` passed. The placement checker found six landmarks, east/x and south/y rank correlations of 1.00, zero overlaps, zero hidden pixels and zero clipped pixels. Both native manifests contain the final Nicosia scene and atlas. Chrome at 1000×780 and 390×844 displayed both tiers, reached the same view by scrolling as by the direct capital URL, and stopped at all four pan edges. Physical iOS and Android devices were not tested.

**Russia and Moscow (20 November 2026).** Six-landmark north-up 720×520 illustration. Geography is planned before buildings: the Moskva enters from the southwest, passes south of Christ the Saviour and the Kremlin's Borovitsky Hill, and bends east beneath St Basil's/Zaryadye. All six sites stay on the river's left/northern bank. The Kremlin plateau rises 12 art pixels, with lower Alexander Garden west of its wall, Cathedral Square inside, Red Square northeast, and Theatre Square north. Isometric-axis river reaches, open granite embankments, planted slopes, merchant houses with dressed stucco, green/grey metal roofs and restrained balconies establish the setting. No unresearched bridge is added. Coordinates are longitude, latitude. Independent OSM values below are polygon-area centroids reconstructed from the outer rings of each building relation, fetched from the public OSM API.

**Country tier and date line.** Natural Earth `RUS`, exact pinned capital **Moscow**. Shared bounds `[19,41,-169,82]` cross 180° (west > east). Both camera inclusion/pull-back and the country generator handle that wrapped interval, avoiding an almost-global bounding box and retaining Chukotka. Cities use the existing 75 km separation, capital castle/two-house group and town alternation. Seven board-country-filtered trees and two coastal boats accompany a dedicated twin-summit Elbrus icon. The continent tier is unchanged. The generated groups are Moscow, St. Petersburg (the source spelling has two spaces after the period), Novosibirsk, Yekaterinburg, Nizhny Novgorod, Samara, Omsk, Kazan and Chelyabinsk. The seven pines are at `(69.125,63.875)`, `(89.875,62.625)`, `(136.375,61.125)`, `(115.375,59.625)`, `(49.375,58.875)`, `(67.125,56.875)` and `(112.375,50.625)`; boats at `(163.375,59.125)` and `(140.125,57.375)` are off the Pacific/Sea of Okhotsk coast. All **28** additions lie inside the wrapped bounds. Pines deliberately avoid the Arctic desert/tundra, staying within 50–64°N west of 140°E. Earlier atlas frames, RGBA pixels and all 1,614 earlier placements are preserved exactly.

**Elbrus fact sheet.** The country icon is anchored at the western summit: **42.4378432,43.3524103**, [OSM node 281936304](https://www.openstreetmap.org/node/281936304), versus independent [GeoNames](https://www.geonames.org/RU/highest-mountains-in-russia.html) **42.438,43.353** (~67 m). Both identify the 5,642 m western summit. The mountain's two glaciated volcanic domes stand north of the main Caucasus watershed; the western summit is higher than the eastern 5,621 m one. The Wikipedia mountain pin and Peakbagger's map-derived western-summit point differ by a few hundred metres from the mapped OSM summit; they are not treated as precise equivalents. The selected OSM/GeoNames pair meets the 100 m agreement criterion. The original **88×70** mesh sprite places the higher western summit left and slightly north of the eastern one, with a saddle, broad snow slopes, shaded rock apron and ground shadow. No building front, tower or flag applies. All four transparent margins are clear. Individual glacier routes and exact contours are compressed; no GPS-survey precision is claimed.

**Visual audit and limits.** Regenerated and viewed all six `moscow-*.png` isolated landmark studies, the house study and `russia-elbrus.png`. Compared them side by side with all eleven requested Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmark studies, then compared all four finished scenes at the same art-pixel size. Iteration reduced overly tall window openings, replaced pale museum trim with red mouldings, textured the Bolshoi's stage-house roof, refined the tented roofs and narrowed/lengthened Ivan's three octagons to preserve its slender silhouette. The final scene preserves every geographic ordering, the six northern-bank settings, raised Kremlin plateau, western Alexander Garden, northeastern Red Square, northern Theatre Square, eastern Zaryadye and open embankments. No defining silhouette or placement shortfall remains in this review. Exact relief iconography, inscription letters, eagle anatomy, Apollo/horse anatomy, ornamental tracery and specific ceramic pattern identities remain miniature abbreviations. These are the remaining fidelity limits; the model does not claim a measured architectural survey. Hidden NW/NE fronts and deliberate SW snaps are explicitly recorded per landmark above.

**Verification (20 November 2026).** Ran `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_russia.py`, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_capitals.py` and `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py`. After the final isolated tower refinement, published the final scene produced by `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py moscow /tmp/pw-moscow/scene-final.png`; the checker prints **OK**, six landmarks, **1.00** on both rank correlations, no reversed pairs, no landmark overlaps, and `hidden=0` / `clipped=0` throughout. The full capital generator reproduced stale differences in five earlier images (Rome, Copenhagen, Prague, Warsaw and Vilnius); their original pre-task bytes were restored. Every pre-existing capital PNG is preserved. `npm test` passes **110/110**, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` pass. `npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-moscow-export-final --max-workers 2` succeeds for both platforms with the final scene. The final browser pass confirms current served PNG hashes and both viewport screenshots without page/console errors.

`npm run web -- --port 8081` confirmed the same app was already running there, so browser checks used that existing server. Chromium checked Russia zoom 5, Elbrus zoom 5.5, the wrapped Chukotka URL, and Moscow at **1000×780** and **390×844**. Scroll-wheel arrival from country zoom matched direct capital arrival; repeated drags stopped pixel-stably at each of the eight tested pan edges. No page/console errors occurred. A final visual-only tower refinement leaves the tested camera code unchanged; the final-asset browser pass successfully rechecked both viewport screenshots and served hashes. Physical Android/iOS devices and measured on-device performance were not verified. Screenshots, source snapshots, comparisons and command logs are in `/tmp/pw-moscow/`. No commit was made.

**Ukraine and Kyiv (19 November 2026).** Six-landmark, north-up 720×520 illustration. Geography is laid out before buildings: Andriivska Hill above Podil in the north; the elevated Upper City around Sophia/Michael; the Pechersk plateau southeast; the broad Dnipro east of all six monuments, bending from NE toward SE along isometric-axis reaches. All sites are on its elevated right (western) bank, inland rather than on a quay. No bridge is invented. Compressed squares connect the Upper City; wooded escarpments descend to open riverside paths. The scene uses historic Kyiv plaster/brick houses with dressed windows, iron balconies, pediments, metal roofs and chimneys. Coordinates below are **longitude, latitude**. OSM building values come from polygon area centroids fetched from the public OSM API; the statue uses its mapped monument node. These are independent of Wikipedia's coordinate points.

| Landmark | Coordinate source A | Independent building source B | Setting, silhouette and defining feature | Front / compass decision and model audit |

Tall stepped stylobate/terrace. [Ukraine's UNESCO submission](https://whc.unesco.org/en/tentativelists/5423/) explicitly confirms the one-plus-four silhouette, colours, orders and **western** entrance; loose descriptions claiming five subsidiary spires are rejected. | West entrance locally snaps SW, exposing the single entrance and paired columns; four corner turrets surround the central drum. Raised 26 art pixels. Matched in the final isolated 4× study; fine gilded relief is pixel-abbreviated. |
