Explicit **local turn toward SW** makes the figure/shield readable, with sword at the viewer's left and shield at right. The city is not rotated. Raised 24 pixels. Matched in the final isolated 4× study: solid faceted robe, articulated arms, head/face, broad blade, thick shield and a drawn trident. Museum pedestal and steel panel seams are compressed; the sculpture is locally turned as stated. |

**Country tier:** Natural Earth `UKR`, bounds `[22.0,44.2,40.3,52.4]`, including Crimea, shared verbatim by the camera. Pinned populated-place name **Kyiv**, selected first at `30.514682,50.435313`, then Kharkiv, Dnipro, Odessa, Donetsk, Lviv, Kryvyy Rih, Mykolayiv and Mariupol (historical source spelling/population order), all retaining the 75 km separation. Seven country-index-filtered trees (two northern pines/five oaks) and two Black Sea/Sea of Azov boats accompany the cities. Offshore candidates stay east of `30.2°E` to avoid duplicating the existing Romanian coastal boat. All 28 additions lie inside the bounds; no earlier icon or continent placement changes. **Khotyn Fortress** (`26.49833,48.52194`) is on the Dniester's right bank. [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khotyn_Fortress) agrees within ~20 m with [KuLaDig's independent heritage inventory](https://www.kuladig.de/Objektansicht/KLD-316868), `26.49842,48.52211`. The [reserve's plan](https://khotynska-fortecya.cv.ua/fortress_map) confirms five towers and an irregular oval citadel: tall pale masonry, red-brick geometric bands, pointed dark brown/grey roofs, open court and southern gate, checked against the [regional tourism authority’s aerial photograph](https://travel.chernivtsi.ua/show/248/khotyn-national-nature-park). Its native 88×88 country icon models five towers, square north/gate towers, round flank towers, brick bands and an open court; all four transparent margins remain clear. The southern gate snaps SW; exact outer Ottoman earthworks are omitted at miniature scale.



**Visual review and verification (19 November 2026).** Six isolated 4× studies and the house study in `docs/art/capitals/kyiv-*.png`, plus `ukraine-khotyn.png`, were regenerated and viewed. Each was compared side by side with Lisbon/Madrid/Paris landmark benchmarks; the finished 720×520 scene was compared with all three full scenes. Photograph review refined Andrew’s gold capitals/oculus, Sophia’s gilded lanterns, Michael’s blue-grey lead roofs, the gate’s brick screen/scalloped chapel, Lavra’s octagonal stages/blue frieze/upper lantern and Khotyn’s dark conical/pyramidal roofs. The models retain all six defining silhouettes; fine Baroque relief, exact subsidiary-dome spacing, Lavra’s precise 32/16/8 columns and the figure’s panel seams/museum geometry remain miniature approximations. These limits are explicit rather than unconfirmed extra architecture.

Ran `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_ukraine.py`, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_capitals.py` and `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py`. The final `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py kyiv` prints **OK**: six landmarks, both rank correlations **1.00**, no reversed pairs, no overlaps, `hidden=0` and `clipped=0` throughout. `npm test` passes **107/107**; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` pass. `npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-kyiv-export-complete --max-workers 2` succeeds for both platforms with the final assets. The complete generator exposed stale render differences in five earlier capital PNGs; their original bytes were restored, and every pre-existing capital PNG, atlas frame/pixel and icon placement was checked against the before-work snapshot. Only the Kyiv scene, appended Khotyn frame and 28 Ukraine country placements are added.

`npm run web -- --port 8081` found the project already running there, so browser verification used that existing server. Chromium at **1000×780** and **390×844** checked Ukraine zoom 5, Khotyn zoom 5.5, direct Kyiv zoom 7, scroll-wheel arrival from Kyiv zoom 5 and all four pan directions at each viewport. Wheel arrival equals direct arrival, repeated drags become pixel-stable at every edge, there are no page/console errors and served asset hashes match the final PNGs. Screenshots, comparison sheets and command logs are in `/tmp/pw-kyiv/`; the exported bundles are in `/tmp/pw-kyiv-export-complete/`. Physical Android/iOS devices and measured on-device performance were not verified. No commit was made.

**Kosovo and Pristina (19 November 2026).** Six-landmark, north-up 720×520 scene: Emin Gjiku and the Imperial Mosque/Clock Tower in the northeastern old town, NEWBORN southwest of them, the National Library southeast of NEWBORN, and Mother Teresa Cathedral southwest of the library. The municipal [tourist map](https://orl-ks.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/tur-harta-prishtina-new-01-eng.pdf) establishes the campus lawns, Mother Teresa pedestrian spine, eastern slopes and covered central rivers. There is no exposed river, invented bridge or boat in this scene. Gentle old-town terraces rise eight art pixels; the eastern residential slope rises sixteen. Coordinates below are longitude, latitude; independent point differences are below 100 m.

| Landmark | Coordinate source A | Independent building source B | Setting, silhouette, defining feature and compass decision | Final model audit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Emin Gjiku Ethnological Museum | [Faculty of Design study](https://fd.si/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/fd_alice-5th.pdf): **21.1679306,42.6686889** | [OSM building way 679772218](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/679772218), [coordinate reader](https://mapcarta.com/W679772218): **21.16819,42.66869** (~21 m) | Northeastern old town, walled domestic courtyard; two-storey white plaster, dark timber frame and grouped divided windows, projecting upper rooms, broad dark clay hips and deep eaves. No tower/dome. Main entrance node lies southwest of the footprint; the SW courtyard front is visible. | Rebuilt as unequal intersecting hip-roof wings with a concave courtyard front, solid white window bays, a broad glazed SE loggia, white scalloped valances and recessed stair. [Ministry of Culture photographs](https://dtk.rks-gov.net/Objekti?heritageId=8943) establish the shape. Exact joinery carvings and other buildings in the complex are abbreviated. |
| Imperial (King's) Mosque | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Mosque,_Pristina): **21.1670,42.6670** | [OSM way 525903102](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/525903102), [reader](https://mapcarta.com/W525903102): **21.16701,42.66699** (~1 m) | Old town, northwest of the Clock Tower; square pale sandstone prayer hall, one broad lead dome, three smaller porch domes, one western minaret with one balcony and pointed lead cap. NW entrance/three-arch portico is on a hidden face; its low porch crowns are modelled behind the taller prayer hall, while the western minaret remains visible. No accidental south-facing replacement portal. | Rechecked one great/three lower porch domes and the western minaret. The NW porch remains behind the prayer hall. Replaced domestic blue sash windows with small dressed, dark arched grilles and replaced horizontal dome stripes with smooth lead panels/radial standing seams. [Aerial reference](https://www.kosovo-vacations.com/royal-mosque.html). |
| Clock Tower (Sahat Kulla) | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clock_Tower_of_Pristina): **21.16726,42.66674** | [OSM way 1366369553](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1366369553), [reader](https://mapcarta.com/de/W1366369553): **21.16728,42.66674** (~2 m) | Old-town street opposite/southeast of the mosque. One **hexagonal**, 26 m shaft; lower 16.5 m sandstone, upper brick with clock faces, gabled clock stage, open arched belfry and shallow lead cap. No onion dome. Multiple clock faces have no single principal front; visible SW/SE clocks are drawn. [Restoration reference](https://chwbkosova.org/its-about-time-restoration-camp-in-the-clock-tower-of-prishtine-pristina/). | Rebuilt the open brick arcade below the black clock dials, hexagonal-face pediments and round inset medallions, stone-shaft arched lights and small photographed weather vane. The six-sided shaft retains its long sandstone lower section. [Restored upper-stage photograph](https://indeksonline.net/en/dritat-e-sahat-kulles-ne-prishtine-do-te-ndizen-neser/) confirms the openings. Roman numerals/rail details are pixel-abbreviated. |
| NEWBORN | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newborn_monument): **21.158209,42.660539** | [OSM way 206455042](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/206455042), [reader](https://mapcarta.com/W206455042): **21.15841,42.66073** (~27 m) | Paved forecourt east of the Youth and Sports Palace, west of Mother Teresa boulevard. Seven freestanding extruded steel letters, 24 m long/3 m high/0.9 m deep; yellow **2008 original**, not a claim about the annually changing contemporary paint. Actual line runs roughly NNE–SSW and front faces ESE; explicitly turned locally to SW to keep NEWBORN legible. City placement stays north-up. No towers/domes. | Rechecked and retained the seven extruded letters, open counters and original yellow finish. It is explicitly the historical 2008 version, with its local front turn; it does not assert the contemporary annually changing paint scheme. DIN curves are pixel-abbreviated. |
| National Library of Kosovo | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Library_of_Kosovo): **21.16222,42.65722** | [OSM way 124951398](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/124951398), [reader](https://mapcarta.com/W124951398): **21.16232,42.65751** (~33 m) | University campus lawns, east/north of the cathedral. Stepped concrete cubes wrapped in metal lattice and many unequal white translucent roof domes. [ISPRS conservation paper](https://isprs-archives.copernicus.org/articles/XLII-2-W5/215/2017/isprs-archives-XLII-2-W5-215-2017.pdf) gives **99** domes; the municipal leaflet says 79, so the conservation research count takes precedence. The [architectural conservation report](https://www.academia.edu/37661503/The_National_Library_of_Kosovo_Pjet%C3%ABr_Bogdani_Historic_Structure_Report_Condition_Assessment) places public access west and administrative access east; the western public approach snaps SW. Exact module/dome spacing is compressed, with 99 explicitly modelled roof caps. | Rebuilt around two dominant convex geodesic skylights, unequal medium domes, smaller peripheral roof lights, dark recessed glazing and projected external diamond mesh. The earlier eleven identical nine-light groups were inaccurate. The model retains the conservation paper’s reported 99 caps; the small-light layout is compressed rather than a measured roof survey. [Aerial photograph](https://www.tourhq.com/article/national1library1of1kosovo). |
| Mother Teresa Cathedral | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_Saint_Mother_Teresa): **21.15944,42.65639** | [OSM way 105555618](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/105555618), [reader](https://mapcarta.com/W105555618): **21.15935,42.65638** (~8 m) | West of the university lawns, south/east of NEWBORN. Long pale stone basilica, red clay nave/aisle roofs, arcaded clerestory, **two** tall square campaniles with clocks/open belfries and crosses, no dome. [Diocesan Easter 2025 photograph](https://www.drita.info/kisha/kisha-ne-kosove/programi-i-festes-se-pashkeve-2025-ne-katedralen-shen-nene-tereza-ne-prishtine/) confirms both towers. OSM footprint has the rounded apse at SSW; the rose-window entrance faces NNE. The towers flank the outer sides of the transept, about a quarter of the building length in from the apse, rather than standing inside the aisles at the rear wall. The [2023 aerial photograph](https://kallxo.com/lajm/sot-prishtina-shenon-diten-e-clirimit/) establishes the tower/transept/apse relationship; the 2025 photograph confirms the completed pair. Its local display turn is explicitly recorded in the model audit. | Rebuilt a longer white-marble basilica with separate aisle slopes, continuous arched clerestory, gabled open barrel-arch porch, rose tracery, stepped forecourt and bronze-toned doors. Tower stages now have separate lower lancets, solid pale clock stages, single lower/paired upper belfry lights. The building alone receives an explicit local 180° turn to expose its NNE entrance toward SW; the transept/tower pair is displayed toward NE. Tower footprints project outside the aisles and sit in from the rounded apse; the far tower is drawn before the nave and the near tower after it, preserving roof occlusion. Geographic landmark ordering stays north-up. [Diocesan 2022 side/aerial photograph](https://www.drita.info/kisha/kisha-ne-kosove/programi-i-festes-se-pashkeve-2022-ne-katedralen-shen-nene-tereza-ne-prishtine/); [2025 completed tower pair](https://www.drita.info/kisha/kisha-ne-kosove/programi-i-festes-se-pashkeve-2025-ne-katedralen-shen-nene-tereza-ne-prishtine/). Relief capitals and exact window carving are abbreviated. |

**Country tier:** Pinned Natural Earth `KOS` has Pristina, Prizren and Pec; only Pristina survives the shared 75 km rule. Bounds `[20.0,41.8,21.9,43.3]` are shared by the country tier and camera. Seven country-index-filtered trees use 20 km decoration spacing; landlocked Kosovo requests zero boats. As with Portugal, no extra country landmark is added: the small country keeps the capital castle/two-house group legible. All ten additions lie inside the bounds; all earlier country and continent placements and atlas pixels remain unchanged.

Six isolated 4× landmark studies and a house study are generated by `scripts/art/landmarks_kosovo.py` in `docs/art/capitals/pristina-*.png`. The architecture review compares each model with building photographs as well as the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris pixel-art benchmarks. Cathedral, museum, library, mosque windows/lead roofs and Clock Tower openings were corrected after that comparison; NEWBORN was retained as a documented historical version. The scene keeps its open foregrounds, north-up site order, paved squares, pedestrian spine and planted campus. The placement checker validates order, clipping and external occlusion; it does **not** establish architectural accuracy. Small reliefs, roof seams and the exact distribution of minor library roof lights remain compressed. Published library dome totals conflict (the conservation paper says 99; the municipal leaflet says 79); the model follows the former without claiming a new roof survey.

**Pristina verification (including the architecture review):** `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_kosovo.py`, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_capitals.py` and `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py` ran successfully. `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py pristina` reports six landmarks, east/x and south/y rank correlations **1.00**, **hidden=0**, **clipped=0**, no overlaps/reversals, and **OK**. `npm test` passes all **105** tests; `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, Python syntax checks and `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` pass. Both platform manifests contain the exact final Pristina PNG, verified by SHA-256. The revised scene is 157,190 bytes (about 154 KiB compressed); its studies and scene were inspected against the photographs cited in the audit table. Only Pristina was re-rendered for this review; the other 39 capital images are unchanged. All 39 previous capital PNGs, previous icon placements and the complete existing atlas are byte-preserved; the all-capitals generator's incidental rewrites of older outputs were restored to their pre-task bytes. Only Kosovo's ten icon placements are added. Chrome checks cover both country/capital URLs, scroll-wheel arrival matching the direct capital URL and repeated drags stopping at all four scene edges at 1000×780 and 390×844 on localhost:8081 (also tested on temporary port 8091). The stale project server was restarted; localhost:8081 remains running for review and the temporary server was stopped. Served scene/atlas hashes match disk and there are no browser console/runtime errors. Physical devices, native performance and Safari remain unverified. No commit was made.


**Lisbon (10 November 2026).** `scripts/art/build_capitals.py` renders a 720×520 isometric scene with these parts:
- São Jorge Castle on its walled hill with umbrella pines;
- the Sé's twin towers and rose window;
- the Rua Augusta arch over Praça do Comércio, with its wave paving and yellow arcades, open to the Tagus;
- the Baixa and Alfama in pastel houses with terracotta roofs;
- a tram street with rails and two trams;
- green trees, jacaranda and blossom trees;
- Belém Tower on its bastion in the river, and sailboats.

The scene has 2-point icon pixels and is drawn in the app by `CapitalLayer`.
- **Arrival:** between zoom 6.3 and 7.0 it grows out of Lisbon's location over the board.
- **Panning:** it then pans, with its edges keeping the screen covered (`clampToCapital`).

**Madrid (10 November 2026)** uses the same pipeline. Its scene includes:
- the Palacio Real with its corner pavilions and Plaza de la Armería;
- the Almudena Cathedral's slate dome and towers;
- the enclosed brick-red Plaza Mayor with its equestrian statue;
- Puerta del Sol's Real Casa de Correos and clock tower;
- the Gran Vía and the domed Metrópolis with its gold Victory;
- the Cibeles fountain and Puerta de Alcalá;
- the Retiro, with its lake, rowing boats and the Palacio de Cristal;
- the Manzanares.

Madrid's houses use cream, ochre, brick and salmon walls under slate roofs, unlike Lisbon's terracotta. Spain's country tier marks Madrid, eleven more cities and the La Mancha windmills at Consuegra and Campo de Criptana.

**Lisbon and Madrid, rebuilt in detail (11 November 2026).** Their landmarks are now built in true isometric 3D in `scripts/art/landmarks_iberia.py`, each checked alone on a test plot (`docs/art/capitals/lisbon-*.png`, `madrid-*.png`). Shared helpers cover terracotta hip roofs, merlons and ashlar courses.

- **Lisbon**
  - **São Jorge:** crenellated curtain walls of old stone, square towers with arrow slits, and the keep flying Portugal's flag.
  - **The Sé:** a fortress-cathedral with twin crenellated towers, a deep portal with archivolts, a rose window, and the crenellated nave and lantern.
  - **Belém Tower:** gun ports, shield-merlons with the cross of the Order of Christ, domed watchtowers, the Venetian loggia and rope mouldings.
  - **The Rua Augusta arch:** paired columns, a clock, and Glory crowning Genius and Valour.
  - **Also:** Pombaline arcades, Lisbon houses (pastel or azulejo walls, white frames, iron balconies, hip roofs, chimneys) and Tram 28.
- **Madrid**
  - **The Palacio Real:** a rusticated base, pilasters, a portico, a statue balustrade, and the great courtyard seen from above.
  - **The Almudena:** belfry towers and the drum and slate dome.
  - **Plaza Mayor:** porticoes, the Casa de la Panadería's painted façade and spires, and Philip III's statue.
  - **Casa de Correos:** its clock tower.
  - **The Metrópolis:** its shaded slate dome with gilding and the winged Victory.
  - **Also:** Puerta de Alcalá, Cibeles, the Palacio de Cristal, the Alfonso XII colonnade, and Madrid houses with a balcony on every floor, slate mansards and occasional corner domes.

Both scenes were recomposed so every landmark stays in view:
- **Lisbon:** the Tagus runs along an isometric axis, with Rossio and the Baixa streets, Alfama lanes and gardens.
- **Madrid:** open squares before the Almudena and around Plaza Mayor, and the Manzanares with a stone bridge.

**Paris, rebuilt in detail (11 November 2026).** The first Paris landmarks were judged too crude. They are now built in true isometric 3D in `scripts/art/landmarks_paris.py`, each checked alone on a test plot (`docs/art/capitals/paris-*.png`):
- **Eiffel Tower:** four open lattice truss legs on stone plinths, great arches springing from the legs, an arcaded first platform, a second platform, the spire, the top cabin and mast.
- **Louvre:** wings with a rusticated arcade, tall windows between pilasters, zinc mansards with dormers and square-domed pavilions around the Cour Napoléon, plus the pyramid's gridded glazing, the small pyramids and basins.
- **Notre-Dame:** twin towers above the rose-windowed centre, three portals, the Gallery of Kings, the nave with flying buttresses and its spire.
- **Arc de Triomphe:** monumental, with its vault, sculpture groups, frieze and attic.
- **Sacré-Cœur:** an ovoid dome on its drum, small domes, the campanile, on a raised Montmartre.
- **Arched bridges,** including Pont Alexandre III's gilded pillars, and the Concorde obelisk.
- **Haussmann blocks:** shopfronts with awnings, framed windows, continuous iron balconies on the 2nd and 5th floors, zinc mansards with dormers, chimney pots.

The Seine now runs along an isometric axis, so bridges cross it squarely. The Left Bank keeps an open, tree-lined strip so the river stays visible.

Before this rebuild, Paris had these parts:
- the Seine flowing east to west, with stone quays, bridges, bateaux-mouches and open, tree-lined south-bank quais;
- Notre-Dame on the Île de la Cité (twin towers, rose window, spire);
- the Louvre's wings around I. M. Pei's pyramid, beside the Tuileries;
- the Champs-Élysées to the Arc de Triomphe on the Étoile;
- the Eiffel Tower over the Champ de Mars;
- Sacré-Cœur's white domes on the Montmartre hill.

Houses are six-storey Haussmann blocks in cream limestone under low zinc roofs. France's country tier marks Paris, eleven more cities, Mont-Saint-Michel and the Saint-Émilion vineyards.

**Berlin (11 November 2026).** Germany uses the same country/capital pipeline, with `DEU` as Natural Earth's administrative code and coverage bounds `[5.8, 47.2, 15.1, 55.1]`. The country tier has Berlin's castle and two houses, eight further cities selected from the pinned populated places (75 km minimum spacing), seven pine/oak trees, two offshore boats and Neuschwanstein at `10.7492°E, 47.5576°N`. No continent placements change.

The 720×520 scene is built in true isometric geometry in `scripts/art/landmarks_berlin.py` and `build_capitals.py`. Eight 4× inspection plots include the seven landmarks and a house study. Real coordinates guide the north-up composition; distances are compressed, and the Spree's banks follow isometric axes for square bridge crossings:

| Landmark | Longitude, latitude | Signature details drawn |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Victory Column | 13.3501, 52.5145 | Red granite relief base, circular mosaic colonnade, segmented shaft with gilded barrels, viewing balcony and filled, winged Victoria |
| Reichstag | 13.3762, 52.5186 | Rusticated plinth, framed windows and pilasters, four corner pavilions, six-column pedimented portico, stairs, ribbed glass dome and German flag |
| Brandenburg Gate | 13.3777, 52.5163 | Two rows of six Doric columns, five genuinely open passages, triglyph/relief attic, side lodges, four individually drawn horses and the Quadriga's chariot and driver |
| Konzerthaus | 13.3927, 52.5136 | Raised central hall, two sculpted pediments, six-column portico, broad staircase, flanking lions and solid Apollo figure with lyre |
| Berlin Cathedral | 13.4010, 52.5191 | Great ribbed copper dome on a windowed drum, lantern and cross, four corner cupolas, arched portals, rose window and entrance steps |
| Rotes Rathaus | 13.4083, 52.5186 | Red brick courses, arcaded ground floor, terracotta frieze, projecting corners, paired clock faces, belfry and pinnacled roof |
| TV Tower | 13.4094, 52.5208 | Tapering concrete shaft, faceted silver sphere, observation/restaurant glazing belt, striped antenna and angular entrance pavilion |

The TV Tower is northeast of the cathedral and town hall; the cathedral is north of the Reichstag; the Reichstag and town hall are north of the Gate. Tiergarten and Victory Column stay west of the Gate, and Gendarmenmarkt's Konzerthaus lies southeast of it. Museum Island is enclosed by two arms of the Spree, with open quays, bridges and excursion boats. Gründerzeit houses use muted ochre, sage and rose stucco, framed two-pane windows, shopfronts, iron balconies, projecting bays, cornices, textured roofs, dormers and chimneys. Footprint reservations and projected silhouette clearances keep landmarks visible. The shared capital clamp now uses inverse projection, like focal zoom, to stop drags exactly at scene edges at Berlin's higher latitude.

Architectural references: [Berlin's official sights index](https://www.berlin.de/en/attractions-and-sights/district/mitte/), [Brandenburg Gate](https://www.visitberlin.de/en/brandenburg-gate), [Berlin Cathedral](https://www.visitberlin.de/en/berlin-cathedral), [TV Tower](https://www.visitberlin.de/en/berlin-television-tower), [Rotes Rathaus](https://www.berlin.de/rbmskzl/en/the-red-town-hall/), [Victory Column](https://www.visitberlin.de/en/siegessaule), [Konzerthaus](https://www.konzerthaus.de/en/visit), and [Bavarian Palace Administration: Neuschwanstein](https://www.schloss.bayern.de/englisch/palace/objects/neuschw.htm). Reference photographs were used for inspection only; generated artwork is original.

**Berlin verification:** all 31 tests, TypeScript, Expo lint and the Android/iOS export pass. Headless Chrome checks cover country/capital URLs, scroll-wheel arrival, repeated drags in four directions and a 390×844 viewport, without browser errors on the finished assets. Every Berlin preview was inspected beside the existing Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks. All previous map-icon placements and atlas frames remain identical; the 28 new Germany icons lie within its own bounds. The Berlin PNG is about 162 KiB compressed (1.43 MiB decoded RGBA). Capital PNG publication is atomic so regenerating art under a running Expo server cannot expose half-written images. Real devices, native performance and Safari remain unverified.

**Amsterdam (12 November 2026).** Netherlands follows the existing country/capital pipeline with Natural Earth `NLD`, coverage bounds `[3.2, 50.7, 7.3, 53.6]` and the scene anchored at `4.8952°E, 52.3702°N`. Amsterdam is explicitly selected first: the pinned populated places give The Hague a larger metropolitan population, which would otherwise remove Amsterdam through the 75 km spacing filter. The result is Amsterdam's castle and two houses, church towns at Eindhoven and Groningen, a church at Zwolle and a house pair at Middelburg. Five oak trees fit the existing land/spacing filters, plus two offshore boats and Kinderdijk at `4.6400°E, 51.8833°N`. Its new sprite shows a brick ground-sailer, a thatched smock mill, dark caps, lattice sails and a polder drainage channel. Existing icon placements, atlas frames and atlas pixels are preserved.

The 720×520 city scene is built in true isometric geometry in `scripts/art/landmarks_amsterdam.py` and `build_capitals.py`. Eight individual 4× plots and a separate house study are in `docs/art/capitals/amsterdam-*.png`. The north-up composition preserves these relative ground positions while compressing distances:

| Landmark | Longitude, latitude | Signature details drawn |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Centraal Station | 4.9000, 52.3783 | Long red-brick frontage, sandstone bands, clock/compass towers, stepped entrance gable, heraldic panels and ribbed glass train shed |
| Anne Frank House | 4.8843, 52.3752 | Restrained dark-brick three-bay frontage, white cornice, broad attic dormer, doorway and rear Annex across the courtyard |
| Westerkerk | 4.8836, 52.3744 | Renaissance brick nave, dressed windows and transept gables; staged tower, clock, galleries and blue imperial crown with golden arches |
| Oude Kerk | 4.8980, 52.3744 | Parallel pitched aisle roofs, dressed windows, buttresses, clock tower and layered copper belfry/spire |
| NEMO | 4.9124, 52.3741 | Rising flared copper hull, faceted rounded prow, panel seams, glazed entrance and stepped roof piazza with planters and railings |
| Royal Palace | 4.8914, 52.3732 | Rusticated sandstone base, pilasters, arched entrances, projecting relief pediment, clock cupola and filled Atlas carrying a globe |
| Magere Brug | 4.9025, 52.3636 | White double lifting portals, projecting balance beams, counterweights, diagonal chains, piers and open railings |
| Rijksmuseum | 4.8853, 52.3600 | Red brick and pale stone bands, Gothic windows, twin steep-roofed towers, stepped central gateway, framed cycle passage and carved panels |

Centraal Station is north and east of Oude Kerk; NEMO lies east and slightly south of Oude Kerk. The Anne Frank House is northeast of Westerkerk, west of the palace and north of both. Rijksmuseum stays south and west of the palace, with Museumplein and Vondelpark to its south/southwest. Magere Brug is southeast of the palace, southwest of NEMO and north/east of Rijksmuseum. The IJ/Oosterdok and three compressed canal-belt elbows surround the centre; waterways follow the tile axes for square bridge crossings. Stone quays, open elm-lined promenades, cobbled streets, Dam Square, gardens and excursion boats establish Amsterdam's waterside setting. Narrow brick houses use six colours, two/three-storey variations, Dutch gables, dressed windows, ground-floor doors/shopfronts, ironwork, hoisting beams, textured slate/clay roofs and chimneys. Footprint reservations and projected silhouette clearances keep the monuments unobscured.

Architectural references: [NS Stations: Centraal architecture](https://mijnstation.nl/en/amsterdam/about-this-station/architecture/), [Amsterdam city archive: Westerkerk](https://www.amsterdam.nl/stadsarchief/canon/windows/14/), [Royal Palace](https://www.paleisamsterdam.nl/en/), [Anne Frank House: the Secret Annex](https://www.annefrank.org/en/anne-frank/secret-annex/), [Oude Kerk](https://www.oudekerk.nl/en/gettinghere/40757), [Rijksmuseum: Cuypers' building](https://www.rijksmuseum.nl/nl/story/cuypers-museum-building), [Renzo Piano Building Workshop: NEMO](https://www.rpbw.com/project/nemo-national-center-for-science-and-technology), [Dutch heritage register: Magere Brug](https://monumentenregister.cultureelerfgoed.nl/monumenten/518383), and [Kinderdijk's mills](https://kinderdijk.nl/en/windmills-pumping-stations/). Ground coordinates were cross-checked against the corresponding landmark coordinate records; country-city coordinates come unchanged from the pinned Natural Earth data. Reference photographs were used for inspection only; the generated art is original.

**Amsterdam verification:** the landmark preview generator, capital scene generator and country-icon generator all run successfully. All 33 tests, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` and `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` pass. Both platform exports contain the final artwork, verified by SHA-256. Chrome checks cover the country and capital URLs, Kinderdijk, wheel arrival (identical to opening the capital URL), repeated drags stopping at all four desktop scene edges, and a 390×844 viewport with its north edge stopping correctly; no browser errors occurred. Every 4× preview was inspected, and the finished scene compared beside Lisbon, Madrid and Paris. No obscured, cropped or unfinished landmarks were identified in the final review. All previous icon placements, atlas frames and atlas pixels remain identical; the 20 added Dutch icons lie within the Netherlands bounds. The scene PNG is about 162 KiB compressed (1.43 MiB decoded RGBA). Real devices, native performance and Safari remain unverified.

**Brussels (12 November 2026).** Belgium joins the existing country/capital pipeline with Natural Earth `BEL`, bounds `[2.5, 49.45, 6.4, 51.6]` and a scene anchor at `4.3517°E, 50.8503°N`. The pinned populated places yield four city groups after capital-first population sorting and the 75 km spacing filter: Brussels (castle and two houses), Liège and Brugge (church between houses), and Arlon (church). Nearby Antwerp and Ghent respect that spacing. Six of the seven requested oak trees fit, and one of the two requested boats fits the existing 150 km boat spacing and country-index coastal filter; Belgium's coarse offshore candidate cells are only about 17 km apart, so a second would overlap. Gravensteen stands separately at its real Ghent site (`3.7206°E, 51.0572°N`), with a square keep, crenellated walls, small wall turrets, gate, paired windows and Leie moat. The new frame is appended without changing existing atlas pixels, frames or icon placements.

The 720×520 scene is built in `scripts/art/landmarks_belgium.py` and `build_capitals.py`. Eight individual 4× inspection plots and a separate house study are in `docs/art/capitals/brussels-*.png`. Ground anchors preserve north/south and east/west relationships while compressing distances:

| Landmark | Longitude, latitude | Signature details drawn |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Atomium | 4.34111, 50.89472 | Nine shaded steel spheres, observation/seam belts, twenty tubes preserving body-centred cubic connectivity, three paired angled supports, glazed entrance and Belgian flag |
| Koekelberg Basilica | 4.31722, 50.86667 | Art Deco brick nave and transept, paired entrance towers, dressed tall windows, arcaded porch, immense segmented green copper dome, gallery, lantern and cross |
| King's House / Maison du Roi | 4.35278, 50.84694 | Three pointed-arch galleries, tracery and balustrades, carved figures, pinnacles, textured steep roof, staged open lantern and flag |
| Saint Michael and Saint Gudula Cathedral | 4.36028, 50.84778 | Twin flat-topped belfry towers, louvres and pinnacles, tall pointed west window, recessed portal, pitched nave, flying buttresses and broad stairs |
| Town Hall | 4.35167, 50.84639 | Asymmetric wings, pointed ground arcade, statue niches, slate dormers, corner turrets, staged open octagonal spire, gilded winged Michael, spear and dragon |
| Royal Palace | 4.36222, 50.84167 | Long dressed-stone frontage, paired pilasters, arched entrances, projecting sculpted pediment, end pavilions, mansards/dormers, stone balustrade, iron fence and Belgian flag |
| Cinquantenaire Arcade | 4.39278, 50.84028 | Three equal open arch tunnels with recessed reveals and voussoirs, coupled columns, attic reliefs, flanking colonnades and four-horse bronze quadriga, chariot and laurel-bearing Brabant |
| Palace of Justice | 4.35167, 50.83667 | Massive stepped stone blocks and corner pavilions, six-column entrance porch, pediment, broad staircase, square upper drum with pilasters and a ribbed gilded dome/lantern |

The Atomium is north and west of the old centre, east of Koekelberg Basilica. The King's House is northeast of Town Hall across Grand-Place; the cathedral is northeast of both. Brussels Park lies north of the Royal Palace, which is southeast of the old centre. Cinquantenaire lies farther east and slightly south of the Royal Palace; the Palace of Justice is south of Grand-Place and west of the Royal Palace. The canal lies west/northwest of the old centre, following an isometric axis with broad open banks, stone bridges and boats. The central Senne is covered, so no open downtown river is added. Distinct cobbled squares, paved streets and formal gardens surround brick and dressed-stone houses, with guild-house gilding, gable scrolls, cornices, shopfronts, iron balconies, occasional Art Nouveau bays, slate roofs and chimneys. Footprint and projected silhouette reservations protect all landmarks from foreground houses and trees.

Architectural references: [City of Brussels: Grand-Place](https://www.brussels.be/grand-place-brussels), [Town Hall](https://www.brussels.be/city-hall), [Brussels heritage inventory: Maison du Roi](https://monument.heritage.brussels/fr.Bruxelles_Pentagone.Grand-Place.29.html), [cathedral architecture](https://cathedralisbruxellensis.be/en/oeuvres/the-gothic-church/), [Atomium's nine spheres and twenty tubes](https://atomium.be/the_atomium_in_figures), [Koekelberg Basilica](https://heritagedays.urban.brussels/en/programme/ganshoren/national-basilica-of-the-sacred-heart/), [Belgian Monarchy: Royal Palace](https://www.monarchie.be/en/heritage/royal-palace-of-brussels), [Palace of Justice heritage record](https://monument.heritage.brussels/fr/Bruxelles_Pentagone/Place_Poelaert/1/30442), [Cinquantenaire heritage record](https://monument.heritage.brussels/fr/0/Parc_du_Cinquantenaire/10005026), and [Ghent: Castle of the Counts](https://historischehuizen.stad.gent/en/castle-counts). Landmark coordinates were cross-checked against the corresponding geographic records; country-city coordinates remain the pinned Natural Earth values. Reference photographs were inspected only; all generated artwork is original.

Adding Brussels exposed a regression in the nearest-capital pan clamp: Brussels and Amsterdam have overlapping reach rectangles, allowing long drags to switch cities and both layers to draw. Camera state now retains the visited `capitalId` from arrival until zooming below 6.3. The layer and clamp use the same active capital, and deep-zoom coverage remains bounded to that visit's reach. Regression tests cover both directions through the overlap, zoom-out release, selecting the next capital and all six capitals' long drags in portrait/landscape.

St Paul's is north of the old City, west of the Tower; the Tower is northeast of Trafalgar Square. Tower Bridge is southeast of the Tower and north/east of the Eye. The Eye is east and north of Westminster, with Buckingham Palace west of the Abbey and south of the Eye. The Palace of Westminster's centre lies east and slightly south of the Abbey. The Thames follows a compressed S-bend, with its reaches on isometric axes and both bridges crossing squarely. Broad open embankments protect the water, while boats, cobbled streets, distinctly paved squares, Green Park and St James's Park establish the setting. London's own stock/red-brick terraces have sash windows, sills, doors with fanlights, iron guards, cornices, parapets, slate roofs, bays and chimney pots. Ground reservations and measured projected silhouette clearances protect every landmark.

Low red/ochre/blue/green corrugated-iron houses have basalt plinths, white timber two-pane frames and sills, doors/shopfronts, cornices, porches/dormers, metal-seam roofs and masonry chimneys. Footprint reservations and measured projected silhouette clearances protect every landmark.

Architectural references: [Hallgrímskirkja: building and history](https://www.hallgrimskirkja.is/en-gb/husi%C3%B0-og-sagan), [Henning Larsen: Harpa](https://www.henninglarsen.com/projects/harpa), [Olafur Eliasson's Harpa façade geometry](https://www.studiootherspaces.net/project/facades-of-harpa.html), [Visit Reykjavík: Sun Voyager](https://visitreykjavik.is/service/sun-voyager), [Perlan: six tanks and glass dome](https://perlan.is/about-perlan), [Visit Reykjavík: City Hall](https://visitreykjavik.is/service/city-hall), [Alþingi: Parliament House](https://www.althingi.is/english/about-the-parliament/the-parliament-house), [Iceland's Cultural Heritage Agency: Landakotskirkja](https://www.minjastofnun.is/is/byggingararfur/fridlyst-hus-og-mannvirki/landakotskirkja-kristskirkja), [Visit Reykjavík: Höfði](https://visitreykjavik.is/service/hofdi-house) and [Visit South Iceland: Gullfoss](https://www.south.is/en/place/gullfoss-waterfall). Exterior photographs were inspected for reference only; all generated artwork is original. Site coordinates were checked against the corresponding Wikipedia/Wikidata geographic records and guide the illustration; country cities retain their pinned Natural Earth values.

Every one of the nine 4× Reykjavík landmark/house studies and the finished scene was inspected beside the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks. Iteration refined Hallgrímskirkja's tower crown, Harpa's projected glazing, Perlan's dome ribs, Höfði's hipped roof, Landakot's traceried belfry and cross-gabled aisle, the waterfront behind Sun Voyager/Höfði, and Gullfoss's transparent margins. No landmark was judged below the benchmark detail standard in the final comparison. Alþingi's tiny guardian reliefs/cypher and Leif Erikson's facial features remain abbreviated at this pixel size. Every painted pixel of all eight landmarks survives in the final scene, with zero pairwise silhouette overlap or clipping. The deterministic Reykjavík PNG is 116,333 bytes (about 114 KiB compressed; 1.43 MiB decoded RGBA). All thirteen earlier capital PNGs and prior icon placements/atlas pixels are unchanged.

**Reykjavík verification:** all 53 tests, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, Python syntax checks and `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` pass. Both platform manifests reference the exact final Reykjavík PNG and icon atlas, verified by SHA-256. The isolated landmark, capital/registry and country-icon generators run successfully. Chrome on temporary port 8095 covers Iceland, Gullfoss and Reykjavík URLs, scroll-wheel arrival matching the direct capital URL, and repeated drags stopping at all four scene edges at both 1000×780 and 390×844. The final browser run has no console/runtime errors, and served PNG/atlas hashes match disk. The temporary server was stopped; existing development servers were preserved. Real devices, native performance and Safari remain unverified. No commit was made.

**Bern (13 November 2026).** Switzerland joins the existing country/capital pipeline with Natural Earth `CHE`, bounds `[5.95, 45.8, 10.55, 47.85]` and scene anchor `7.4474°E, 46.9480°N`. The capital-first selection keeps Bern ahead of larger Geneva and Zürich. Six groups fit the existing 75 km rule: Bern (castle/two houses), Geneva and Zürich (church/two houses), Lugano (church), Chur (house pair) and Sion (church). Four of seven requested pines survive the existing land/spacing filters. Switzerland is landlocked, so the unchanged offshore filter yields no boats. Chillon Castle stands at `6.9275°E, 46.4142°N` with a new original sprite: lake islet, pale masonry, tall square keep, round Savoyard towers, dressed openings and brown-red roofs. Its frame is appended without changing previous icon placements or atlas frames.

`scripts/art/landmarks_switzerland.py` builds eight true isometric landmarks and Bernese townhouses. Nine isolated 4× inspection plots are in `docs/art/capitals/bern-*.png`. Their ground anchors preserve the real north/south and east/west order, with distances compressed into 720×520:

| Landmark | Longitude, latitude | Signature details drawn |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Zentrum Paul Klee | 7.47389, 46.94917 | Three unequal undulating steel roof meshes, curved structural ribs, glazed end walls, mullions and low plinth |
| Nydeggkirche | 7.45736, 46.94881 | Sandstone Gothic nave, lancets, tiered buttresses, steep tiled roof, square clock tower, bronze relief door panels and pointed roof |
| Rathaus | 7.45239, 46.94875 | Late Gothic windows, broad tiled hip roof, dormers, raised arcaded porch, twin opposing stair flights, stone handrails and Swiss flags |
| Käfigturm | 7.44394, 46.94816 | Genuinely open vaulted gate, rusticated masonry, framed windows, gilded time dial, steep red roof, bell lantern and attached stair house |
| Zytglogge | 7.44778, 46.94806 | Open gate vault, dressed courses, west/east clock dials, east astrolabe with eccentric disc/zodiac ring/sun arm, filled polychrome automata, roof lantern and copper spire |
| Bern Minster | 7.45139, 46.94722 | Single soaring tower, clock, paired belfry lancets, octagonal upper stage, open eight-rib tracery spire, corner pinnacles, coloured Last Judgement portal and buttressed aisles |
| Federal Palace | 7.44417, 46.94667 | Sandstone wings, rustication, pilasters, arcaded entrances, central relief pediment, ribbed great copper dome, two smaller domes, gold lantern, escutcheon, stairs and Swiss flag |
| Bern Historical Museum | 7.44917, 46.94306 | Castle-like wings, steep tiled roofs, unequal cylindrical towers and conical caps, stepped central gable, polychrome entrance mosaic, carved portal and stairs |

Paul Klee lies north/east of the Old City; Nydeggkirche is east and slightly north of Rathaus. Rathaus is east/north of both clock towers and north/east of the Minster. Käfigturm is west and slightly north of Zytglogge. The Federal Palace lies east/south of Käfigturm and west/south of Zytglogge and the Minster. The Historical Museum is south of the centre, east of Zytglogge and west of the Minster. The Aare wraps the peninsula along two compressed tile-axis reaches, with Nydegg's stone bridge, Kirchenfeld's iron double arches, jade water highlights and rowing craft. Open promenades keep the foreground river visible. Bundesplatz, Münsterplatz, the Minster Terrace, northeastern rose beds and museum gardens balance the sandstone streets and arcaded houses. Houses have two/three-storey variations, six sandstone colours, dressed two-pane windows, shutters, recessed shops/doors, occasional ironwork/flags, plinths, cornices, textured tiled roofs, dormers and capped chimneys.

Architectural references: [Bern Welcome: Zytglogge](https://bern.com/en/explore/tourist-attractions/attractions/zytglogge-clock-tower), [Käfigturm](https://bern.com/en/explore/tourist-attractions/attractions/kaefigturm), [Bernese Minster](https://bern.com/en/explore/tourist-attractions/attractions/bernese-minster), [Parliament Building](https://bern.com/en/erleben/attraktionen/sehenswuerdigkeiten/bundeshaus-und-bundesplatz), [City of Bern: city map](https://a.storyblok.com/f/272028/x/14f24686d5/bern-city-map_2024.pdf), [Historical Museum: castle architecture](https://www.bhm.ch/en/visit/practical-information), [Zentrum Paul Klee: three-hill architecture](https://www.zpk.org/en/about-us/architecture), [Chillon: keep and lake-facing halls](https://www.chillon.ch/en/castle/highlights/) and the corresponding Wikipedia coordinate/photo records. Photographs are inspection references only; the generated art is original. The astrolabe's lettering, automata faces, Last Judgement figures and museum mosaic are abbreviated at this pixel size.

Every final 4× Bern study and the composed scene was inspected beside the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks. No landmark was judged below the benchmark detail standard in the final comparison; fine lettering, faces and miniature narrative reliefs remain abbreviated. All 61,770 painted pixels of the eight landmarks survive in the final scene with zero pairwise overlap, overwriting or clipping. The Bern PNG is 164,096 bytes (about 160 KiB compressed; 1.43 MiB decoded RGBA). All fourteen earlier capital PNGs, previous icon placements, continent scenery, atlas frames and earlier RGBA pixels remain unchanged. Chillon's geometry was reduced after browser inspection to keep Bern's marker clearer; it is drawn natively at 59×60 pixels without image resampling.

**Moldova and Chișinău (18 November 2026).** Natural Earth `MDA` joins the existing country/capital pipeline with shared bounds `[26.6,45.4,30.2,48.5]` and scene anchor `28.8353°E,47.0228°N`. The exact pinned name **Chișinău** selects its castle/two houses at `28.857711,47.005024`; Bălți (`27.905315,47.759086`) and Cahul (`28.194444,45.907881`) get church/two-house groups. These 17 new icons all lie inside the shared bounds; all earlier country and continent placements, sprite frame rectangles and RGBA pixels remain unchanged.

`scripts/art/landmarks_moldova.py` builds eight true isometric landmarks and Bessarabian houses. Eight isolated 4× landmark studies, a house study and the Soroca icon study are in `docs/art/capitals/chisinau-*.png` and `moldova-soroca.png`. The scene is `assets/world/capitals/chisinau.png`, 720×520, 154,235 bytes (about 151 KiB compressed; 1.43 MiB decoded RGBA). The generated registry and literal image imports come from `build_capitals.py`.

**Two-source coordinate fact sheets.** All pairs below are **longitude, latitude** in degrees. OSM coordinates refer to the building/monument itself: building-footprint centres or the monument footprint, not the enclosing square. The independently maintained [municipal tourist map's geocoded records](https://visit.chisinau.md/en/otladka-oshibok-na-sajte/) provide additional checks. Wikipedia and Wikidata count as one source family. Source exterior photographs were inspected; no reference photograph is copied into the generated art.

| Landmark and chosen coordinate | Independent coordinate comparison | Setting, count, defining details and front; model check |

Low cruciform cream Empire body; **one** cylindrical drum and dark olive/bronze hemispherical dome/cross; **four six-Doric-column porticoes**. Main axial front faces SW. Model matches the low body, single dome, six columns on each visible SW/SE porch, pediments, steps, framed round-headed openings, courses and cornices. The two northern porticoes are hidden by the building in this projection and omitted from painted geometry; they are not shown on a visible wall. No building turn. Real figure faces NE out of the park; it is **deliberately turned 180° to SW**, putting its own left/raised hand on the viewer's right. Facial detail and pedestal relief are abbreviated. White-stone tetrapylon with **four square piers**, intersecting open round vaults, rectangular attic, clock and classical cornices; **no dome or statue**. The model retains the four-pier volume, actual open passages, radial voussoirs, pilasters, courses and SW clock. Its NE–SW main axis stays in place; the SW face is visible. |

Model retains all these identifying volumes, dressings, patterned bands and approach steps. Front faces SW on Kogălniceanu Street and remains SW, without a turn. Long **two-storey L-shaped wings**, yellow plaster/white rustication, dressed arched windows, **one corner clock tower**, small metal cap/lantern and entrance balcony. Model matches the L plan, one tower at the wings' junction, two clock dials, cornices, gables, balcony/ironwork and capped roof. Real NE boulevard front and SE return are **deliberately turned 90° clockwise to SE/SW**; the tower stays at their junction. National flag has vertical blue/yellow/red and a miniature eagle/red breast shield/gold aurochs mark, not a plain Romanian tricolour. Heraldry and civic lettering are abbreviated; other real flags are omitted. [Municipal architecture/photo](https://visit.chisinau.md/en/obiective_turistice/cladirea-primariei-municipiului-chisinau-bd-stefan-cel-mare-si-sfant-83/). |
| **National History Museum**, `28.8280886,47.0225926` — [OSM building 26790058](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/26790058), not the enclosing museum parcel | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_History_Museum_of_Moldova): `28.82806,47.02250` (11 m); municipal `28.8281315,47.0225865` (3 m) | Inland southwest of park, south of Ethnography Museum and slightly south of City Hall. Broad pale classical wings, **four giant Corinthian portico columns**, central concave metal pavilion roof/circular arms/small lantern, lower balustrades, wide stairs, **Capitoline Wolf with twins on red pedestal**. Model matches the long low wings, four fluted columns with leaf-capital pixels, rising concave roof, cartouche, lantern, balustrades, stairs and solid wolf/legs/twins. Real NE front on 31 August Street is **deliberately turned 180° to SW**. Lettering, arms and wolf anatomy are abbreviated. [Municipal source](https://visit.chisinau.md/en/obiective_turistice/muzeul-national-de-istorie/), [museum's own close exterior photograph](https://www.nationalmuseum.md/img/logofb.jpg). |
| **Water Tower / City History Museum**, `28.82242625,47.01933070` — [OSM building 12309864](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/12309864) | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chișinău_Water_Tower): `28.82222,47.019306` (16 m); municipal `28.8224708,47.0193078` (4 m) | Southwestern upper town on Mateevici, above/east of Valea Morilor's valley. **One octagonal limestone shaft**, three masonry stages with brick string courses, projecting corbelled timber/glazed upper tank room and broad polygonal red roof; **no spire**. Model retains the eight facets, stages, divided windows, brick bands, brackets, wide upper room/roof and approach steps. East/SE entry snaps to SE; the octagonal crown is symmetric. Its raised ground is 18 art pixels above the valley. [Municipal source/photo](https://visit.chisinau.md/en/obiective_turistice/muzeul-orasului-chisinau/). |

**Soroca fact sheet.** [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soroca_Fort) gives `28.3054778,48.1612167`; [OSM fortress building 12593748](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/12593748) has bounding-centre `28.30525185,48.1613268`, about 21 m away, within the same fortress footprint. The fortress is on the **west/Moldovan bank of the Dniester**. Its defining circular stone curtain, **four round towers and one rectangular eastern river-facing gate tower**, open court and restored conical/pyramidal clay caps are present in the native 72×68 icon. [Moldova's tourism authority](https://turism.gov.md/en/obiective_turistice/cetatea-soroca/) confirms the circular five-tower plan; [Visit Moldova](https://www.visit.md/en/tour/sorokskaya-krepost/) distinguishes four round towers from the rectangular gate. Tiny inner galleries/loopholes and the hidden river-facing gate opening are abbreviated/hidden; no flags are invented. No resampling is used to fit the atlas, and all four transparent margins were checked.

**Geography and composition.** The layout was written down before drawing. Every longitude and latitude rank remains correct: cathedral far NE; belfry SW of it; Stephen west and slightly south of belfry; arch east/south of Stephen; Ethnography Museum far west/south of arch; City Hall far east/south of Ethnography; History Museum west/slightly south of City Hall; water tower south/east of Ethnography and west/south of History. Cathedral Park surrounds the cathedral/belfry; Stephen Park lies SW of its statue. The compressed boulevard follows the isometric i axis NW–SE south of the arch. The upper town rises southwest: Ethnography 10 art pixels, History 8, water tower 18; the Cathedral Park/square group stays at 0. Continuous base tiles and rock-faced terrace steps avoid gaps. A small northeastern slice of **Valea Morilor lake** lies at the southwest edge, below/west of the water tower, with open wooded banks and highlighted teal water. The **Bîc is outside this central composition to the northeast**; no central river, bridge, coast or unconfirmed boat traffic is invented. Distinct pale/rose square slabs, cobbled streets, planted park paths and paved blocks organise the city.

Houses are low Bessarabian limestone/plaster townhouses in cream, sage, rose, blue-grey, ivory and lilac. One/two-storey variants have framed divided glass and sills, dressed window hoods and corner masonry, doors or glazed shops, plinths, cornices, occasional projecting iron balconies, clay or grey-green metal hip roofs with texture/ridges, gable-front variants and capped chimneys. Measured landmark silhouettes and reserved squares keep houses/trees out of the foreground of every landmark.

**Visual comparison and model limits.** Every final 4× study and the finished scene was inspected alongside the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks. Iteration opened/shaded the belfry vaults, enlarged Stephen's cross and modelled his raised left arm, corrected capped water-tower rings overwriting upper windows, raised/shaped the History Museum's concave roof, enlarged preview canvases so the museum is not cropped, gave Soroca transparent roof-tip margins, and moved the belfry west to eliminate its overlap with the cathedral while preserving both geographic ranks. All eight have the benchmark's projected volumes, lit/shaded faces, dressed openings and material details. Fine heraldry, statue faces, relief identities, ceramic motifs, inscriptions and the wolf's anatomy remain abbreviated at this size; the rear cathedral porticoes and Soroca's east gate opening are hidden as described above. These small details are less literal than their references; no unresolved missing defining silhouette remains. Individual north/east-facing fronts are only exposed by the three explicit local model turns, never by rotating/mirroring the city.

**Verification.** `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_moldova.py`, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_capitals.py`, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py`, Python syntax checks, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py chisinau`, `npm test` (**101/101**), `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, and `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-chisinau-export --max-workers 2` pass. Placement reports **eight landmarks, rank correlation 1.00 on both axes, hidden=0, clipped=0 and no overlaps**. Both native export manifests contain the exact final scene and atlas bytes, verified by SHA-256. Adding Moldova puts Kyiv inside the camera's existing country pull-back margin, so the projection test's truly uncovered example changes to Moscow; direct Kyiv maximum zoom remains unchanged. All earlier capital PNG bytes are preserved; full regeneration exposed pre-existing differences in five earlier renders, which were restored to their original bytes without changing those generators.

**Browser and final review:** Chrome on temporary port 8085 passes Moldova, Soroca, Chișinău and Bucharest regression URLs, wheel arrival equal to the direct capital URL, and repeated drags stopping at all four scene edges at both 1000×780 and 390×844. The successful run has no console/runtime errors; served scene/atlas hashes match disk. The initial CI server had started before the generated registry was finished and served its startup snapshot; restarting it after generation corrected the stale preview, and all checks were repeated successfully. Both native exports also match the final assets by SHA-256. The new scene re-renders pixel-identically; all 37 earlier capital PNGs, prior atlas frame rectangles/pixels and all earlier icon placements are unchanged. Soroca’s nontransparent bbox `(9,7,69,64)` lies strictly inside its 72×68 canvas. The implementation review found no remaining correctness or scope issues. The temporary server was stopped after verification; the existing port-8084 server was left running. Real devices, native runtime performance, Safari and an EAS build are not verified; no commit is made.

**Adding a country:**
1. Add a scene function and a `CAPITALS` entry in `scripts/art/build_capitals.py`. It writes `src/data/capitals.json` and the generated `src/data/capital-images.ts`.
2. Add a `COUNTRY_TIER` entry in `scripts/art/build_map_icons.py`.
3. Add a `COVERAGE` entry in `src/features/world/globe-camera.ts`.

Tests check that all three agree.

The Valletta tasks below are superseded by this per-country capital approach.

**Dependencies:** connected continent/country rendering and the shared art contract.

- [ ] **P4-01 — Prepare the bounded capital pack.** Pin Malta/Valletta OSM inputs; extract coast/water, streets, parks, and building footprints with source/license records and reproducible generation.
- [ ] **P4-02 — Render recognizable capital scenery.** Compose streets, buildings, greenery, waterfront, and curated landmarks using the world’s palette, pixel grid, outlines, and lighting. Preserve geographic position even when sprites use a three-quarter perspective.
- [ ] **P4-03 — Connect country to capital.** Reveal the capital’s real detail around its coordinates, retain coarse coverage while loading, and cap useful zoom. Returning restores the same country context. Other capitals stay coverage-limited until packs exist.
- [ ] **P4-04 — Verify the complete visual journey.** Walk Earth → Europe → Malta → Valletta and back. Check landmarks/roads against sources, visual continuity, input interruption, asset/disk/decoded costs, and available platforms. Record unavailable native checks.

**Exit criteria:** the capital is recognizable scenery with real geographic anchors, not an overview marker or standalone city illustration. Complete the visual milestone before adding the game UI.

**Austria and Vienna (13 November 2026).** Austria follows the existing country/capital pipeline with Natural Earth `AUT`, coverage bounds `[9.5, 46.35, 17.2, 49.05]` and a Vienna scene anchor at `16.3738°E, 48.2082°N`. Capital-first population sorting and the 75 km filter select Vienna (castle/two houses), Linz and Graz (church/two houses), then Salzburg, Innsbruck, Klagenfurt and Bregenz alternating church/house groups. Nearby Wiener Neustadt and Eisenstadt are excluded by spacing. Seven pines fit the existing country-index/land/spacing filters; Austria is landlocked, so no offshore boats are invented. Melk Abbey stands at its real rocky terrace above the town (`15.3315°E, 48.2286°N`), with yellow monastery wings, dressed windows, red roofs, twin clock towers and copper onion caps, and the great dome. All 23 added country icons fit Austria's own bounds. The 46×56 sprite is appended to the atlas; existing frames and pixels remain unchanged. Athens replaces Vienna as the uncovered-capital example in existing tests.

`scripts/art/landmarks_austria.py` builds eight true isometric landmarks and Vienna's stucco townhouses. Nine isolated 4× inspection plots are in `docs/art/capitals/vienna-*.png`. The layout was chosen before the builders: the Prater and Riesenrad in the northeast, Rathaus northwest of the core, St Stephen's east of the Hofburg, the Opera and Karlskirche farther south, Upper Belvedere southeast, and Schönbrunn far southwest. Ground anchors preserve every relative longitude/latitude order, compressing distances independently into 720×520:

| Landmark | Longitude, latitude | Signature details drawn |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Giant Ferris Wheel / Riesenrad | 16.3959, 48.2167 | Two connected steel rims, thirty radial spokes, separate A-frame supports, axle, fifteen level red glazed cabin volumes and entrance pavilion |
| Rathaus | 16.3578, 48.2108 | Five unequal Gothic towers, open stone spire tracery, corner pinnacles, twin clock dials, ground arcade, steep slate roof and a filled armoured Rathausmann with banner |
| St Stephen's Cathedral | 16.3731, 48.2085 | Dominant south tower with octagonal open tracery, shorter domed north tower, paired Romanesque west towers, Giant's Door, circular west window, buttresses and steep glazed chevron/eagle roof |

| Vienna State Opera | 16.3692, 48.2029 | Renaissance wings, lower entrance arcade, open upper arched loggia, raised copper roof with dormers, paired volumetric winged horses with riders and entrance stairs |
| Karlskirche | 16.3713, 48.1983 | Tall copper dome on a windowed drum, lantern and cross, six-column temple portico, relief pediment, twin spiral-carved columns with statues, pagoda-capped side belfries and stairs |

The northeast Danube Canal follows an isometric axis with two square stone crossings, open quays and boats. The main Danube is beyond the scene; no river is invented through the centre. Distinct cobbles, paved squares, a compressed Ringstrasse loop, Rathauspark, Resselpark and the Schönbrunn/Belvedere gardens separate house blocks. Vienna's cream, buff, salmon, sage and grey-blue Gründerzeit houses have two-pane framed windows, sills, moulded pediments, recessed shopfronts, iron guards, cornices, plinths, occasional oriels, dormers, textured slate/clay roofs and capped chimneys. Both ground footprints and measured projected silhouettes reserve space before houses and trees are placed.

Fort St Elmo is north/east of all other sites. St Paul's lies west/north of the Carmelite Basilica, which lies west/north of St John's. Every pair of real east/west and north/south anchor orderings is preserved. Open forecourts and measured projected bounds protect all landmarks. | Gëlle Fra | 6.128889, 49.609444 | Stepped granite plinth, tapered obelisk, solid gilded woman with robe/head/bent arms and laurel wreath, two bronze mourning figures and memorial panel |

Ville Haute and Grund houses use cream, ochre, rose, blue-green and sage stucco, with dressed divided windows/sills, timber doors or glazed shops, occasional iron guards/balconies, plinths/cornices, steep textured slate roofs, dormers and capped chimneys. Distinct cobbled streets, palace/Constitution forecourts, Kirchberg lawns, wooded Pétrusse valley, Alzette bends, open quays, a small rowing boat and a low square-crossing stone bridge separate the neighbourhoods. Both waterways follow isometric axes. Footprint and measured projected pixel masks reserve open space around monuments so houses/trees cannot obscure them.

Architectural references: [Luxembourg City Tourist Office map](https://www.luxembourg-city.com/en/plan-your-stay/traveller-information/citymap), [Grand Ducal Palace](https://monarchie.lu/en/residences/grand-ducal-palace), [Notre-Dame Cathedral](https://luxembourg.public.lu/en/visit/arts-and-culture/cathedral-notre-dame-luxembourg.html), [Gëlle Fra](https://luxembourg.public.lu/en/society-and-culture/history/gelle-fra.html), [Bock promontory](https://www.luxembourg-city.com/en/place/fortification/bock-promontory), [Philharmonie architecture](https://www.philharmonie.lu/en/architecture), [Mudam architecture](https://www.mudam.com/architecture) and [Vianden Castle](https://www.visitluxembourg.com/place/vianden-castle), plus the corresponding Wikipedia coordinate/exterior-photo records (including Neumünster Abbey and Adolphe Bridge). Reference photographs were viewed locally; all procedural art is original and no dependency was added.

Every standalone study and the completed scene was viewed beside the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks. Iteration fixed cornice caps covering façades/roofs, differentiated the cathedral's spires, broadened the Philharmonie's chamber shell, broke up the Bock's rock face, added the bridge's approach arcades, gave Vianden transparent margins and moved Adolphe Bridge southwest to remove overlap with Gëlle Fra. The benchmark comparison found no remaining subject noticeably below the required detail standard. Sculpted faces, Renaissance carving and lettering remain abbreviated; the Philharmonie's 823 real columns are represented by a reduced, spaced column screen at this pixel size. Country icons remain tightly grouped at zoom 5 because Luxembourg is small; their real sites and the existing zoom thresholds are retained. Eupen now falls within Luxembourg's 1.5° capital reach, so the existing Belgium country-only example uses De Panne instead. Athens remains uncovered.

All nineteen earlier capital PNGs, prior country/continent placements, atlas frames and existing atlas pixels remain unchanged. The seven new country-tier icons fit Luxembourg's own bounds; Vianden has transparent margins on all four sides.

All 73 tests, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint` and `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` pass. Every new 4× study was viewed beside Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks, and the completed scene was compared with all three finished scenes. Iteration corrected the castle's crenellated flag tower, differentiated the cathedral cap from St Peter's three open lanterns, detailed the Blackheads astronomical dial, moved landmarks to eliminate overlap and reserved the Opera garden before housing. No remaining landmark was identified as noticeably below the benchmark standard; faces, zodiac marks and fine sculpture/carving remain abbreviated at native pixel size. Measured bounds keep all eight landmarks inside 720×520, with zero overlapping landmark pixels and zero pixels occluded by houses, trees or other foreground objects. All pairwise longitude/latitude orderings remain intact. All 22 added country icons lie within Latvia's own bounds; previous atlas frames/pixels and continent/other-country placements are unchanged. The Riga PNG is 181,624 bytes (about 177 KiB, 1.43 MiB decoded RGBA). Real devices, native performance and Safari remain unverified. No commit was made.

**Lithuania and Vilnius (13 November 2026).** Natural Earth `LTU` uses matching country/camera bounds `[20.85, 53.85, 26.85, 56.5]` and capital anchor `25.2797°E, 54.6872°N`. The pinned capital name is `Vilnius` (`25.316635°E, 54.683366°N`). Capital-first sorting and the unchanged 75 km filter retain Vilnius, Kaunas, Klaipėda and Šiauliai. The capital has a castle and two houses, Kaunas/Klaipėda have churches between houses, and Šiauliai has a church. Seven pines and one offshore boat fit the existing country-index/coast filters. **Trakai Island Castle**, at `24.93306°E, 54.6525°N` on its actual Lake Galvė island, has an appended sprite with brick keep, ducal hall, open court, round towers, clay roofs, lake and timber bridge. The sprite was reduced after browser inspection found its original tall silhouette hiding Vilnius's nearby castle group; transparent margins protect its outline.

The layout was chosen before drawing: Neris along the northern edge; Gediminas Tower on its landscaped hill; the Three Crosses farther east; the Grand Dukes' palace below the tower and east of the cathedral; St Anne's east of St Johns'; Town Hall farther south and west; the Gate of Dawn at the southern approach. `scripts/art/landmarks_lithuania.py` builds eight true projected 3D landmark studies, a plaster-house study and the Trakai sprite study at 4× before integration. `SITES` records real coordinates and compressed north-up anchors, preserving every pairwise longitude/latitude ordering.

| Landmark | Real longitude, latitude | Drawn signature details |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gediminas Tower | 25.29069, 54.68676 | Octagonal red-brick tower, stone foundation/ruin, recessed openings, flat observation terrace, low parapet and Lithuanian tricolour |
| Three Crosses | 25.2975, 54.68639 | Exactly three thick concrete crosses, taller central stem, solid shaded arms, flared feet and stepped curved shared plinth |
| Palace of the Grand Dukes | 25.28889, 54.68611 | White courtyard ranges, low dark roofs, dressed window rows, attic panels, domed turret, chimneys and rusticated portal |
| Vilnius Cathedral and bell tower | 25.28778, 54.68583 | Six-column portico, triangular pediment, three filled saints, Helena's cross, St Casimir chapel/cupola, detached circular tiered belfry, clock and dark arched openings |
| St Anne's Church | 25.29326, 54.68304 | Three clustered Gothic brick turrets with open fingers, ogee arch hoods, lancet tracery, moulded brick courses, steep clay roof and side buttresses |
| St Johns' Church and campanile | 25.28833, 54.6825 | Undulating Baroque front, pilasters, figure niches, clay-roofed nave and separate five-stage belfry with arched openings, clock, copper cap and cross |
| Vilnius Town Hall | 25.28694, 54.67806 | Six-column portico, city shield relief, triangular pediment, dressed windows, string courses, broad steps and flag |

The Neris follows an isometric axis with two open quays, highlighted water, rowing boats and a square stone crossing. Cathedral Square, the university forecourt and Town Hall Square stay open, with distinct cobbles and a compressed Pilies/Didžioji connection. Landscaped hill parks and Bernardine Garden have trees and flower beds. Houses have framed divided windows and sills, timber doors/shopfronts, plinths, cornices, occasional iron guards, sculpted Baroque gables, clay hips/gables, dormers and capped chimneys. Footprint and measured projected silhouette reservations prevent foreground buildings and trees covering landmarks.

Coordinate records: [Gediminas](https://mapcarta.com/W24569542), [Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Cathedral), [palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Grand_Dukes_of_Lithuania), [St Anne](https://mapcarta.com/W387669997), [St Johns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Johns,_Vilnius), [Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Town_Hall), [gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gate_of_Dawn), [crosses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Crosses) and [Trakai Island Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trakai_Island_Castle). The new studies were viewed alongside Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks and the completed scene compared with their finished scenes. Daugavpils and Kaunas fall within Vilnius's existing 1.5° arrival reach, and Šiauliai falls within Riga's; Rēzekne and Klaipėda remain country-only examples. The existing arrival rules are retained.

**Slovenia and Ljubljana (13 November 2026).** Natural Earth `SVN` uses matching country/camera bounds `[13.35, 45.4, 16.65, 46.9]` and capital anchor `14.5061°E, 46.0514°N` (the city's reference coordinate, at Prešeren Square and the Triple Bridge). The pinned capital name is `Ljubljana` (`14.514969°E, 46.055288°N`). Natural Earth lists only Ljubljana and Maribor in Slovenia; both are kept by capital-first sorting and the unchanged 75 km filter. The capital has a castle and two houses and Maribor a church between houses. Slovenia is small and heavily forested, so its decorations use a 40 km spacing (as Malta and Luxembourg use their own); three oaks fit, and no sea cell passes the existing coast filter for a boat. **Bled Island**, at the pilgrimage church's own position (`14.09004°E, 46.36223°N`) rather than the lake centre or Bled town, has an appended 52×64 sprite: wooded island with a stone shore, white Church of the Assumption with clay roof and slender bell tower under its steep spire, the stair down to the landing, and lake water. Transparent margins surround it on every side, and the country view keeps Ljubljana's castle group visible below it.

The layout was chosen before drawing. The Ljubljanica runs south-west to north-east through the scene along the isometric j axis, so the Triple Bridge and the Dragon Bridge cross it squarely along i. Prešeren Square and the Franciscan Church are on the north bank; the Plečnik market colonnade follows the south bank between the two bridges; the cathedral and Vodnik Square's open market lie behind it; the Town Hall and Robba Fountain are farther south and west; Castle Hill rises in the south-east. `scripts/art/landmarks_slovenia.py` builds eight true projected 3D landmark studies, a house study and the Bled sprite study at 4× before integration. `SITES` records real coordinates and compressed north-up anchors, preserving every pairwise longitude/latitude ordering.

| Franciscan Church of the Annunciation | 14.5060028, 46.0518389 | Red-pink screen front with two tiers of white pilasters (Ionic, Corinthian capitals), side volutes, three statue niches, stone portal and stair, pediment and copper Madonna on the crest; clay nave roof |
| Prešeren Monument | 14.5063083, 46.0514083 | Three tonalite steps, block inscribed with the name, bronze poet with open frock coat and book, granite pedestal with stylised lime-tree relief, seated muse holding the laurel branch over him; Ravnikar's radial square paving around it |

The river has open stone quays, tree-lined promenades, highlighted water and rowing boats. Prešeren Square, Town Square, Cyril-Methodius Square and Vodnik Square (with market stalls) stay open with distinct paving. Castle Hill is a raised, wooded plateau with rock retaining walls. Houses have rusticated ground floors, framed two-pane windows with sills and hoods, timber doors and shopfronts, plinths, cornices, occasional iron guards, steep textured clay hips with eyebrow dormers and capped chimneys, and occasional Secession houses with ceramic friezes and copper-helmeted corner turrets. Footprint and measured projected silhouette reservations stop foreground buildings and trees from covering landmarks.

Architecture/layout and coordinate references: [Ljubljana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljubljana), [Ljubljana Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljubljana_Castle), [Ljubljana Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljubljana_Cathedral), [Franciscan Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscan_Church_of_the_Annunciation,_Ljubljana), [Prešeren Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre%C5%A1eren_Monument), [Prešeren Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre%C5%A1eren_Square), [Triple Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Bridge), [Dragon Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_Bridge_(Ljubljana)), [Central Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljubljana_Central_Market), [Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljubljana_Town_Hall), [Bled Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bled_Island), [Visit Ljubljana: castle](https://www.visitljubljana.com/en/ljubljana-castle/) and the [Around Us Bled church listing](https://aroundus.com/p/9544788-assumption-of-mary-church) for the church's GPS point. These are text references; no photographs were inspected for this capital. All artwork is original and no dependency was added.

The new studies were viewed alongside Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks and the completed scene compared with their finished scenes. Refinement replaced glass-wall windows with framed, centred openings, rebuilt the cathedral's west front between narrower towers, slimmed the statues, rebuilt the Prešeren figures, replaced invented bridge piers with lamps, corrected the bridge decks' visible faces, turned the market loggias into open colonnades, moved the Robba Fountain aside so the arcade shows, raised Castle Hill, reduced the corner turrets and moved the castle to remove a 287-pixel overlap with the cathedral. Still short of the benchmarks: the Prešeren poet and muse, the Franciscan niche saints and the river gods are abbreviated figures at native resolution; the Pentagonal Tower is drawn square in plan; the Outlook Tower's square plan, the Franciscan Church's small east bell turret and the castle flag are not confirmed by the text sources; the Triple Bridge's stairs to the lower terraces and the Dragon Bridge's sixteen small dragons are omitted or reduced to lamp finials. Maribor and Klagenfurt fall within Ljubljana's 1.5° arrival reach; Murska Sobota falls within Bratislava's. Lendava and Lienz remain country-only examples. The existing arrival rules are retained.
