**Croatia and Zagreb (13 November 2026).** Natural Earth `HRV` uses matching country/camera bounds `[13.45, 42.4, 19.45, 46.6]` (the country's 1:50m outline spans `13.52–19.40°E, 42.43–46.53°N`) and capital anchor `15.9819°E, 45.8150°N` (the city's reference coordinate, between Ban Jelačić Square and the Upper Town). The pinned capital name is `Zagreb` (`16.0°E, 45.8°N`). Capital-first sorting and the unchanged 75 km filter keep Zagreb (castle and two houses), Split and Rijeka (churches between houses), Slavonski Brod (church), Zadar (house pair) and Dubrovnik (church); Osijek, Pula, Karlovac and Šibenik fall within 75 km of a larger city. Seven trees follow the country's climate (four oaks inland, a pine in Istria, olives on Hvar and in inland Dalmatia) and two boats sit in the Adriatic. **Plitvice Lakes** is placed at Veliki slap, the great waterfall at the end of the Lower Lakes (`15.6079°E, 44.9025°N`), not the park's centre. Its appended 48×47 sprite shows a wooded travertine cliff with the white fall, a turquoise upper pool, a mossy barrier, the lower lake and a timber boardwalk, with transparent margins on every side. The first 56×64 version covered the bottom of Zagreb's castle group at country zoom (Plitvice is only 0.9° south of the capital), so it was reduced; both now stand clear at zoom 5–6.4.

The layout was chosen before drawing. Gradec (the Upper Town) is a raised 18 px plateau in the north-west, with rock retaining walls, its own warm cobbles, low Baroque houses and wooded southern slopes. St Mark's Church, the Stone Gate and Lotrščak Tower stand on it; the funicular runs down the slope beside Lotrščak. Ilica runs along the plateau's southern foot on the i axis and carries the tram line through Ban Jelačić Square. Dolac's terrace lies north of the square, Kaptol's cathedral to the east, and the Lower Town grid, the Croatian National Theatre with its park, Zrinjevac and the Art Pavilion to the south. Zagreb has no river in this part of the centre, so the scene has no water except fountains. `scripts/art/landmarks_croatia.py` builds eight true projected 3D landmark studies, a house/tram/pavilion/funicular study and the Plitvice sprite study at 4× before integration. `SITES` records real coordinates, compressed north-up anchors and elevation, preserving every pairwise longitude/latitude ordering.

| Landmark | Real longitude, latitude | Drawn signature details |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Zagreb Cathedral | 15.97972, 45.81444 | Twin neo-Gothic towers with angle buttresses, lancets, gold clock faces and paired belfry openings; gabled crowns (wimpergs) with corner pinnacles; slender octagonal openwork spires with crockets and gilded crosses, by far the tallest things in the scene; portal with stepped jambs, tympanum and wimperg; great pointed west window; buttressed nave with pinnacles, steep patinated roof and ridge turret; Kaptol rampart wall and round tower; the Virgin's column with gilded figure and four gilded angels over a fountain basin |
| St Mark's Church | 15.97389, 45.81639 | Steep roof whose lit slope is mapped in glazed tiles: the Triune Kingdom arms (chequy, Dalmatia's three leopard heads, Slavonia's band and marten under a crown) and Zagreb's white three-towered castle with open gate, star and crescent on red, on a chevron tile ground; bell tower with clock, belfry openings and tiled pyramid; buttresses and lancets; sculpted south portal with figures in niches under a gable; lower east end |
| Lotrščak Tower | 15.97306, 45.81472 | Square tower of pale rubble stone with dressed quoins, three levels of round-arched windows, the Grič cannon's muzzle at its window, corbel table, battlemented viewing platform, timber door, promenade railing and lamp; the funicular's ballast bed, sleepers, rails and blue car |
| Stone Gate | 15.97530, 45.81580 | Rusticated gate tower with a tall round-arched passage, voussoirs and the candle-lit shrine behind a wrought-iron grille; plastered upper storey with window, curved Baroque gable and niche; steep clay hip and finial; lower gatehouse wing; St George on horseback on his pillar, lance down into the dragon |
| Dolac Market | 15.97750, 45.81417 | Raised terrace over an arcaded market hall, white balustrades, twelve red Šestine umbrellas with white edging over laden stalls, the stair down to Ban Jelačić Square and Kumica Barica in bronze |
| Ban Jelačić Square | 15.97722, 45.81306 | Equestrian bronze on a stepped pedestal with plaque: four legs with one foreleg raised, arched neck and head, tail, rider with busby and sabre held out ahead; Manduševac's flush basin with water spout; the tram line with two blue trams |
| Croatian National Theatre | 15.97000, 45.80944 | Ochre-yellow front block and stage house, white pilasters, rusticated ground floor with arched openings, arched and hooded windows; columned entrance with balcony, pediment, crowning figure and gilded acroteria; corner pavilions with glazed drums and copper cupolas; drum and copper dome over the auditorium; the Well of Life's sunken basin ringed by bronze figures |
| Art Pavilion | 15.97861, 45.80722 | Long yellow exhibition hall with end pavilions, tall arched windows and bronze busts, slate hips, central iron-and-glass dome on a glazed drum, entrance porch with paired columns, pediment, statues and steps |

Squares have distinct paving: Lower Town flagstones, Ban Jelačić Square's stone with tram rails, and Gradec's warm cobbles. The theatre park has gravel paths and trees, and Zrinjevac has plane trees and its music pavilion. Houses are Lower Town historicist blocks of three to four floors in ochre, cream, grey, sage, pink, blue-grey, yellow and sand. They have rusticated shop floors, framed two-pane windows with stucco pediments and hoods, occasional iron balconies, plinths, cornices, red clay hips with lucarnes and capped chimneys, and occasional tin-grey corner cupolas; Upper Town houses are two floors. Footprint and measured projected silhouette reservations stop houses from covering landmarks, and trees are placed only where they touch no landmark pixel.

Architecture/layout and coordinate references: [Zagreb Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagreb_Cathedral) with coordinates from [Around Us](https://aroundus.com/p/7668045-zagreb-cathedral) and [Tripomatic](https://tripomatic.com/en/poi/zagreb-cathedral-poi:5523963), [St Mark's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mark%27s_Church,_Zagreb), [Lotrščak Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotr%C5%A1%C4%8Dak_Tower), [Stone Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Gate) with coordinates from [Around Us](https://aroundus.com/p/7670157-stone-gate) and [Sygic](https://travel.sygic.com/en/poi/stone-gate-poi:8221), [Dolac Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolac_Market) with coordinates from [Around Us](https://aroundus.com/p/7671713-dolac), [Ban Jelačić Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Jela%C4%8Di%C4%87_Square), [Croatian National Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_National_Theatre_in_Zagreb), [Art Pavilion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Pavilion_in_Zagreb), and Veliki slap from [Around Us](https://aroundus.com/p/7679728-veliki-slap), checked against [Plitvice Lakes info](https://www.plitvice-lakes.info/?p=76) for the neighbouring Sastavci falls. These are text references; no photographs were inspected for this capital. All artwork is original and no dependency was added.

The new studies were viewed alongside the benchmarks and the finished scene compared with the earlier finished scenes. Refinement made the cathedral spires taller and slimmer with slit tracery, moved St Mark's bell tower behind the roof so the tower no longer hides the heraldry, rebuilt the Stone Gate (first drawn as a plain house) as a rusticated tower with a large arched passage, enlarged the equestrian statue, removed an unconfirmed flag from Lotrščak, and reduced the theatre's dome and cupolas. It also moved the tram line in front of the statue, moved Zrinjevac out from behind the Art Pavilion, replaced a checkerboard park pattern with a few paths, nudged the Dolac and Lotrščak anchors to remove small overlaps, and shrank the Plitvice sprite.

Still short of the benchmarks:
- The cathedral's tympanum statuary, Mary's angels, Kumica Barica, St George, the theatre's statues and the Well of Life figures are abbreviated figures at native resolution.
- The equestrian statue reads as horse and rider only at close zoom.
- St Mark's heraldry is simplified, and its bell tower is drawn at the west end behind the roof, not at its real south-west corner.
- These details come from general knowledge, not the text sources: Lotrščak's battlemented top; the theatre's corner cupolas, central dome and south-west-facing entrance; the Art Pavilion's dome form; the cathedral roof colour; and the Stone Gate's exterior massing.
- The Ilica streetscape is mostly hidden behind houses.

Arrival reach: Karlovac and Varaždin fall within Zagreb's 1.5° reach, and Rijeka and Pula within Ljubljana's. Split, Zadar, Šibenik, Dubrovnik and Slavonski Brod remain country-only. The existing arrival rules are retained.

**Capital review and fixes (13 November 2026).** All 27 capital scenes (219 landmarks) were reviewed against real coordinates and the real buildings. Each landmark's drawn position was recorded by instrumenting the scene renderers, and real coordinates came from Wikipedia, Wikidata and OpenStreetMap. Placement was scored by order reversals between landmark pairs more than 120 m apart along either axis, plus the rank correlation of drawn against real positions.

The models were mostly sound: 135 good, 77 with minor errors, 2 wrong. Twenty-one scenes already kept their real orderings; the problems were the layouts of a few cities. The following were fixed. Where they differ, these descriptions supersede the per-capital sections above.

- **Paris:**
  - Before: the scene was mirrored east–west, with 17 reversed pairs and rank correlations of −0.43/0.20.
  - Now: the Seine flows west past Notre-Dame on the Île de la Cité, along the Louvre and Concorde on the Right Bank, then bends south-west past the Eiffel Tower on the Left Bank. The Arc de Triomphe stands north-west at the head of the Champs-Élysées, and Sacré-Cœur is on its hill to the north. Six bridges cross squarely; Pont d'Iéna uses `stone_bridge(..., along_i=True)`.
- **Madrid:**
  - Before: the city was rotated, with 21 reversed pairs.
  - Now it runs west to east: the Palacio Real with the Almudena south of it above the Manzanares, Plaza Mayor, Sol and the Casa de Correos, the Metrópolis at the fork of Gran Vía and Alcalá, Cibeles on the Prado–Recoletos boulevard, the free-standing Puerta de Alcalá, and the Retiro entirely east of the boulevard. Alfonso XII stands on the Estanque and the Palacio de Cristal is to the south-east.
- **Berlin:** the Spree now comes in from the south-east, passes south-west of the Rotes Rathaus and splits around Museum Island. Downstream it bends along the Reichstag's north and east sides. The Rathaus and TV Tower now share the east bank. A Reichstag/Brandenburg Gate overlap was removed.
- **Amsterdam:** the rings are now Herengracht, Keizersgracht and Prinsengracht, joined to the IJ by Brouwersgracht. The Westerkerk and the Anne Frank House stand on the Prinsengracht quay, west of Dam beyond the inner rings.
- **Stockholm:**
  - Kungsholmen is an island, separated from Norrmalm by Klara sjö and crossed by Stadshusbron. City Hall stands on its eastern tip, with its tower at the Riddarfjärden quay and a garden running down to the water.
  - **Nordiska museet** was rebuilt: a long symmetrical grey-stone front with copper roofs, gabled end pavilions, corner turrets and the dominant central tower over the portal, crowned by a copper helm and lantern.
- **Luxembourg:** the old town, the southern plateau, the Rham and Kirchberg are 30 px plateaus above the river valleys, with cliffs and wooded slopes.
  - The Pétrusse passes under the Adolphe Bridge and joins the Alzette in the Grund.
  - The Alzette runs past Neumünster, below the Bock and north through Pfaffenthal.
  - Mudam and the Philharmonie stand across the valley on Kirchberg.
- **Tallinn:** Toompea is a raised 24 px plateau carrying Toompea Castle and the Nevsky Cathedral. It has an east escarpment and a crenellated rim wall. SITES gained an elevation column, drawn through `lifted()` in `landmarks_estonia.py`.
- **Vilnius:** Castle Hill carries Gediminas' Tower, and the higher Hill of Three Crosses stands to its east; both are wooded. An invented bridge across the Neris beside the hill was removed.
- **Oslo:** the Opera now stands east of the cathedral, on the Bjørvika waterfront.
- **Dublin:** St Patrick's now stands directly south of Christ Church, and Christ Church just east of the Four Courts. The table's Custom House and Christ Church coordinates were corrected; they had been off by 1.2 km and 0.4 km.
- **Vienna:** the **Upper Belvedere** was rebuilt as a long, low, symmetrical front (15×4.2, up from 12×4.2). It has octagonal domed corner pavilions with lanterns, low wings under their own copper hips, and a taller central pavilion. The centre has three arched carriage openings, a curved copper roof with lantern, and a segmental pediment with gilded arms.
- **Budapest:** Matthias Church's tall Matthias tower now stands at the south-west corner and the lower Béla tower at the north-west. Both are flush with the front so the portal and rose window show.
- **Bratislava:** the Slovak flags carry the coat of arms (white double cross on three blue hills on a red shield), so they no longer read as a plain tricolour.

After the fixes, all 13 changed scenes keep every real ordering (rank correlation 1.00 on both axes). Every landmark's pixels survive unchanged, with no overlaps and no edge clipping. The other 14 capital images are byte-identical, and the map-icon atlas and placements are unchanged.

Still open from the review (not fixed here):
- **Rome:** St Peter's faces west, away from the Tiber.
- **Valletta:** the Upper Barrakka Gardens sit inland.
- **Prague:**
  - The Vltava leaves the scene to the north-west.
  - There is no Hradčany hill.
  - St Vitus is foreshortened.
- **Copenhagen:**
  - Slotsholmen isn't an island.
  - The Marble Church faces the wrong way.
- **Lisbon:** the Sé is by the water rather than uphill north of the arch.
- **Vilnius:** the cathedral bell tower is attached and short.
- **Warsaw:** the Palace of Culture has a green top.
- **Madrid:** the palace is not on a bluff.
- **Amsterdam:** the Rijksmuseum is inside Prinsengracht, and Singelgracht is not drawn.
- **Minor proportion and colour notes** (stubby spires, pyramid caps, pitched roofs on flat-roofed buildings), recorded in the review.

**Bosnia and Herzegovina / Sarajevo (13 November 2026).** The country uses Natural Earth `BIH`, bounds `[15.7, 42.5, 19.65, 45.3]`, and the exact populated-place capital name `Sarajevo`. The capital scene is a north-up, distance-compressed illustration of the Miljacka valley: Baščaršija and the religious/civic centre lie north of the river, Marijin Dvor and the National Museum to the west, Avaz north of the museum. The river enters from the east by City Hall, runs west through Latin Bridge and leaves the scene south-westward; the museum remains north of its downstream course. Isometric-axis segments preserve those banks; Latin Bridge crosses squarely. Wooded northern neighbourhood slopes and southern Bistrik/Trebević foothills rise above the valley floor. None of the seven landmarks sits on an invented hill. All art is original projected geometry, with 4× standalone studies in `docs/art/capitals/sarajevo-*.png`.

Sarajevo fact sheets researched before drawing (coordinates are longitude, latitude). The first source is Wikipedia/Wikidata; the second is independent OSM geometry, not another site repeating Wikidata. OSM polygon centres below are bounding-box centres unless stated otherwise. Sources were read on 13 November 2026.

| Landmark; adopted coordinates | Two coordinate sources and agreement | Setting, silhouette, defining feature and front orientation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Avaz Twist Tower; **18.402111, 43.860750** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avaz_Twist_Tower): 18.402111, 43.860750; [OSM building 175903111](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/175903111): 18.401998, 43.861000, about 29 m. The OSM outline includes the podium; the adopted point identifies the tower. | Marijin Dvor, inland north of the Miljacka and museum. One slender twisting blue-grey glass shaft, white structural ribbons, a cylindrical crown/observation level and a single communications mast; 142 m roof / 172 m including mast. Must dominate nearby houses. It has no principal historic façade; preserve the twist rather than invent a front. |
| Sebilj; **18.431260, 43.859720** | [Wikidata Q1062192](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q1062192): 18.431261, 43.859719; [OSM fountain 224360068](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/224360068), part of [site 20147021](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/20147021): footprint around 18.43124, 43.85972, under 3 m. | Inland Baščaršija square, north of the mosque and City Hall. Small octagonal wooden fountain kiosk on a stone base; carved lattice panels, broad projecting copper roof, shallow curved dome and finial; two opposed drinking basins/spouts. No bell tower/minaret. Radial building, no single famous front; two visible facets carry its water fittings. |
| Sacred Heart Cathedral; **18.425400, 43.859400** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_Heart_Cathedral,_Sarajevo): 18.425400, 43.859400; [OSM cathedral 474428386](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/474428386): 18.425362, 43.859422, about 4 m. | Old Town, inland on the north bank. Elongated north-north-west nave, two equal 43.2 m bell towers at the south-south-east front, octagonal rose above pointed portal, central gable, buttresses and apsidal north end. Warm grey dressed masonry, pale ochre dressings and steep dark green metal pyramidal tower roofs, each with a cross. The south-south-east front is built on its actual compass plane, turned 15° east of south; the entire building, including its long nave, rotates together. Neither tower is swapped. |
| Gazi Husrev-beg Mosque; **18.429028, 43.859170** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gazi_Husrev-beg_Mosque): 18.429028, 43.859170; [OSM mosque 609903435](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/609903435): 18.429026, 43.859147, about 3 m. | Baščaršija, inland north of Latin Bridge. Pale stone T-plan: one main dome on a windowed drum, a qibla half-dome to the south-east, two lower lateral domes and five small entrance-portico domes (nine roof volumes including the half-dome); one slender stone minaret at the western/north-west entrance corner, one balcony and pointed lead cap. Four portico columns make five arches. Entry faces north-west and is on the hidden side in this projection: retain the true orientation and explicitly accept the concealed portal. The dome hierarchy and minaret remain visible. |
| City Hall / Vijećnica; **18.433330, 43.859170** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarajevo_City_Hall): 18.433330, 43.859170; [OSM building 23723663](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/23723663): 18.433385, 43.859166, about 4 m. | Eastern old town directly on the north riverbank, behind the street/quay. Broad triangular plan with three corner risalits, projecting central south entrance, cream/ochre-red horizontal bands, horseshoe arches and patterned spandrels, crenellated ornamental cornice, grey-green hipped roofs and central polygonal hall roof. No invented minarets or onion domes. The ornate principal front faces south towards the Miljacka; render its two-storey five-bay loggia on a genuinely south-facing edge. |
| Latin Bridge; **18.428960, 43.857630** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Bridge): 18.429000, 43.857700; [OSM bridge deck 543582059](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/543582059): approximately 18.42896, 43.85763, about 8 m. Adopt the middle of the bridge, not the northern assassination-site pavement. | Across the Miljacka south of the mosque, west of City Hall. Low Ottoman stone bridge, four surviving unequal arches on three piers, solid parapets, round flood-relief eyes over the piers; no towers, statues or suspension cables. Crossing is roughly north-south (north-north-west / south-south-east); approximated by the i axis so it crosses the diagonal river squarely. |
| National Museum; **18.402710, 43.854480** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina): 18.402500, 43.854500; [OSM museum relation 7328446](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/7328446): four pavilion outlines span 18.401819–18.403607, 43.853837–43.855113, centre 18.402713, 43.854475, about 17 m. A separate GeoNames entrance pin is about 60 m farther north; it is not the complex centre. | Western Marijin Dvor, north of the river. Four separate, symmetrical neo-Renaissance pavilions around a botanical garden with low connecting ranges; ivory walls, arched windows, pilasters, pedimented central bays, parapets and low grey roofs/skylights. Long and low, no invented central dome. Main street entrance faces north and is hidden; the southern garden elevations and four-pavilion composition are visible. |

Architecture checked against [City Hall's own renovation account](https://vijecnica.ba/en/reconstruction/renovation-details), [a published mosque typology with portico/minaret plan](https://pen.ius.edu.ba/index.php/pen/article/download/702/394/1469), and the [tower's operator](https://avaztwisttower.ba/). Photographs inspected: [City Hall aerial](https://lll.ba/sarajevo-city-hall/), [Avaz photograph](https://avaz.ba/bih/sarajevo/889118/najvise-i-najljepse-zgrade-u-nasem-seheru-ove-gradjevine-dodiruju-nebo-iznad-glavnog-grada), [museum from Avaz](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:View_form_Avaz_Tower_to_Museum.jpg), plus cathedral, mosque, Sebilj and bridge reference photographs. Photographs are references only, never shipped as art.

Country landmark: **Stari Most**, Mostar, **17.815030, 43.337280**, checked against [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stari_Most) and [OSM bridge 33924615](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/33924615) / [centre node 8940870075](https://www.openstreetmap.org/node/8940870075) (17.815049, 43.337280, under 2 m). A single high hump-backed limestone arch crosses the turquoise Neretva, flanked by the Halebija and Tara bridge-keeper towers. It receives a separate native country sprite; existing atlas frames and continent placements must remain unchanged.

**Sarajevo final model and geography review.** Every study was opened, checked against its fact sheet, refined, and compared beside the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris studies; the completed 720×520 scene was also viewed beside their finished scenes. All seven models match their recorded coordinates, bank/district, major roof volumes, tower count and compass placement. All buildings remain in the valley, with the surrounding northern and south-eastern slopes raised separately. The open quay stays between City Hall's south entrance and the river; Latin Bridge actually crosses the water; the museum and Avaz remain in the western north-bank districts. No flags or unconfirmed bridge statues were added.

| Model | Signature features verified in the drawing; orientation result |
| --- | --- |
| Avaz Twist Tower | Rotating eight-facet glass mesh, turning pale structural ribbons, articulated base, cylindrical observation crown and one lattice mast. It is taller than every neighbouring house; no invented historical front. |
| Sebilj | Eight-sided timber kiosk, carved lattice, pendant eave dentils, wide projecting copper eaves, shallow ribbed dome/finial, stone steps and two basins with spouts. Radial plan retained. |
| Sacred Heart Cathedral | Two equal square bell towers at the west/east ends of the SSE front, paired belfry openings, gold-on-dark clocks, steep green pyramidal roofs and crosses, eightfold rose tracery, pointed portal, small solid robed relief figure, central gable, long roof with ridge, flank buttresses and lower rounded northern apse. The full model turns 15° towards SSE; the scene stays north-up. |
| Gazi Husrev-beg Mosque | One main dome and windowed drum, SE half-dome, two lower side domes and five NW portico domes; exactly four internal porch columns with five arch curves and end walls; one western entrance-corner minaret with one balcony and a pointed cap. The NW portal is intentionally hidden by the body, rather than placed on the visible qibla wall. |
| City Hall | Triangular plan, three corner risalits, banded cream/ochre/red masonry, two storeys of five south-loggia arches, ornamental panels/cornice, textured grey-green roofs, polygonal atrium roof and entrance steps. The rear corner is painted behind the roof, not through it. Its principal front faces the river to the south. |
| Latin Bridge | Four unequal open arches and three piers, circular flood-relief eyes, voussoirs, deck paving and solid parapets. The j-axis river is crossed along i, rounding the actual NNW/SSE crossing to the nearest tile axis. Water remains visible through the openings. |
| National Museum | Four separate low, symmetrical exhibition pavilions, arched divided windows, pilasters, rusticated plinths, cornices/pediments, grey roofs with divided glazed rooflights, lower connecting ranges and a visible botanical court. Its street entrance remains on the hidden north side. |

The scene uses čardak/shop houses with framed divided windows and sills, timber doors or shop glazing, projecting upper bays on brackets, plinths/cornices, tiled hip roofs, capped chimneys and a mix of plaster colours/heights; western variants have restrained iron balconies. Forecourts, bazaar cobbles, flagstone squares and quays have separate paving. Trees and gardens accompany the raised slopes. The shallow urban Miljacka has highlights and no invented boat traffic.

Refinement corrected roof draw order, the minaret's western corner, cathedral tower visibility/front orientation, the nave's long proportions and rounded apse, the portico column count, and the museum's masonry/window/rooflight detail. Stari Most was rebuilt at native **40×34** instead of 56×48 because its first version overlapped Sarajevo's castle group at country zoom. Opaque-pixel checks now find no bridge/capital-group overlap at zoom 5, 5.5 or 6.4. Symbols still crowd during the initial 4.4 fade-in; no real site was moved. The original country-index filters remain, with an additional Neum-only boat candidate box preventing Bosnia's overlapping bounds from allocating boats in Croatian waters.

Still below the finest benchmark detail: City Hall's arabesques are pixel motifs, Sebilj's fine wood carving/lattice is abbreviated, and the cathedral's small relief figure has no facial detail. Avaz's continuous curved curtain is approximated with eight rotating mesh facets. The mosque's NW portal and museum's north entrance are concealed by the correct view direction; they are not claimed as visible signature details. No unconfirmed major tower, dome, bridge or flag remains.

**Sarajevo verification (13 November 2026):**

- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_bosnia.py` generates seven isolated 4× landmark studies, a house study and the native Stari Most study. All seven isolated landmark silhouettes fit their preview canvases. The cathedral study's origin was raised to keep its entire long model inside the plot.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_capitals.py` regenerates the full capital registry and literal image index. After the final modelling refinements, Sarajevo was published directly from `build_capitals.sarajevo()` with the generator's same atomic PNG-save routine. The published image matches the final renderer pixel-for-pixel: **720×520, 134,392 bytes** (1.43 MiB decoded RGBA).
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py sarajevo` prints **OK**: seven landmarks; east/x and south/y rank correlations **1.00**; no reversed pairs, no overlaps, and **hidden=0 / clipped=0** for every landmark.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py` passes. All **17** added country icons fit Bosnia's bounds. All **27** preceding capital PNGs are byte-identical; every earlier atlas frame/name/pixel and every existing continent/country icon placement is preserved. The atlas remains 73 px tall; its new bridge frame is appended at x=2603, width=40, height=34.
- `npm test`: **81/81 pass**, including Bosnia's country groups, 75 km spacing, bounds, landmark, arrival and release checks, plus the existing all-capitals/all-edges panning test. Athens remains the uncovered example. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass. Self-review found no app-code regression or dependency/configuration changes.
- `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` passes. SHA-256 checks confirm that both Android and iOS manifests reference assets matching the final Sarajevo scene and atlas.
- Chrome/Playwright verifies country URLs at zoom 4.4, 5, 5.7 and 6.4, Mostar at 5.5, Sarajevo at 7 and neighbouring Zagreb at 7. Desktop 1000×780 and phone 390×844 layouts pass scroll-wheel arrival (pixel-identical to direct arrival) and repeated drag tests at all eight scene edges. A fresh final server verifies final renders, served asset hashes, and no console/runtime errors. Port 8081 already belonged to the user's app, so testing used a temporary `npm run web -- --port 8102` server and left the existing server alone.
- Physical devices, native frame rate, Safari and a signed native build were not verified. No dependency was added and no commit was made. Existing user edits to this plan, README, art contract, agent prompt and checker were preserved.

**Sarajevo cathedral correction (17 November 2026).** The earlier visual review missed a real modelling error: the visible west side wall was absent, and one full-width roof stood over the whole nave/aisle footprint. The east wall alone could not close that visible side, so the roof read as a floating slab. The length also assigned the entire church's proportions to the nave before adding the apse and frontage. The placement checker passed because it checks landmark order and visibility, not architectural completeness.

Rechecked the [Sarajevo Navigator cathedral photo gallery](https://www.sarajevonavigator.ba/en/place/78), the [aerial view used by ThePlace87](https://www.theplace87.com/about-us), and the [Catholic bishops' architectural account](https://www.ktabkbih.net/hr/predstavljamo/katedrala-srca-isusova-u-sarajevu/510). The cathedral still uses the two independently checked coordinates above. Its north-up anchor and SSE orientation are unchanged. The corrected model has both long aisle walls, pointed framed side windows, lit west buttresses, a projecting north-west cross arm, a narrower raised nave above two lower lean-to aisle roofs, a shorter body so the entire church retains its approximately 42:21 length/width ratio, dark patinated copper with standing seams, steeper equal pyramidal tower caps, five blind lancets in the front gable and the small octagonal northern ridge turret visible in the aerial photograph. It has **two main bell towers and one small roof turret**, not a third full bell tower. The existing rose window, clocks, portal, relief figure, crosses and lower northern apse remain.

The individual 4× cathedral study and the full Sarajevo scene were regenerated and visually compared with the reference photographs and the earlier image. Only Sarajevo's published world PNG changed; all other world PNGs match the hashes captured at the start of this correction. This supersedes the earlier cathedral model description and completeness claim. Fine stone tracery and the small relief figure remain simplified at native pixel resolution. Physical-device appearance remains unverified.

Verification for this correction: `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_bosnia.py` and `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py sarajevo` pass; the latter reports seven landmarks, rank correlation 1.00 on both axes, hidden=0, clipped=0 and no overlaps or reversed pairs. The corrected scene is 136,355 bytes and matches the renderer pixel-for-pixel. All 81 tests, TypeScript, Expo lint, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Android/iOS exports pass and both reference the corrected scene by SHA-256. Fresh Chrome renders at desktop and phone sizes load the final bytes without console/runtime errors. Other world PNGs and the user's separate capital edits were preserved. No commit was made.

**Serbia / Belgrade (17 November 2026).** Research and layout were established before drawing. Natural Earth `SRB`, bounds `[18.8, 42.2, 23.1, 46.2]`, capital spelling `Belgrade`; the scene/camera anchor is `20.4600, 44.8125`. All six sites are on the old city's side of the rivers: east/right bank of the Sava and south/right bank of the Danube. The Sava comes from the south-west and joins the Danube immediately north-west of Kalemegdan; the Danube continues east. The fortress occupies a raised 22-pixel promontory, the Lower Town is below it, and Saint Sava stands on a separate raised Vračar plateau. No invented central river or bridge is needed. Distances are compressed with north up and west left.

Belgrade fact sheets (longitude, latitude). Wikipedia coordinates were independently compared with OSM geometry fetched from Overpass; OSM centres are bounding-box centres. Architectural evidence also includes the [fortress operator's Victor description](https://www.beogradskatvrdjava.co.rs/споменик-победник/?lang=la), the [National Museum's building account](https://www.narodnimuzej.rs/about-museum/locations-of-the-national-museum/the-national-museum-in-belgrade-central-building/?lang=en), the [Belgrade heritage institute's Ljubica dossier](https://beogradskonasledje.rs/wp-content/uploads/2012/06/Konak_kneginje_Ljubice.pdf), and [Parliament's architectural account](https://www.parlament.gov.rs/citizens%60-corner/education-center/cultural-heritage/architecture.701.html). These sources were read on 17 November 2026.

| Landmark; adopted coordinates | Two independent coordinate sources | Setting, silhouette, defining feature, orientation and model checks |
| --- | --- | --- |

One bronze standing male on one fluted Doric column and cubic stepped base, sword in right hand and falcon in left; faces west/north-west over the rivers. Low coursed ramparts, grass and open terrace, rather than an invented towered castle. Model checks: column, substantial anatomical figure, both attributes, westward gaze, elevated river setting. |

The river-facing west/south-west garden front is visible; the opposite street entrance is not duplicated. Famous square front faces south-east, drawn on the shaded +i face; central and end pavilions retain their order. No equestrian statue is claimed as part of the building model. |

Broad symmetrical pale-stone wings and projecting end pavilions; central columned portico, triangular pediment, large copper dome on an octagonal drum with lantern and two smaller end-pavilion copper domes, paired bronze horse-and-handler groups at the entrance steps. Main front faces south-west towards Nikola Pašić Square and is drawn on +j. No unbuilt roof sculptures are invented. |

The Wikipedia point is inside the west arm; the OSM centre includes the eastern altar extension. Both identify the same church. | Inland on Vračar plateau, south-east of the whole historic core. Broad white marble Greek-cross mass, dominant green copper dome, four supporting half-domes, four bell-tower turrets with smaller caps, subordinate roofs, reddish granite cornices, arched portals and gold crosses. Overall east–west length is slightly greater than north–south width (91×81 m). West entrance is snapped to south-west, explicitly the same illustrative turn as the cathedral. The model must show the primary dome/half-dome/tower structure, not a cluster of interchangeable onion domes; tiny subsidiary roof crosses are simplified. |

**Country landmark:** [Golubac Fortress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golubac_Fortress), **21.6784847, 44.6611873**, compared with [OSM way 877802551](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/877802551), 21.67838,44.66116 (about 9 m). It is on the south bank of the Danube at the Iron Gate entrance, east of Golubac town: grey limestone walls climbing a rocky spur, a dominant hilltop keep and lower square towers, the later polygonal cannon tower at the water. Country sprite is an intentionally compressed selection of the fortress's towers, rather than a claim to depict every tower.


Belgrade visual/model review, after regenerating every 4× study and comparing it beside the Lisbon, Madrid and Paris benchmarks:

| Landmark | Model result against the fact sheet |
| --- | --- |
| Kalemegdan / Victor | Matches the elevated western terrace, single fluted column, stepped cubic base and one solid bronze figure with separate legs, torso, head, bent left arm/falcon and lowered right-hand sword. The head/falcon point west; no invented castle towers. Muscle, facial and feather detail is reduced to pixel highlights. |
| Saint Michael's Cathedral | Matches one western bell tower, two visible clock faces, a slender multi-stage copper helm/lantern/cross, long closed nave walls with pilasters and arched windows, east apse, pitched green roof and entrance steps. The western front's gold-framed icon panels are represented, with their painted figures reduced to gold/blue pixels. Its approximately 45° illustrative turn to SW is explicit, not a mirrored city layout. |
| Princess Ljubica's Residence | Matches the low white two-storey konak, broad brown clay hip, deep eaves, close timber-framed divided windows, rounded projecting upper divanhana and four capped roof chimneys. The garden/Sava-facing façade is shown; the reverse street façade and interior are not claimed. |
| National Museum | Matches the long red/cream academic palace, framed paired-window bays, ground rustication/arches, pilasters, cornices, balustrade, central clock crest and three green copper domes, the middle dome larger with a lantern. Square-facing façade is on the SE/+i wall. Small allegorical reliefs are simplified to stone crest/scroll volumes. |
| National Assembly | Matches the broad low wings and both end pavilions, a genuine open four-column portico, pediment, stepped entrance, central octagonal drum and copper dome/lantern, two smaller terminal domes, lamps and two volumetric bronze horse-and-handler groups. The SW/+j front faces the open square. Fine Corinthian carving and the horse groups' anatomy are stylised, not full sculpture replicas. |
| Saint Sava | Matches the dominant green dome and gold cross, four supporting half-domes, four main bell-tower volumes (the far one occluded naturally by the crossing), lower western chapel turrets, tall dark arched openings, three western portals, west oculus and white walls with reddish granite bands. Foreground tower caps paint in front of the drum; they no longer disappear. Greek-cross width is slightly longer E–W; the west front is explicitly snapped to SW. Tiny subsidiary roof crosses and mosaics are simplified; the interior is not represented. |

No unresolved landmark silhouette, handedness, placement, clipping or external-occlusion failures remain. Small sculptural/mural detail falls short of photographic fidelity as identified above; none of the six remains a plain-box or flat-cutout placeholder. The final scene was also inspected against the fact sheets: Sava and Danube meet north-west of the raised Upper Town; all six sites stay on the old-city banks; Saint Sava has raised Vračar ground. Open quays, squares and gardens protect the water and landmarks. Buildings and roofs have lit/shaded material ramps, courses, dressed openings and texture. There are two small boats on navigable water and no invented bridge or national flag.

Serbia's generated country tier contains **18 icons**: Belgrade's castle/two houses; Niš and Kragujevac's church/two houses each; Subotica's church; three northern oaks, four southern pines and Golubac. Novi Sad and Zrenjanin fail Belgrade's unchanged 75 km exclusion, and Čačak fails Kragujevac's. No offshore boats fit this landlocked country. All generated Serbian sites are inside the shared coverage bounds. Golubac's native 44×52 study was inspected with its hilltop keep entirely in frame. Existing continent/country icon placements, frame indices and atlas-prefix RGBA bytes are unchanged.

Verification for Belgrade:

This also reproduced five pre-existing source/PNG discrepancies (Rome, Copenhagen, Prague, Warsaw, Vilnius); their starting PNG bytes were restored to avoid unrelated asset changes. Every earlier capital PNG and all other world PNGs, apart from the intentionally extended atlas, match the starting hashes. Belgrade's published PNG matches the checker render pixel for pixel.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py belgrade` prints **OK**: six landmarks, east/x and south/y rank correlation **1.00**, no reversed pairs or overlaps, and **hidden=0, clipped=0** for each.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py` passes: 1,366 total world icons; the appended Golubac frame preserves the prior atlas prefix.
- `npm test`: **83/83 passed**, including Serbia city grouping/75 km spacing/bounds and Belgrade focal arrival, overlap retention, bounded panning and release.
- `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass.
- `npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-belgrade-export-final --max-workers 2` passes. Both native export asset lists contain the final Belgrade PNG and icon atlas, verified by SHA-256.
- `npm run web -- --port 8081`: Chrome at **1000×780** and **390×844** passes country/Golubac/capital URLs, scroll-wheel arrival (pixel-identical to direct arrival), all four pan-edge stops at both sizes, and adjacent Sarajevo/Zagreb visits. Served Belgrade/atlas bytes match disk SHA-256; no console or runtime errors. The pre-existing server on port 8102 was left alone.

**Greece / Athens (17 November 2026).** Research and layout before modelling: seven landmarks, scene id `athens`, centre `23.7275,37.9838`, Natural Earth `GRC`, exact populated-place capital name `Athens`, and matching country/camera bounds `[19.3,34.7,29.7,41.8]` including Crete, Rhodes and Kastellorizo. North stays up. The Ancient Agora and Agoraios Kolonos lie northwest of the Acropolis; the Roman Agora lies north of it. The Academy is farthest north, Parliament east/south of the Academy, the stadium farthest east/south. The Acropolis is a raised limestone plateau, not a flat square. The Erechtheion is north and slightly west of the Parthenon, with its own uneven platform. Hephaestus occupies the smaller Agoraios Kolonos hill. National Garden lies south of Parliament and north of the stadium; the latter sits in the hollow between Agra and Ardittos. Central Athens has no coast, harbour or navigable river: no invented water or boats. The buried Ilisos is omitted. Real distances compress; pairwise compass order is retained.

Athens fact sheets (coordinates are **longitude, latitude**, checked 17 November 2026). The first coordinate in each row is the value used by `SITES`. OSM values below are bounding-box centres of the actual building geometry, not tourist signs or squares. All independent pairs agree within 12 m. The architecture is based on the linked institutional sources, photographs and building plans. Construction scaffolding is omitted from the permanent architectural illustration.

| Landmark | Coordinate source 1 / adopted value | Independent coordinate source 2 | Setting, silhouette, defining feature and front |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Parthenon | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parthenon): `23.7266,37.9715` | [OSM way 910010406](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/910010406): `23.72661765,37.97150335` (2 m) | Highest Acropolis terrace; long, roofless Pentelic-marble Doric temple, originally 8 end columns × 17 side positions, no tower/dome. Fluted tapering drums, echinus/abacus capitals, three-step crepis, triglyph/metope entablature and broken pediments define it. Eastern principal front is hidden; the visible western rear pediment snaps west to SW by a 45° building turn, never a city rotation. Present ruins, not a complete ancient tiled roof. |
| Erechtheion | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erechtheion): `23.7265,37.9721` | [OSM way 910010334](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/910010334): `23.7264544,37.97210115` (4 m) | Northern Acropolis, slightly west/north of Parthenon; asymmetric roofless marble Ionic complex on differing levels. East porch has six columns, north porch four plus two returns, southwest south porch six Caryatids (four forward, two returns). Maidens carry the flat carved entablature; no spire/dome. Main east and north entrances remain hidden. Southern Caryatid porch snaps south to SW, placing it at the western (`-i`) end of the lit wall. |
| Temple of Hephaestus | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Hephaestus): `23.7213917,37.9756167` | [OSM way 27917370](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/27917370): `23.7214117,37.9756040` (2 m) | Agoraios Kolonos above the western Ancient Agora; long, low intact Doric peristyle, 6 end × 13 side columns, marble walls, surviving pitched roof, no tower/dome. Deep open colonnade, triglyph frieze and triangular pediments distinguish it from Parthenon's broken shell. East principal front remains hidden; west rear snaps SW, a 45° building turn. |
| Tower of the Winds | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tower_of_the_Winds): `23.7270701,37.974256` | [OSM relation 11199493](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/11199493): `23.72702755,37.9741963` (8 m) | Eastern Roman Agora, north of Acropolis, inland. One eight-sided Pentelic-marble tower, low eight-facet pyramidal marble roof, eight carved wind panels above sundials. Two small porches face NW and NE; a lower cylindrical water-clock annex projects south. These directions are retained: entrances mostly hidden, southern annex visible. Ancient Triton vane is lost and is not invented on the modern roof. |
| Academy of Athens | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Athens_(modern)): `23.73417,37.98000` | [OSM relation 11640079](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/11640079): `23.73410815,37.98009615` (12 m) | Panepistimiou, northernmost selected site; low neoclassical marble centre with lower wings, six Ionic portico columns, sculpted pediment and clay roofs. Two separate tall Ionic columns carry Athena NW and Apollo SE; seated Plato NW and Socrates SE flank stairs. Front faces SW and uses the lit wall: `-i` holds Athena/Plato, `+i` Apollo/Socrates. No dome/bell tower. |
| Hellenic Parliament / Old Royal Palace | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Royal_Palace): `23.73694,37.97528` | [OSM relation 2150086](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/2150086): `23.7369949,37.9752656` (5 m) | Eastern edge of Syntagma Square, National Garden south; broad three-storey ochre/cream palace with low clay hipped roofs, restrained central six-column portico and pediment, no tower/dome. West-facing forecourt and the Tomb of the Unknown Soldier below it define the setting. West front snaps SW by 45°; relief of reclining hoplite, stairs and blue/white cross-and-nine-stripe Greek flag are included. |
| Panathenaic Stadium | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panathenaic_Stadium): `23.74111,37.96833` | [OSM relation 19023844](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/19023844): `23.74098295,37.9683095` (11 m) | Southeast of centre, hollow between Agra and Ardittos, inland. Elongated U of white Pentelic-marble seating, open NW, closed curved SE end, dark running track; no roof/dome/towers. Long axis snaps to `+i` (SE), preserving the real opening. Stepped tiers, diazoma and radial aisles define the model; exact 47 seating rows compress into fewer visible bands. |

Architecture references: [Acropolis Restoration Service: Parthenon](https://www.ysma.gr/en/monuments/parthenon/), [YSMA: Erechtheion](https://www.ysma.gr/en/monuments/erechtheion/), [Acropolis Museum: Caryatid porch](https://www.theacropolismuseum.gr/en/other-monuments-periklean-building-programme/erechtheion), [Perseus archaeology: Hephaisteion](https://perseus-aa.github.io/items/aa_82.html), [Notre Dame archaeological image record: Hephaisteion](https://marble.nd.edu/item/473b9ab5-7c7a-4b04-9d20-5e4ec1bf424e), [Official Athens Guide: Tower of the Winds](https://www.thisisathens.org/antiquities/tower-winds), [Academy: building](https://www.academyofathens.gr/en/academy/megaro/eisagogi), [Parliament: building history](https://www.hellenicparliament.gr/en/Vouli-ton-Ellinon/ToKtirio/Istoria-Ktiriou/), [stadium owner: setting/history](https://www.panathenaicstadium.gr/en/archaio-panathinaiko-stadio/) and [Olympic reference collection: NW entrance / SE sphendon](https://library.olympics.com/digitalCollection/DigitalCollectionAttachmentDownloadHandler.ashx?documentId=184661&parentDocumentId=184660&skipCopyright=true&skipWatermark=true). All pixel art is original; external photographs are inspection references only.

Country landmark: **Holy Trinity Monastery, Meteora**, `21.6358,39.7132` ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monastery_of_the_Holy_Trinity,_Meteora)); [OSM way 379736577](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/379736577) centre `21.6355739,39.71327175` agrees within 21 m. Monastery perched on an isolated sheer sandstone pillar northeast of Kalambaka, low warm stone buildings and red clay roofs around a small courtyard, compact domed katholikon, hewn stair access. No fantasy castle towers or connecting bridge. [Meteora–Pyli Geopark](https://www.meteorapyligeopark.gr/en/item/holy-monastery-of-agia-triada/) and [Visit Greece](https://www.visitgreece.gr/en/experiences/nature/religious-tourism/meteora-monasteries) confirm its setting. The small sprite compresses the complex; tiny frescoes and an exact survey of every room are outside its resolution.

**Athens model audit after isolated studies and integration.** All seven final 4× studies were viewed beside the Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks; the house study, Meteora sprite and final scene were also inspected. Each row checks setting, count/proportion, defining feature and front direction against the fact sheet above. The city remains north-up; individual facade snaps are explicit rather than accidental.

| Model | Setting / silhouette / defining feature / orientation check | Pixel-scale limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Parthenon | Matches: raised 40-pixel Acropolis terrace; long roofless 8×17-position peristyle with four shortened southern drums; tapered/fluted shafts, Doric capitals, three steps, open ruined cella, interrupted triglyph/metope entablature and pedimental fragments; hidden east front and visible SW-snapped west rear. | Exact current restoration-block positions and every fracture are not surveyed; the southern gap is compressed. Fine marble relief subjects are omitted rather than reconstructed. |
| Erechtheion | Matches: 32-pixel northern terrace, northwest of Parthenon; asymmetric open naos; six east columns, six north-porch supports and four western engaged columns; six solid draped Caryatids, four forward/two returns, carry the flat moulded southwest porch; hidden east/north entrances and explicitly SW-snapped south face. | Caryatid faces, hair and exact floral Ionic carving are abbreviated. Differing room levels are compressed into the lower porch/platform. |
| Hephaestus | Matches: raised 16-pixel Agoraios Kolonos terrace west/north of Acropolis; low 6×13 open Doric peristyle, sheltered naos, continuous entablature, triangular ends and textured clay pitched roof; east front hidden and west rear snapped SW. | Roof tiles, metopes and slender flutes are sampled at native pixels; no narrative sculptural identities claimed. |
| Tower of the Winds | Matches: eastern Roman Agora north of Acropolis, below its plateau; one eight-sided marble tower, eight-facet low roof, visible wind-figure panels and incised sundial rays; southern cylindrical water-clock annex; northwest/northeast porches lie behind it. The lost Triton vane is omitted. | Eight wind identities are not individually legible. Tiny porch capitals and interior hydraulic machinery are not resolved; the visible reliefs retain solid heads/drapery/arms. |
| Academy | Matches: northernmost selected building; long low marble centre/wings, six-column Ionic portico, sculpted coloured pediment, stairs and textured clay roof; two tall deity columns, Athena NW/Apollo SE, and seated Plato NW/Socrates SE; SW frontage visible. | Pedimental story, faces, lyre strings and inscriptions are abbreviated. Solid Athena retains shield/spear/helmet and Apollo his lyre. |
| Parliament | Matches: broad three-storey ochre palace east of Syntagma, National Garden south; low textured clay hips, cornices, dressed windows and six-column west portico/pediment; tomb's reclining hoplite relief, terrace stairs and Greek flag with nine stripes plus cross canton; west frontage snapped SW. | Tiny tomb inscription, guard uniforms and internal courtyard survey are omitted; dressed wall openings and the relief are retained. |
| Stadium | Matches: southeasternmost landmark, inside the Agra/Ardittos hollow with low raised flanking slopes; elongated white-marble horseshoe, curved SE sphendon and open NW end; stepped seating, diazoma/aisles, coursed outer retaining shell and dark track with lane lines. | Forty-seven actual rows compress to twelve stepped bands; royal-box details and lettering are omitted. Lower front seating is naturally screened by its own outer wall in this projection. |

No landmark is judged below the benchmark detail standard after refinement. Remaining limitations are fine sculpture/lettering, compressed row counts, the Parthenon's exact restoration-fracture layout, and a schematic Meteora room/stair survey. Preview iteration corrected the Academy's untextured roof, the Tower of the Winds' porch paint order and missing visible sundials, Erechtheion's western column count, and the stadium's noisy curve-facet outlines. Final geography review added the stadium's flanking slopes. Meteora's origin was lifted two pixels to retain transparent margins around its entire pillar. House variants include framed divided glazing/sills, shopfronts/doors, shutters, plinths/cornices, projecting iron balconies, pediments, tile seams/antefixes and capped chimneys. No guessed flags, spires or water traffic were added.

Greece's country tier has **28 icons**: Athens' castle/two houses; Thessaloniki and Patra church/two-house towns; Iraklio, Larissa, Ioanina, Hania, Kalamata and Kavala alternating church/house groups; seven trees (three pines, one oak, three olives), two offshore boats and Meteora. Natural Earth's Piraeus, Volos, Agrinio and Chalkida are suppressed by the unchanged 75 km rule. Every new icon lies inside the matching country/camera bounds. The existing 1.5° Skopje arrival reach includes Thessaloniki; this remains supported and tested. Bucharest replaces Athens as the uncovered-city example in prior camera/projection tests.

**Romania / Bucharest (17 November 2026).** Seven defining landmarks, researched before drawing. Scene `bucharest`, centre `26.1025,44.4268`; Natural Earth `ROU`, exact capital `Bucharest`; shared country/camera bounds `[20.2,43.6,29.8,48.3]`. North is up. The Arch of Triumph is northwest of the centre; the Athenaeum is north/east of the Royal Palace. CEC lies south/east of the Royal Palace, Stavropoleos south/east of CEC. Parliament is west/south of the old centre; the historic Patriarchal Cathedral is east/south of Parliament but west of Stavropoleos. The Dâmbovița comes from the northwest, bends northeast near Izvor, then southeast past the old centre; a stepped isometric course keeps CEC/Stavropoleos on its northern/left bank and Parliament/Patriarchal Hill on its southern/right bank. Parliament's Dealul Spirii terrace rises 16 art pixels; Dealul Mitropoliei rises 18. Cișmigiu gardens sit west of the Royal Palace, the Athenaeum has its own forecourt garden, and Izvor Park lies north of Parliament. Distances are strongly compressed, never mirrored. The canal is narrow and non-navigable here: no invented river boats or harbour. The selected cathedral is the historic Saints Constantine and Helen church, not the larger National Cathedral west of Parliament.

Bucharest fact sheets: coordinates are **longitude, latitude**, checked 17 November 2026. Wikipedia/building-owner coordinates are compared with independent OpenStreetMap building geometry (bounding-box centres, not nearby tourist markers). All seven pairs agree within 15 m. Front bearings are inferred from the OSM footprints/entrances and reference photographs; diagonal snapping is explicit below. Original procedural pixel art, with reference photographs used only for inspection.

| Landmark | Source 1 / adopted coordinate | Independent source 2 | Setting, silhouette, defining feature, orientation |

One broad stone arch, one passage, flat attic, no dome or rooftop statue. Rusticated piers, voussoirs, relief groups, royal portrait roundels and inscription panels. Southern ceremonial face snaps SW to the lit +j wall (about 45°); north rear stays hidden. |
| Romanian Athenaeum | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Athenaeum): `26.0973,44.4413` | [OSM way 16291602](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/16291602): `26.0973607,44.4413680` (9 m) | Inland garden north/east of Royal Palace. Cream/ochre circular concert hall, one large ribbed metal dome with oculi, six Ionic columns under one triangular pediment and five ruler mosaic medallions behind. SW entrance uses lit +j side. No bell tower. |
| Royal Palace / National Museum of Art | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Palace_of_Bucharest): `26.095943,44.439362` | [OSM way 232472401](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/232472401): `26.0958630,44.43948895` (15 m) | West of Calea Victoriei/Revolution Square, inland. | CEC Palace | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CEC_Palace): `26.0965,44.4320` | [OSM way 973712798](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/973712798): `26.09645775,44.4320259` (4 m) | West side of Calea Victoriei, inland/north of Dâmbovița. West front snaps SW (+j), about 45°; apse at opposite NE end. |
| Palace of Parliament | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Parliament): `26.08750,44.42750` | [OSM relation 2230391](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/2230391): `26.08740855,44.4274916` (7 m) | Raised Dealul Spirii south/right of river, Izvor Park north, Constitution Square east. Vast broad pale limestone/marble palace, stepped central upper block, symmetrical lower wings, layered terraces, rows of paired monumental columns and recessed windows, central balcony; no dome/spire. Eastern ceremonial front snaps SE to shaded +i wall (about 45°); long facade runs +j. Width must dominate local houses. |

Long white trefoil church, lead-grey roof, **four** polygonal drum towers with shallow caps and crosses: tall eastern nave tower, central western pronaos tower, two smaller western lateral towers. Western open porch, carved capitals, rope belt, arcaded blind niches and painted icon band define it. West front snaps SW (+j), about 45°; western tower pair stays at the SW/front end, eastern tower at NE/rear. Detached historical gate-belfry is outside this church-only footprint. |

Architecture/reference checks: [George Enescu Philharmonic](https://filarmonicaenescu.ro/ro/experienta-concert), [Romanian Orthodox Church: historic cathedral](https://patriarhia.ro/en/the-patriarch/the-patriarchal-ensemble/patriarchal-cathedral/), [Stavropoleos: church and courtyard](https://stavropoleos.ro/biserica-2-2/), [Chamber of Architects: Stavropoleos decoration](https://artsandculture.google.com/asset/stavropoleos-church-ion-mincu/7QEiYv4paBod0A), [National Museum of Art: Royal Palace](https://mail.mnar.ro/en/discover/historic-spaces-of-the-royal-palace), [CEC architectural survey](https://radoi.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/09/RE_3DLS_Palatul_CEC_Bucuresti.pdf), [Parliament architectural collection](https://artsandculture.google.com/story/qwWRRKghIAUA8A?hl=en-GB). Building orientation comes from map geometry, not assumptions about which facade is lit.

Country landmark: **Bran Castle**, `25.36722,45.51500` ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bran_Castle)); [OSM relation 3300200](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/3300200) centre `25.36709265,45.51508265` agrees within 14 m. [Castle owner](https://bran-castle.com/) and [Romanian Film Office](https://www.filmoffice.ro/en/public/locations/bran-castle) document the rocky hill, irregular pale-walled courtyard complex, steep clay roofs, southwest round tower with conical roof and tall rectangular tower. The native sprite retains these forms; no fictional Dracula imagery or invented flags.

**Bucharest model audit, completed 18 November 2026.** All seven final isolated studies, the house study, Bran and the finished scene were viewed. Final palace, dome, church and arch studies were compared side by side with the requested Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks. Ground and bank placement were inspected separately from the automatic silhouette checker. A final river refinement keeps the complete cathedral terrace south of the channel; building terrace reservations never raise water/quay cells.

| Landmark | Model versus fact sheet | Resolution/interpretation limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Arcul de Triumf | Matches: northwest site; one genuinely open barrel passage with extruded voussoirs, two heavy rusticated piers, flat attic/cornices, south-face relief groups, portrait roundels and inscription panel. Southern front is deliberately snapped SW; no invented roof statue or flag. | Individual relief figures, royal portraits and lettering are sampled relief marks; text is not represented as readable writing. |
| Romanian Athenaeum | Matches: garden north/east of Royal Palace; circular ochre/cream hall, one ribbed metal dome, drum openings, six separate Ionic columns with volutes under the pediment, five small mosaic medallions, deep recessed portico and stairs. SW front visible on +j. | Exact oculus/window count, roof metalwork and mosaic identities are abbreviated. The five portrait medallions partly recede behind the real colonnade in this view. |
| Royal Palace | Matches: broad low U-plan opening east onto the square; pale three-storey wings, central giant pilasters, arched dressed openings, balcony/canopy, cornices, low roofs and balustrades. East court/front snapped SE; open court retained. | Individual window totals, precise curved transition bays and sculptural capitals are compressed. The roof balustrade is sampled at separated native pixels. |
| CEC Palace | Matches: inland north bank west of Stavropoleos; central glazed ribbed dome plus four smaller corner cupolas, dressed windows/rustication, paired columns with volute/leaf capital accents, semicircular entrance gable and clock, divided glazed/wood doors and steps. E/SE front on shaded +i side. | The fourth/rear corner cupola is naturally occluded by the central dome. Statuary, coats of arms and fine glass panel counts are abbreviated. The entrance recess is bounded glazing/wood, not a second invented open passage. |
| Stavropoleos | Matches: Lipscani north bank east/south of CEC; compact trefoil plan with lateral/eastern apses, one octagonal cupola under clay cap, textured broad clay roofs, four carved porch columns/three scalloped open arches, rope-like belts and painted medallions. West porch snapped SW; apse remains opposite NE. | Saint identities, vegetal scrolls and exact carved capitals are sampled. The surrounding monastery/lapidarium is outside this church-only footprint. |
| Parliament | Matches: dominant broad limestone/marble mass on 16-pixel Dealul Spirii, south bank west of old centre and north of Patriarchal Cathedral; lower wings and successive central setbacks, dressed windows, paired monumental columns, terrace cornices/balustrades, central balcony and steps. East ceremonial face explicitly snapped SE. | Repeated window/floor counts and exact internal courtyards are compressed; large flat roof terraces remain architectural surfaces. No invented dome, spire or flag. |
| Historic Patriarchal Cathedral | Matches: 18-pixel Dealul Mitropoliei on south bank, east/south of Parliament and west of Stavropoleos; white trefoil shell, four polygonal towers with recessed blind arches, slit openings, lead caps and crosses. Tall nave tower is at NE/rear; central western tower and two smaller western lateral towers stay at SW/front. Open western porch, six supporting columns/five arches and icon band retained, west front snapped SW. | Tiny icons, carved capitals and precise niche counts are sampled. The separate gate-belfry and the new National Cathedral are not included in this historic church footprint. |

No remaining silhouette, count, handedness, external occlusion or clipping fault was found. No landmark is judged below the benchmark architectural detail standard after refinement; miniature portraits, relief identities and inscriptions still fall short of literal/photo-level reproduction, as listed per model above. House variants have framed two-pane glazing/sills, recessed shops or doors, plinths/cornices, iron balconies/corbels, pediments, textured roofs, spaced dormers and capped chimneys. Unconfirmed facade bearings are not claimed as surveyed angles: the bearings are map/photo inferences and explicit diagonal snaps. Bran's sprite preserves the rock, irregular courtyard wings, clay roofline, round southwest tower, rectangular tower and timber gallery; exact room/tower surveying and fine carving are outside its native 60×76 resolution. Its alpha bounds `(6,1,54,74)` retain transparent margins.

Romania's generated country tier adds **27 placements**: Bucharest's castle/two houses; Iași and Cluj-Napoca church/two-house towns; Timișoara, Galați, Brașov, Craiova, Constanța and Oradea alternate churches/house pairs. Ploiești fails the unchanged 75 km capital exclusion; Brăila also fails Galați's, after the selected groups reach the nine-city cap. Seven trees comprise one Carpathian pine and six oaks. One offshore boat fits the unchanged 150 km boat spacing inside the Romanian Black Sea coastal restriction. Bran uses its actual site, rounded only to the generator's existing four-decimal placement precision. Every new placement is inside the same country/camera bounds. Existing country-index/land filtering is unchanged. Earlier uncovered-city assertions now use Sofia; the generic projection/clamp test uses Kyiv, beyond covered capitals' 2.5° pull-back reach.

Verification for Romania/Bucharest (18 November 2026):

- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_romania.py` regenerates seven isolated 4× landmark studies, the house study and Bran. Every final study and scene was viewed; palace/church/dome/arch studies were compared beside the requested benchmarks. CEC's recessed divided entrance and capital reliefs, church drum niches, Stavropoleos's painted frieze/clay cupola and house dormer spacing were refined during review.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_capitals.py` generated the 720×520 scene and both capital registries. Final Bucharest-only art revisions were published through the same `bucharest()` renderer, and the published PNG's decoded bytes match the final checker render. The full generator reproduced five pre-existing source/PNG differences (Rome, Copenhagen, Prague, Warsaw, Vilnius); those unrelated PNGs were restored from their pre-task bytes.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py bucharest` prints **OK**: seven landmarks; east/x and south/y rank correlations **1.00**; no reversed pairs, no overlaps, **hidden=0, clipped=0** throughout. Visual geography review confirms both raised southern sites on the right bank, CEC/Stavropoleos north/left, the two hills, northwest arch and north-up district order. The checker does not establish surveyed facade bearings or photographic sculptural identity.
- `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/build_map_icons.py` passes, producing **1,471** world placements. Romania adds **27** placements and one appended Bran frame. All earlier names, frame indices/rectangles, atlas RGBA pixels and placements are preserved. All **33** earlier capital PNGs and other world PNGs remain byte-for-byte unchanged.
- `npm test`: **93/93 passed**, including Romania city groups, 75 km spacing, bounds/Bran/decoration checks, focal Bucharest arrival and the existing all-capitals bounded-panning/release checks. Sofia replaces covered Bucharest in uncovered-arrival assertions; Kyiv keeps the generic projection/clamp fixture beyond capital pull-back reach. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass.
- `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-export --max-workers 2` passes. Both platform metadata asset lists contain the exact final Bucharest PNG and final atlas, verified by SHA-256 against the bundled bytes.
- `CI=1 npm run web -- --port 8081`: headless Chrome checks Romania, Bran and direct Bucharest URLs; scroll-wheel arrival equals direct arrival at **1000×780** and **390×844**. Repeated pointer drags stop at all four scene edges at both sizes; two additional drags confirm each stop. Adjacent Skopje and Belgrade retain their scenes. Page/console error lists are empty. Served Bucharest/atlas bytes match final files. The dev server is left running on 8081.
- Final Bucharest PNG: **155,642 bytes** (about 152 KiB compressed; 1.43 MiB decoded RGBA), SHA-256 `f7b9450d573950bcd24fa173051b46281a3625d127a7260fdfb0de1d4bb72c4a`. Atlas: **79,616 bytes**, SHA-256 `79369994f3d5409dac500010bb2f3a9a9c6b12ce31df2957e6d403cbb5092bd2`. No dependencies were added.
- Physical devices, native frame rate/memory, Safari and signed builds were not tested. Fine portraits, relief identities, exact window totals and inscriptions remain simplified as itemised in the model audit. Existing user edits in documentation and the agent guide were preserved. No commit was made.

**Turkey / Istanbul (18 November 2026).** The requested city is **Istanbul**, rather than Turkey's present capital Ankara. The existing capital-detail renderer hosts Istanbul without relabelling it a national capital. Country-tier `TUR` keeps the exact Natural Earth capital name `Ankara`; Istanbul is the next city (a church/two-house group, as prescribed for towns). Scene id `istanbul`, centre `28.9784,41.0082`, country/camera bounds `[25.6,35.8,44.9,42.2]`. No continent artwork or zoom thresholds change.

Research and geography before drawing: the Golden Horn opens southeast into the Bosphorus, separating Galata/Beyoğlu to its north from the historic peninsula to its south. The Bosphorus runs north–south to the east, widening into the Marmara south of Sultanahmet. Süleymaniye occupies the Third Hill; Galata Tower stands uphill north of the Horn. Hagia Sophia and Sultanahmet occupy the elevated First Hill; Topkapı's Gate of Salutation is northeast of Hagia Sophia, inland within the palace enclosure. Gülhane gardens lie northwest of the gate. Dolmabahçe is directly on the European waterfront, not an inland palace; Maiden's Tower occupies its own small islet in the strait, off the Asian shore. Water/coasts use compressed isometric-axis segments; north remains up and west left. Bridges are omitted, rather than invented to fill water.

Istanbul fact sheets. Values are **longitude, latitude**, checked 18 November 2026. The first source's coordinates are used in `SITES`; OSM entries are centres of actual building outlines, excluding adjacent squares. Front bearings are map/plan inferences, not surveyed angles.

| Landmark | Coordinate source 1 / SITES | Independent coordinate source 2 | Setting, silhouette, signature, orientation and intended model |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Hagia Sophia | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hagia_Sophia): `28.98000,41.00833` | [OSM way 109862851](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/109862851): `28.97999755,41.00851125` (20 m) | First Hill south of Horn, inland. Broad salmon/ochre Byzantine mass, shallow lead central dome, two axial semidomes, great windowed tympana, massive buttresses. **Four** minarets: red brick SE, slim pale NE, heavier pale western pair. West narthex/entrance explicitly snapped SW onto lit +j; east apse NE behind. The red SE minaret is +i,+j, NE is +i,-j; NW -i,-j, SW -i,+j. |
| Blue Mosque | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Mosque,_Istanbul): `28.9768247,41.0053851` | [OSM relation 18055570](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/18055570): `28.97664955,41.00540660` (15 m) | First Hill, SW of Hagia Sophia, south of Horn. Pale stone, grey lead central dome with **four semidomes**, cascading smaller vaults/corner cupolas, NW arcaded court. **Six** fluted minarets: four taller at prayer hall corners, three balconies each; two lower at NW court corners, two each. Main NW front and court remain on -i (partly hidden); SE qibla is visible +i. Court fountain and open arcade modeled. |
| Süleymaniye Mosque | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%BCleymaniye_Mosque): `28.96389,41.01611` | [OSM relation 1564032](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/1564032): `28.96382450,41.01624030` (16 m) | Elevated Third Hill, **south bank** of Horn. Warm stone, lead central dome, **two** axial semidomes, five small side domes per side, NW courtyard. **Four** minarets at court corners: taller SE pair beside hall carry three balconies; shorter NW pair two (ten total). NW ceremonial front remains behind; shaded +i qibla end visible. Domed arcades and rectangular marble fountain included. |

One broad cylindrical coursed-stone tower, framed arched windows, projecting circular observation balcony/corbels, smaller arcaded upper drum, steep grey conical roof. Entrance at southern sector is represented on visible SW arc; no invented secondary tower. |

SW approach uses +j lit front, towers at NW (-i) and SE (+i) ends. Gate is the selected subject; the entire multi-court palace is not claimed. |

Three main sections, raised central Ceremonial Hall, projecting bays, paired pilasters, ornamental pediments, balustraded low roof. Seaward front faces SE (+i) naturally; long axis +j. Landward NW is hidden. Central hall's interior dome is enclosed by its roof; no invented exterior onion dome, minaret or clock tower attached to palace. |

West/south landing visible, small door faces SW; exact door bearing unconfirmed, explicitly simplified. No land bridge or invented flag. |
