Temporary restoration equipment is omitted. Interior mosaics/Iznik tiles are not painted onto exterior walls.

Country landmark: **Paşabağ fairy chimneys**, `34.8535,38.6783`, in Cappadocia's Monks Valley near Zelve; grouped volcanic-tuff pillars with darker resistant caps and hewn cell openings. [Ministry: Göreme National Park](https://www.ktb.gov.tr/EN-99801/nevsehir---goreme-national-park.html), [UNESCO](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/357/). The sprite depicts the named rock site, not generic mountains or imaginary towers.

**Turkey / Ankara (18 November 2026).** Add the national capital, scene id `ankara`, centre `32.8597,39.9334`, with Natural Earth `TUR` country coverage `[25.6,35.8,44.9,42.2]`. The existing Istanbul research above is preserved; this implementation concerns Ankara. Six defining sites are researched before drawing: Hacı Bayram Mosque, Ankara Castle's upper Akkale, the Museum of Anatolian Civilizations' Mahmut Paşa bedesten, Ethnography Museum, Anıtkabir's mausoleum and Kocatepe Mosque. Historic Ankara houses use timber-framed projecting plaster upper storeys and broad clay hip roofs.

Geography/layout before buildings: north stays up, west left. Hacı Bayram occupies the northern Ulus/Augustus terrace, west and north of the citadel. Akkale crowns the northeastern rocky hill; the bedesten sits below its southwestern slope. Ethnography Museum is southwest on Namazgâh Hill. Anıtkabir stands farther west/south on Rasattepe within Peace Park. Kocatepe stands southeast of Kızılay on its own elevated site. Atatürk Boulevard provides a compressed north/south paved corridor, with Gençlik Park west of Ulus and Hamamönü houses east of Namazgâh. No open central river, coast, bridges or boats are invented: the historic Bent Deresi is covered in the modern city. Terraced elevations are drawn before buildings; open forecourts and silhouette reservations protect the landmarks.

Ankara fact sheets, researched 18 November 2026. Coordinates are **longitude, latitude**. Source 1 values go into `SITES`; source 2 uses actual OSM building bounds (or the castle's upper fort), not street/square centroids. Bearings are inferred from map geometry and reference photographs, not surveyed angles.

| Landmark | Coordinate source 1 / SITES | Independent coordinate source 2 | Setting, silhouette, defining detail and front orientation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Hacı Bayram Mosque | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hac%C4%B1_Bayram_Mosque): `32.85778,39.94417` | [OSM way 155197582](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/155197582): `32.8579664,39.9443925` (29 m) | Northern Ulus terrace, immediately west of Augustus temple. Long rectangular brick hall on stone base, broad red-tile hip roof, dressed pointed upper windows, north/west additions. **One cylindrical brick minaret, two balconies, southeast of the southern domed tomb**. North entrance is hidden; western side is snapped SW to expose the window/porch treatment. The tomb stays south and the minaret southeast; no main-hall dome. |
| Ankara Castle / upper Akkale | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ankara_Castle): `32.86500,39.94167` | [OSM way 874846724](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/874846724): `32.86565185,39.9416340` (56 m) | Rocky northeastern summit above the museum. Ankara stone, reused pale masonry and brick courses, massive irregular roofless walls, angular/pentagonal projecting bastions and open walks. Selected upper-fort walls/bastions are illustrated; the entire inner citadel has 42 towers and is **not** represented by an invented compact 42-tower castle. No conical roofs. Southern approach is visible, explicitly snapped SW; broad upper battlement is the defining silhouette. |
| Museum of Anatolian Civilizations / bedesten | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Anatolian_Civilizations): `32.86194,39.93833` | [OSM way 363095473](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/363095473): `32.8617633,39.9379522` (45 m) | Atpazarı, southwest below the castle, inland. Long low Ottoman stone/brick covered market, **ten lead domes in two rows of five**, lower tile-roof perimeter shops, striped stone entrance arch and recessed timber door. Short southern entrance end snaps SW; the two dome rows extend NE. Kurşunlu Han's separate courtyard is outside this bedesten-only model. |
| Ethnography Museum | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnography_Museum_of_Ankara): `32.854894,39.932668` (coordinate indexed with the article) | [OSM way 217483084](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/217483084): `32.8552989,39.93267195` (35 m) | Namazgâh Hill southwest of the old town. Broad symmetrical pale-stone national-style wings, a single lead dome with bronze ornamental band, **four marble columns supporting three pointed entrance arches**, palmette cornice and stairs. Famous west front snaps SW; subsidiary roofs stay low, no minarets. Forecourt equestrian Atatürk is outside the building footprint and omitted rather than drawn as a stick figure. |
| Anıtkabir / mausoleum | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An%C4%B1tkabir): `32.83778,39.92556` | [OSM way 28241512](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/28241512): `32.83801345,39.92563525` (22 m) | Rasattepe/Anıttepe in western Peace Park, southwest of Namazgâh. Broad, low ochre travertine colonnaded rectangle, flat corniced roof, recessed Hall of Honour, monumental stair and relief walls. **Eight square columns on each short end, fourteen on each long side** (44 posts across four face rows; corner returns compressed). Ceremonial front faces approximately SW and stays visible on +j. No invented dome/spire; surrounding ten symbolic towers are outside this mausoleum-only model. |
| Kocatepe Mosque | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kocatepe_Mosque): `32.86083,39.91667` | [OSM relation 6276462](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/6276462), outer way 422198754: `32.86069245,39.91673355` (14 m) | Raised Kocatepe southeast of Kızılay; southernmost selected landmark. Pale stone, lead roofs, **one central dome, four half-domes, twelve subsidiary domes, four corner minarets each with three balconies** and pointed caps. Main entrance/courtyard faces north and remains behind the prayer hall; south-facing qibla elevation snaps SW. The rear courtyard arcade is compressed; its 26 small roof domes are outside this prayer-hall-only model, not silently replaced by another count. |

Source descriptions are checked against exterior photographs; image-search captions are not architectural evidence.

Country landmark: **Pamukkale travertines**, `29.12333,37.92389` ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamukkale)); [OSM way 15494594](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/15494594) bounds centre `29.12236625,37.9245036` is 109 m away on the same extensive terrace field. This is a landscape extending hundreds of metres, explaining the centre difference; neither point is the town centre. [UNESCO](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/485/) documents white calcite cascades and terraced thermal basins above the plain. Model irregular stepped white rims, shaded mineral curtains and turquoise pools; no buildings, towers or flags.

**Ankara model audit, 18 November 2026.** All six isolated 4× studies, houses, Pamukkale and the final city scene were viewed. Side-by-side comparisons used the requested Lisbon castle/Sé, Madrid palace/Almudena and Paris Sacré-Cœur/house studies; the remaining requested previews and all three finished benchmark scenes were also opened. Iteration raised Kocatepe's minarets relative to its main dome, added the Ethnography Museum's corner pediments and tall openings, gave the bedesten a darker rubble-stone ramp, flattened Pamukkale's terraces to expose all five pools and brought its shadow against the base. The first placement check found minaret/bedesten and minaret/museum intersections; spacing was adjusted while retaining both geographic ranks. No placement-check exceptions were added.

| Landmark | Final model versus fact sheet | Remaining limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hacı Bayram Mosque | Matches its northern Ulus terrace and west-of-citadel position; long brick prayer hall, broad textured clay hip roof, stone plinth, dressed pointed windows and low open porch; separate pale southern domed tomb and **one SE minaret with two balconies**. North entrance is knowingly hidden; west treatment snaps SW. | The attached Augustus temple, full modern north extension and interior painted ceiling are outside this mosque/tomb model. Tomb calligraphy, carved plaster lattices and exact window totals are abbreviated. |
| Ankara Castle / upper Akkale | Matches the northeastern **36-pixel raised rocky summit**, above/northeast of the bedesten; open irregular ward, roofless massive walls, five selected projecting angular/pentagonal bastions, battlement walks, masonry repairs/spolia, brick bands, slits and internal stone stairs. Southern approach snaps SW. | This is a compressed upper-fort segment, not a survey of all 42 inner-citadel towers or every outer curtain. Exact restored/ruined merlon positions and the detailed cliff geology are schematic. No roofed central keep or conical turrets are invented. |
| Museum of Anatolian Civilizations / bedesten | Matches its lower **14-pixel southwest citadel slope**; long low market hall, **ten lead domes in two rows of five**, individual ribs/seams, brick drums, darker rubble masonry, dressed windows, lower tile-roof shops, striped south-end portal, divided timber door and steps. Southern entrance snaps SW. | Perimeter shop/window counts are compressed; the Kurşunlu Han courtyard and archaeological garden exhibits are outside the bedesten model. Tiny doorway lettering is omitted. |
| Ethnography Museum | Matches **20-pixel Namazgâh Hill**, west/south of the old centre and north/east of Anıtkabir; broad symmetrical wings, low roofs, central lead dome with bronze band, **four supports/three open pointed arches**, carved corner aedicules/pediments, palmettes, tall framed openings and broad stairs. West front snaps SW. | Bronze vegetal reliefs and inscriptions are sampled, and the front's exact tall-window arrangement is compressed. The equestrian statue is outside the building-only study; no antenna-like stand-in is drawn. |
| Anıtkabir / mausoleum | Matches western **22-pixel Rasattepe**, southwest of Namazgâh, inside Peace Park; long low travertine hall, flat roof and cornices, recessed bronze openings, **eight posts on each short end and fourteen along each long side**, broad stairs, lectern and bounded relief panels. SW ceremonial front stays visible. | The model has 44 posts in four face rows, with paired end returns; exact corner junctions are compressed. The surrounding ten symbolic towers, Road of Lions and full ceremonial plaza are outside this mausoleum-only footprint. Relief identities, inscription text and the 42 real stair risers are compressed. The plain flat roof is an architectural surface, not an invented pitched/domed roof. |
| Kocatepe Mosque | Matches the southeastern/southernmost **12-pixel raised Kocatepe site**; square pale prayer hall, dressed pointed windows, **one central dome, four outward half-domes, twelve small cupolas and four corner minarets with three balconies each**, faceted caps, corbelled balcony rings, rail openings and crescent finials. North entrance stays behind the hall; qibla/south side snaps SW. | The 26-domed north courtyard arcade is outside the prayer-hall footprint. Several subordinate cupolas naturally recede behind the main shell; this is self-occlusion, not external covering. Tiny muqarnas, calligraphy and exact window/lead-panel totals are sampled. |

All six models meet the benchmark's architectural-detail standard after the above refinements; the explicitly abbreviated sculpture, lettering, masonry surveys and fine muqarnas still fall short of literal photographic reproduction. Every selected landmark retains its defining silhouette, material ramps and real relative position. House variants have divided framed windows/sills, shops or timber doors, exposed timber bands, projecting enclosed cumba on brackets, plinths/cornices, clay tile seams/ridges and capped chimneys. Gençlik Park stays west of Ulus, Peace Park surrounds the western mausoleum, and the citadel's upper/lower terrain is visible. The checker cannot verify hill identity or hidden front bearings; those were inspected against the fact sheets separately.

Pamukkale's five irregular white mineral terraces have distinct shallow turquoise pools, scalloped lips, shaded calcite curtains and a ground shadow. The native 68×68 alpha bounds `(6,16,61,57)` leave transparent margins on every edge. The sprite abbreviates a broad natural terrace field rather than claiming an exact basin count or profile. No settlement or thermal-pool boats are invented.

The pinned Natural Earth capital is `Ankara` at `32.8624,39.9292`; the illustrative scene's city centre is separately `32.8597,39.9334`. All cities meet the existing 75 km rule, and every placement is inside the shared `[25.6,35.8,44.9,42.2]` country/camera bounds. Country-index/land/coast filters are unchanged. World placement count is **1,499**; no earlier placements are removed.

**Andorra / Andorra la Vella (18 November 2026).** Research precedes modelling. Scene id `andorra`, centre `1.5218,42.5078`, shared country/camera bounds `[1.4,42.42,1.79,42.66]`, Natural Earth `AND`, exact populated-place capital name **Andorra** (`1.516486,42.500001`). This is a compressed illustration of the capital valley, explicitly including adjoining Escaldes-Engordany and Santa Coloma, not six sites within the old town alone.

Geography planned before buildings: north up, west left. Casa de la Vall occupies the raised Roc de la Vall southwest of Sant Esteve, both on the Gran Valira's right/northwestern bank. Santa Coloma is much farther southwest, also on the right bank, below the western Enclar slopes. The Valira del Nord enters from the north and joins the Valira d'Orient west/northwest of Caldea; their combined Gran Valira passes Pont de Paris and flows southwest. Caldea lies east of the northern branch and north of the eastern branch; Sant Pere Màrtir lies farther east and south of Caldea on the southern side of the Valira d'Orient. Raised wooded valley sides, open quays and distinct old-town cobbles/modern slabs precede buildings. No seacoast or boating is invented. [Municipal valley map](https://www.turismeandorralavella.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/Mapa_ALV-EE_des2021.pdf) and OSM river geometry were inspected.

Fact sheets researched 18 November 2026. Values are longitude, latitude. Independent OSM building coordinates below are bounding-box centres from the actual footprint, fetched from the OSM map API; not entrances or nearby squares. Front bearings are map/photo inferences, not surveyed measurements.

| Landmark | Coordinate source 1 / SITES | Coordinate source 2 | Setting, silhouette, defining feature and front |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Casa de la Vall | [Wikipedia](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_la_Vall): `1.520556,42.506667` | [OSM way 512803160](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/512803160): `1.52050825,42.50660805` (8 m) | Elevated old-town rock, right bank. Broad rectangular rubble-stone manor, slate ridge roof, granite round doorway, two projecting round corner watch turrets and a rear square defensive/dovecote tower. South valley front snaps SW; northern rear tower remains behind the roof. Signature: fortified farmhouse with paired corbelled turrets, not a generic castle. [House architecture](https://www.casadelavall.ad/en/arquitectura), [museum exterior guide](https://museus.ad/media/files/museus/casa-de-la-vall/casa-de-la-vall-discurs-ang.pdf). |

Grey granite, pale newer apses, intersecting slate roofs and one square northern bell tower with paired arched belfry openings and low pyramidal cap. Defining surviving Romanesque apse has Lombard blind arcades and sawtooth frieze. West portal snaps SW; north tower stays behind the nave, contemporary south apses remain visible. One dominant 80 m mirrored-glass tapered tower, crystalline triangular roofs over broad low spa halls, steel glazing grid, terraces and outdoor thermal water. Signature: spiked glass mountain, never a masonry church spire. Broad southwest-facing glazed slopes visible; tower stands at the northeastern/rear part of the hall. Coordinates refer to different parts of this large complex, all within 100 m. [Operator's architecture and aerial photo](https://www.caldea.com/en/history). |
| Sant Pere Màrtir | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esgl%C3%A9sia_de_Sant_Pere_M%C3%A0rtir,_Escaldes-Engordany): `1.54165,42.50888` | [OSM way 202606880](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/202606880): `1.54160795,42.5089104` (5 m); [museum map pin](https://maps.google.com/?ll=42.508843,1.541835) (16 m) | Escaldes, south of Valira d'Orient, east/south of Caldea. Western main front snaps SW with tower at its -i/northwest end. [Government museum photographs](https://museus.ad/ca/monuments/sant-pere-martir) checked against footprint. |

Low rubble-stone rectangular nave, square eastern apse, slate gable, southern lean-to porch and one 18 m cylindrical Lombard tower beside the south wall near the eastern apse. Four belfry tiers with paired openings, shallow conical slate cap and reddish upper detailing. Southern entry snaps SW; tower at +i/eastern end of that side. Signature is the round tower, not a square campanile. |

Country landmark: **new Meritxell sanctuary**, `1.59088,42.55463` ([OSM way 202635437 / Mapcarta](https://mapcarta.com/W202635437)); [Wikipedia](https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouveau_sanctuaire_de_Meritxell) `1.590833,42.554722` agrees within 11 m. Bofill's modern sanctuary has a low stone complex, white-edged monumental arches, open arcaded courtyard and a separate square bell tower. [Official tourism description](https://visitandorra.com/en/culture/sanctuary-of-meritxell/). The country sprite will select the open court, layered arches and bell tower, not the neighbouring old chapel. Pine candidates use the existing native country-index/land filtering at 0.01° and 5 km spacing; the universal 75 km city rule stays intact.

**Andorra model audit, 18 November 2026.** All six isolated 4× studies, houses and the final scene were opened. All eleven requested Lisbon/Madrid/Paris studies and their three finished scenes were inspected; side-by-side comparisons paired Casa with Lisbon's castle/Sé, Sant Esteve with Sé, Sant Pere with Almudena, Caldea with the Louvre glazing, Santa Coloma with Sacré-Cœur/houses and Pont de Paris with Madrid's detailed garden/house study. Iterations corrected unbounded window openings, replaced an incorrectly assumed rose on Sant Pere with its actual arched front galleries, restored Casa's long gable ridge, exposed the apse roof caps, separated intersecting landmark silhouettes, moved Sant Esteve south of Sant Pere to retain real latitude order, and padded Meritxell's native frame to leave transparent margins. No checker exceptions or camera behavior changes were added.

Southern front snaps SW; the rear tower remains north/behind the roof. | Window totals and stone repairs are compressed. The photographed northern frontage/entry is not reproduced on a visible southern wall. Interior murals are omitted; apse profiles remain simplified projected curved volumes. |

Road lettering, exact tubular joint welds, traffic and neighbouring fountains are omitted. The model is not a stone bridge or a generic single-mast cable bridge. |
| Caldea | East of northern tributary/north of eastern tributary, northeast of bridge, in adjoining Escaldes; **one dominant mirrored tapered glass tower**, broad low spa halls under varied crystalline roof peaks, contrasting glass faces, individual mullions/glints, entry wing, stone plinth, terrace guards and outdoor thermal basin. Southwest glazed slopes visible; tower stays at the rear/northeast. | Crystal facets use a simplified tessellation, not an exact roof survey. West frontage snaps SW; tower stays at the -i/NW end. | Sgraffito figures are incised tonal fields, not identifiable reproductions of Sergi Mas's religious scenes. Exact stone carving/window totals and side-porch artwork are abbreviated. Front is exposed deliberately, not left accidentally on a hidden side. |

Four separately bounded belfry tiers, four paired-opening sectors, a single-opening exception in the lowest tier, reddish upper band, shallow conical slate cap. South porch snaps SW; eastern end +i/SE. | Tiny sculpted heads/crosses and exterior polychrome fragments are omitted; lowest-tier single opening is an illustrative selected sector, whose exact compass bearing was not independently confirmed. The round tower's mild real deformation is idealised. |

The six landmarks retain the requested material/structure/detail standard against the benchmark studies. Fine fresco identities, heraldic lettering, exact carved friezes and full-complex surveys still fall short of literal reproduction as listed above. Houses have one/two/three-storey stone/plaster variations, dressed divided windows and sills, doors/glazed shops, timber galleries on brackets or occasional ironwork, plinths/eaves/cornices, slate seams/ridges and capped chimneys. Valley sides and the old-town rock are raised before buildings; all water stays visible through open quays. The checker cannot identify the tributaries or terrace identity, so those were compared visually with the municipal map/OSM geometry separately.

**Sofia / Bulgaria — researched layout and landmark fact sheets (18 November 2026).**

The 720×520 scene illustrates central Sofia in its inland basin. No river, coast, harbour or invented bridge. Serdica's western centre is lower; St Sophia and Alexander Nevsky occupy the gentle eastern rise (8 art pixels). St George stands below surrounding street level in its archaeological courtyard. Banski Square is between the mosque and baths; the City Garden is west of the theatre. Yellow ceramic street paving follows the central Tsar Osvoboditel corridor. Distances are compressed, north up/east right. Vitosha is outside this central crop, not a fabricated downtown hill.

Coordinates below use the bounding-box centres of OSM building ways fetched through the public OSM API; the second coordinates are Wikipedia's independently published points. Mapcarta independently exposes the same OSM feature IDs. Coordinates are longitude, latitude. Do not use inaccurate OSM `height=9` for the cathedral: the cathedral's own architectural page gives the actual monumental heights.

| Landmark; selected lon,lat | Two coordinate sources; agreement | Setting, silhouette, defining details and front |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Banya Bashi Mosque; **23.3225614,42.6995055** | [OSM way 96640830](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/96640830); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banya_Bashi_Mosque) 23.32250,42.69944, 9 m. | NW centre, west of baths, inland. Square alternating brick/stone hall, one broad lead dome and **one** slender minaret at western corner, one balcony and pointed cap. NW porch has **three small domes** in addition to main dome ([archaeological study](https://intarch.ac.uk/journal/issue66/21/full-text.html)). Keep NW porch hidden rather than accidentally showing an SW entrance; show south-facing arched windows. Minaret remains west. |
| Central Mineral Baths; **23.3238787,42.6992879** | [OSM way 166213386](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/166213386); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia_Central_Mineral_Baths) 23.32389,42.69917, 13 m. | Immediately east/SE of mosque, Banski Square and garden in front. Broad low multi-wing Secession building, cream/ochre and red striped ceramic walls, arched windows, central entrance pavilion, curved copper/green roof caps and clay roof wings. Defining feature: polychrome ceramic geometric/floral bands. West famous front snapped SW. [Museum architecture/history](https://sofiahistorymuseum.bg/en/13-%D0%B7%D0%B0-%D0%BD%D0%B0%D1%81/145-history-of-the-municipal-central-mineral-baths-building) verifies multi-wing roof/ceramic ornament and entrance portraits Apollo/Tyche. |
| St George Rotunda; **23.3228560,42.6969088** | [OSM way 171437780](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/171437780); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Saint_George,_Sofia) 23.32278,42.69694, 7 m. | South of mosque, west of baths and theatre. Sunken archaeological court inside Presidency/hotel enclosure. Red brick cylindrical upper body on cruciform/square lower body; one low clay-tiled conical roof over internal dome, no belfry/minaret. Arched windows, brick arches and excavated low walls. West door snapped SW. Interior frescoes are not exterior decoration. |
| St Sophia Basilica; **23.3314339,42.6965492** | [OSM way 96646571](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/96646571); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Sophia_Church,_Sofia) 23.3314250,42.6965083, 5 m. | On gentle eastern rise, NW of cathedral, NE of Russian church. Long low red-brick cruciform basilica, raised central nave/crossing, lower aisles, eastern apse, clay gables. Modern exterior has **no freestanding bell towers or onion domes**; Wikipedia's historical tower/cupola statement is not copied into the modern silhouette. Defining feature: austere stepped brick cross-plan and round-headed openings. Three-door west front snapped SW; apse at hidden NE. [Archaeologist Magdalina Stancheva’s illustrated architectural account](https://magdalinastancheva.net/sofia/1_Sofia.pdf) verifies the three-bay narthex, raised central nave and internal crossing dome. |
| Alexander Nevsky Cathedral; **23.3327954,42.6958183** | [OSM way 155447771](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/155447771); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Alexander_Nevsky_Cathedral,_Sofia) 23.332956,42.695833, 13 m. | East of all other selected sites, on open square/eastern rise, SE of basilica, slightly north of Russian church. Broad low ivory neo-Byzantine mass with stepped green copper half-domes, one large central gold dome, one taller gold-capped western bell tower, two small green entrance domes at north/south. Triple western doors, arched window groups, decorated cornices, western icon mosaic. Defining feature: cascading green roofs and **two gold crowns**. West front snapped SW with bell tower on that end. [Cathedral architecture](https://www.cathedral.bg/en/architecture) and [construction history](https://cathedral.bg/en/history) verify western tower, triple doorway and northern/southern entrance domes. |
| Russian Church of St Nicholas; **23.3289372,42.6956956** | [OSM way 162791009](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/162791009); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Church,_Sofia) 23.3291,42.6957, 13 m. | South of basilica and SW of cathedral, garden beside Tsar Osvoboditel. White Russian Revival cross-plan hall, green glazed tent roofs, **five gold onion domes** (tall central + four smaller), ornamental ceramic borders and projecting arched porch. Southern boulevard porch snapped SW; all four corner dome positions preserved. Defining feature: five gilded bulbs above green roofs. |
| Ivan Vazov National Theatre; **23.3265503,42.6941792** | [OSM way 1026031878](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1026031878); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Vazov_National_Theatre) 23.32639,42.69417, 13 m. | Southernmost selected site; east edge of City Garden, east of rotunda, west of Russian church. Red neoclassical body, **six** white front columns, triangular Apollo/Muses relief pediment, **two** symmetrical square roof towers carrying bronze chariot groups. West famous front snapped SW; north tower at NW (-i), south at SE (+i). Defining feature: six-column white portico against red walls and paired sculptural roof groups. [Theatre's own architectural description](https://www.nationaltheatre.bg/en/news/narodniyat-teatr-dava-nachaloto-na-kampaniya-za-osvetyavane-na-unikalnata-si-sgrada) confirms six columns and Apollo/Muses. |

Country landmark: **Rila Monastery**, complex centre **23.3401159,42.1333100**, [OSM way 432365600](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/432365600), [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rila_Monastery) 23.34028,42.13333, 14 m. In the Rilska valley, south of Sofia; enclosed multi-storey white cloister with dark timber galleries/striped arcades, clay roofs, central five-domed Nativity church, separate square medieval Hrelyo tower. Country sprite compresses selected cloister wings and church/tower; not every room, bay or fresco. No fictitious extra tower or offshore setting.


Sofia model review (seven isolated 4× studies plus the six-house study regenerated and viewed, then compared side by side at the same integer enlargement with the named Lisbon/Madrid/Paris studies and finished scenes):

| Landmark | Model match, signatures drawn, orientation and limits |
| --- | --- |
| Banya Bashi Mosque | One broad ribbed lead dome, stone/brick courses, round-headed dressed openings, cornices, western octagonal brick minaret with one corbelled balcony/iron guard and lead cone. Three smaller porch domes are built on the hidden NW side. The western minaret stays on the screen-left corner; no SW entrance is invented. Main dome/minaret counts and the inland NW setting match. Individual calligraphic inscriptions are omitted. |
| Central Mineral Baths | Long low ochre/cream wings, red ceramic courses, green geometric/floral eave bands, tall dressed arched openings, projecting central/end pavilions, rounded ribbed green roof caps, clay wing roofs, arched entrance crown, portrait medallions and steps. The west front snaps SW, with Banski garden open in front and mosque to its NW. Real pavilion roof forms and minor bays are compressed; the tiny portrait tesserae do not resolve Apollo/Tyche's identities. The curved pavilion caps replaced initial generic hip caps after studying the municipal tourism brochure. |
| St George Rotunda | Cruciform lower brick body, cylindrical upper drum, one low clay-tiled conical roof over its internal dome, dressed arched windows, western vestibule/door, courses, eaves and low excavated Serdica walls. It remains south of mosque in a six-pixel depression; west entrance snaps SW. No bell tower or invented exterior frescoes. Enclosing Presidency/hotel wings are abbreviated into surrounding city fabric; individual ancient foundations are illustrative. |
| St Sophia Basilica | Lower aisles and raised nave/crossing form an elongated brick cross-plan, with three western portals, clay gables/ridges, round-headed openings, cornices, eastern apse and stairs. Stays NW of Alexander Nevsky on the gentle eastern rise. West front snaps SW; eastern apse is on the hidden NE side. Internal crossing dome is under the roof, with no fabricated external onion dome or modern belfry. The hidden apse is rounded/compressed rather than a surveyed three-wall plan; exact reconstructed roof junctions and all archaeological phases are not modelled. |
| Alexander Nevsky Cathedral | Central gilded dome, taller west bell tower/gilded cap, visible open bell chamber with solid bells, three west doors, western mosaic medallion, stepped green roof/apse volumes, north/south entrance caps, dressed arched openings, stone courses, cornices, ribs, crosses and steps. West front explicitly snaps SW, retaining its west tower; cathedral remains east of every other selected site, south of basilica and slightly north of Russian church on the eastern rise. The great domes dominate neighbouring houses. Fine lace-like stone carving, mosaic identities and exact half-dome junctions fall short of the larger Paris studies; small entrance-cap finish is not independently resolved by the cited construction history. |
| Russian Church | Five gold onion bulbs (central plus four corners), tall tapered green glazed tent, white drums, ceramic/scalloped eave motifs, dressed arched windows, gilded cornices, projecting porch/icon medallion and stairs. Real boulevard-facing southern porch snaps SW; rear corner domes can be naturally hidden by the higher central tent. Remains south of basilica and SW of cathedral beside its garden. Exact icon identities, kokoshnik curvature and tile motifs remain pixel abbreviations. |
| Ivan Vazov Theatre | Red multi-storey body, white cornices/dressings, six actual portico columns/capitals, triangular pediment with solid miniature Apollo/Muses relief volumes, twin equal-height square towers with dark bronze horse/chariot/figure groups, slate-textured roofs, entry doors and stairs. West front snaps SW; north tower is at the NW (-i) end, south tower at the SE (+i) end. City Garden stays west/NW; theatre is the southernmost selected landmark. Chariot/Apollo anatomy and individual relief identities remain less resolved than the large Madrid studies. Towers were changed from gold to dark bronze after photograph comparison. |

Houses have six Sofia plaster colours, one/two/three-storey variants, framed divided glazing and sills, ground doors or glazed shopfronts, ceramic eave friezes, plinths/cornices, iron balconies or projecting oriel bays, textured clay hip roofs/ridges and capped masonry chimneys. Squares, cobbles, yellow ceramic paving, Banski/City/Russian church gardens and the eastern rise were inspected in the finished scene. No river, coast, bridges, flags or pleasure boats are invented in central Sofia. A continuous ground substrate prevents holes along stepped tiles and the excavation.

Rila's 72×66 country sprite has an open court, selected white cloister wings with dark/striped galleries, clay roofs, five domes on the central Nativity church, and a separate square Hrelyo tower to its NE. Native nontransparent bounds `(4,5,68,61)` leave padding on all four sides. The sprite compresses a large monastery rather than claiming every cloister bay, dome fitting or fresco. Bulgaria's unchanged 75 km population spacing retains **Sofia, Plovdiv, Varna, Burgas, Ruse, Stara Zagora, Pleven, Shumen and Montana**; nearby Pernik is excluded. Seven decorations are two pines/five oaks. One Black Sea boat fits the unchanged 150 km spacing. These 27 additions stay in the shared country/camera bounds and retain the existing land/country-index filtering.

**Belarus / Minsk (19 November 2026).**

Building coordinates use centres of OSM building bounding boxes fetched from /api/0.6/way/<id>/full.json. Wikipedia/Wikidata are a single independent source family. All pairs agree within 100 m; footprint centres resolve offsets onto adjacent paving. North is up. Distances are compressed independently along each axis.

| Landmark | OSM longitude, latitude; second source | Setting, silhouette, front, model matches and limits |
| --- | --- | --- |

Model matches the two equal tiered towers, open arched belfries/louvres, green-grey ogee caps/crosses, shaped stepped gable, framed icon panel, arched door, pilasters/cornices and green nave hip. The real gable scroll curves and icon painting are compressed. |

**Geography and integration:** the initial layout/fact sheet preceded modelling. Final ground anchors are library `(598,148)`, opera `(394,225)`, Victory `(540,297)`, Holy Spirit `(280,344)`, City Hall `(254,465)` and Red Church `(86,476)`; footprint centres are placement anchors, while the checker measures painted base extents. Upper Town cathedral/City Hall stand on ten-pixel terraces; the opera stands six pixels up on Trinity Hill. Every east/west and north/south ordering survives compression. The Svislach enters from the northwest, bends around the south/west of the opera, curls northeast, then southeast/southwest toward Gorky Park and the scene's southeast edge. Cathedral, City Hall and Red Church remain on the southwest/west side, opera and Victory on the northeast/east side; the library stays far northeast and inland. The separate Slepianka stream near the library lies outside the represented river corridor. The course was checked against [OSM Svislach way 1120540737](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1120540737). [Independence Avenue bridge 25379436](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/25379436) and [25379437](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/25379437), approximately `27.5710,53.9066`, confirm the low modern crossing southwest of Victory: a flat paved deck with open rails crosses the +i river along +j. No medieval bridge arches are invented. Open pale quays, rose avenue paving, distinct square slabs/cobbles, green gardens and small upstream recreational rowboats complete the illustration. Tall houses/trees are excluded using reservations and measured landmark silhouettes.

**Belarus country tier:** Natural Earth `BLR`, matching camera/country bounds `[23.1,51.2,32.8,56.2]`, exact capital name `Minsk`; capital scene centre `27.5667,53.9`. Unchanged population ordering and 75 km spacing retain Minsk, Homyel, Mahilyow, Vitsyebsk, Hrodna, Brest, Babruysk, Baranavichy and Pinsk. Minsk has its castle/two houses, Homyel/Mahilyow churches between houses, later cities alternate church/house pair. Barysaw and Orsha are excluded by spacing. Seven country-index-filtered trees (one northern pine, six oaks), zero offshore boats and Mir Castle add 26 country-tier sprites. Existing continent decoration stays unchanged.

**Mir Castle:** country anchor `26.4730000,53.4512389` from [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mir_Castle_Complex); [OSM building relation 1579104](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/1579104) centre `26.4729222,53.4511932` differs by about 7 m. [UNESCO](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/625/) independently places the complex at `26.4727222,53.4510833` (25 m). The square brick complex stands inland southwest of Minsk, with the palace on north/east courtyard sides, **four corner towers and a western gate tower**, square lower/octagonal upper stages, clay pyramidal caps, pale decorative niches and palace dressings. The native 72×68 sprite models five towers, wall courses, restored pale wings, hip roofs, framed openings and a dark arched gate; the west gate is locally presented SW for visibility. Court depth/individual niche counts are compressed. All four transparent margins remain; its nontransparent bounds are `(5,4,67,66)`. No source photograph pixels enter the art.

**House palette and visual review:** Minsk uses warm grey/cream, muted sage, blue-grey, apricot and lilac stucco with pilasters, rusticated doors/shops, divided framed windows/sills, occasional iron balconies, stone plinths/cornices, green-grey standing-metal or red-clay textured hips/ridges, pediment variants and capped chimneys. Six isolated 4× landmark studies, houses and Mir are regenerated with `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_belarus.py`. All studies and the final scene are inspected, alongside the eleven named Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmark studies and all three completed scenes. Iteration strengthens cathedral belfry openings/pilasters, varies brick/window framing, reduces library grid noise, adds opera parapets/roof seams and gives the monument bounded sculptural relief/wreaths. City Hall is moved south and Red Church farther west to remove silhouette overlap without reversing geographic order. Overall silhouettes/material depth meet the scene-scale benchmark; the explicitly abbreviated icon, relief, coat-of-arms, sculpture and fine tracery details above remain less resolved than large reference photographs.

**Minsk verification:** `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/landmarks_belarus.py`, the full capital builder, the map-icon builder, Python compilation, `.venv/bin/python scripts/art/check_capital.py minsk`, `npm test` (**103/103**), `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx expo lint`, and `CI=1 npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir /tmp/pw-minsk-export-final --max-workers 2` pass. The checker finds exactly six landmarks, **east/x 1.00, south/y 1.00, hidden=0, clipped=0, no overlaps**. Final scene regeneration is pixel-identical. Both native export manifests contain the final Minsk scene and atlas, verified by SHA-256 after the last belfry refinement.

**Browser and scope:** `npm run web -- --port 8081`, Chrome at **1000×780** and **390×844**: Belarus, Mir Castle, Minsk and neighbouring Vilnius URLs load; wheel arrival matches direct arrival pixel-for-pixel; all eight pan-edge stops pass. The final asset smoke check confirms served scene/atlas SHA-256 and no console/runtime errors. All 38 earlier capital PNGs, earlier atlas frame rectangles/pixels and earlier icon placements are preserved. Full regeneration exposed pre-existing byte differences in Rome, Warsaw, Vilnius, Prague and Copenhagen; their original PNG bytes were restored without editing those generators. The final Minsk background uses denser blocks and clearer cobble paving, and Mir is reduced from its first 84-pixel draft to native 72×68. Existing user documentation remains intact. No dependencies, Expo/React Native APIs, native project directories, continent board, rendering or navigation code are changed; the camera receives one coverage row. No commit created.

**Limits:** the cathedral icon/gable scrolls, City Hall crest, church rose tracery, opera sculpture anatomy/lyre and monument relief/heraldic identities remain compressed or omitted as stated per model. The library's full stylobate/pane count, exact terrain heights and bridge engineering details are illustrative. Physical iOS/Android devices, native runtime performance, Safari and EAS builds are not verified. Export checks validate the bundles/assets, not device rendering. Six landmark silhouettes/counts, settings, explicit front turns and material depth were visually checked against the fact sheets and benchmarks.

## Phase 4b — Living world

Started 20 November 2026, before the gameplay slice: animation of the map itself, from space inward. One 12-frames-a-second clock drives everything, and the system reduce-motion setting keeps the scene still. Rules: [ART_CONTRACT §9](docs/art/ART_CONTRACT.md#9-motion-the-living-world-started-20-november-2026).

- [x] **L-01 — Space and planet.** Idle spin after 3 seconds without input, drifting clouds and wave highlights, twinkling and flashing stars, a shooting star every 11 seconds, a floating satellite with a panel glint and blinking beacon. Verified in Chrome (390×844): the spin starts after 3 seconds, stops on a drag and resumes; reduce motion shows zero changed pixels over 9 seconds. Not yet measured on a physical phone: an idle screen now redraws the full-screen shader 12 times a second.
- [x] **L-02 — Continent board.** Animals blink and shift their weight (2 extra frames each, own rhythm; 28 of 29 species, the orca has no eye), trees sway as gusts sweep east, windmill sails turn, volcano ash puffs, boats bob, sea glints twinkle and surf rolls in to the coasts, and 48 flocks of gulls with shadows fly great circles round the planet. Boats bob in place rather than drifting, so they can never sail onto land. The atlas is now packed into rows at most 2048 pixels wide (it was one 3922-pixel row, wider than some phone GPUs accept). Verified in Chrome: each effect seen moving, and reduce motion shows zero changed pixels on the board and at country zoom.
- [ ] **L-03 — Country tier.** Done: capital pennants flutter and chimneys smoke (Central, Atlantic and Nordic houses; translucent, so placement is unchanged). Left: the selected pin bouncing, once places can be selected (Phase 5).
- [ ] **L-04 — Capital scenes.** Done: water glints (water found by colour in each finished scene by `scripts/art/capital_motion.py`, 39 of 44 scenes have water), gulls and drifting cloud shadows, only for the capital being visited. Left: chimney smoke, flags and a few two-frame people at the anchors exported by P5-01.
- [ ] **L-05 — Measure on devices.** Frame time and battery on a mid-range Android phone and an iPhone, idle and while zooming; lower the idle rate or pause off-screen effects if needed.

## Phase 5 — Gameplay slice on three capitals

**Dependencies:** the Lisbon, Paris, and Athens scenes (built). New capitals stay paused until P5-08 passes.

**Goal:** prove the core loop end to end before adding more content: country sheet → capital scene → "find it" → collectible card → saved progress.

- [ ] **P5-01 — Export landmark positions from the scene builder.** `scripts/art/build_capitals.py` also writes a data file per capital: each landmark's stable id, name, and hit shape in scene pixels, plus animation anchors (water regions, chimney tops, flagpoles, boat lanes) for P6-05. `check_capital.py` already measures each landmark's visibility and clipping; reuse that geometry. Scene PNG bytes stay unchanged. Tests check that every landmark has a non-empty, unclipped shape and list any too small to tap comfortably at phone scale.
  - *Landmark export (25 November 2026).* The exporter shares the scene checker's geometry and writes stable IDs, names, boxes and masks for 325 landmarks across 44 capitals. Lisbon, Paris and Athens passed first, and all 44 scene PNGs retained their SHA-256 checksums. At full zoom on a 390-point-wide viewport, the test flagged Madrid's Cibeles Fountain (62×44), Paris's Luxor Obelisk (14×64), and Reykjavík's Leif Erikson Statue (32×58) for having a box side under 48 points. Physical-phone tap comfort and P5-05 integration remain unverified. Animation anchors are pending.
- [x] **P5-02 — Add the content catalog.** Stable IDs and parent links for countries, capitals, and collectibles (landmarks, wildlife, landscapes). Each collectible has one or two checked sentences and its sources, drawn from the existing fact sheets and `scripts/geography/scenery.json`. Record which countries have content, so one without content is never shown as completed. Document continent/territory policy.
  - Added 1 continent, 44 countries, 44 capitals, 325 landmarks, 4 wildlife species and 0 landscapes (schema only). Opened the cited pages and checked all 17 slice facts (Lisbon 4, Paris 6, Athens 7). Unchecked facts: 0. Tests verify the input inventory, parent links, citation format and completion eligibility (Portugal, France and Greece). Placement coordinates and wildlife URLs were copied from the repo and were not independently reverified. No browser or device checks for this data-only task. [Continent and territory proposals](docs/content/CATALOG.md#continent-and-territory-policy) await owner decisions.
- [x] **P5-03 — Add minimal contextual navigation.** Title/back/focus controls and a country sheet over the map, with accessible alternatives to gestures. Sheets stay collapsible; the mounted scene and camera are preserved.
  - The sheet selects covered countries from the board raster and counts checked catalog landmarks, with zero found until P5-07. Chromium at 390×844 verified Portugal’s collapsed and expanded sheet, Lisbon focus and exact camera return, sea and Close deselection, back to continent, keyboard selection, drag and pinch exclusion, Iceland’s empty pack, and the shared sheet in `/ui-kit`. Physical iPhone and Android phones, screen readers, and Safari remain unverified.
- [ ] **P5-04 — Add discovery states.** Undiscovered / Discovered / In progress / Completed, derived from records. Opening a country discovers it; collectibles count only when found. Browsing never records travel.
- [x] **P5-05 — Build the "find it" challenge.** A prompt names a landmark or hidden detail; taps are tested against P5-01 shapes. Wrong taps get gentle feedback, and after several misses an assist highlights the area. Finding it opens the collectible card.
  - Wired up the checked landmark packs for Lisbon (4), Paris (6) and Athens (7), a stepped prompt and three-miss assist, a sourced find card, and session records shared with the country sheet. Lint, typecheck and tests passed. At 390×844 in Chromium, verified Lisbon miss feedback, drag and pinch exclusion, two finds updating Portugal to 2 / 4, and all four finds showing completion. Also checked the first find in Paris and Athens and the `/ui-kit` shake with reduced motion on and off. Physical phones, screen readers and small-target touch comfort are pending.
  - **P5-08 playtest:** Paris's Luxor Obelisk is the narrow target flagged by P5-01 (14×64 pt at this viewport). Lisbon's Belém Tower and Castelo de São Jorge start outside the portrait view and required panning to find.
- [ ] **P5-06 — Add collectible cards.** Name, pixel image cropped from the scene or its 4× study, one or two sentences, source link, and date found.
- [ ] **P5-07 — Add versioned local persistence.** Hydration, schema validation/migrations, ordered saves, failure/retry, undo, and explicit reset. Keep transient camera/input state separate; install storage only when used. Verify relaunch, corrupt/unavailable storage, and content updates.
- [ ] **P5-08 — Playtest gate.** Play the slice on a physical iPhone and Android phone. Record: whether "find it" is fun, whether landmarks are big enough to tap, whether UI fits over the scenes, which pipeline changes all capitals need, and the open decisions (XP, body font). Apply pipeline changes before resuming Phase 4.

## Phase 6 — UI kit and game feel

**Dependencies:** the Phase 5 slice, so components are judged on real screens.

**Reordered (24 November 2026):** the kit's foundation (P6-01, P6-03, P6-04) was built before Phase 5, so the gameplay screens are composed from real components instead of throwaway UI, and so Codex and Claude build from one shared set of parts. Components are still to be re-judged on the Phase 5 screens and on phones. Contract: [docs/ui/UI_KIT.md](docs/ui/UI_KIT.md).

- [x] **P6-01 — Game theme tokens.** Replace the template light/dark colours in `src/constants/theme.ts` with one game palette from the art contract (night navy, parchment, gold, progress green, ink). One UI pixel = 2 points; spacing and borders in whole UI pixels; fixed type sizes.
  - *Done (24 November 2026):* `Palette`, `Frames`, `Space`, `Size`, `Type`, `U`/`px()` in `src/constants/theme.ts`, hues taken from the art scripts. The Expo template's light/dark colours, themed components, colour-scheme hooks, `global.css` and the placeholder `/explore` route were removed; the app has one game look in both system modes. `tests/ui-kit.test.mjs` checks every size is whole UI pixels and that interface code uses no raw colours, font sizes, radii, blur or system icons.
- [ ] **P6-02 — Nine-slice frame generator.** A script in `scripts/art/` writes in-palette frames (parchment and navy panels; gold, cream, and green buttons; chip; tab; progress-bar parts) and 16×16 UI icons, drawn with nearest-neighbour sampling.
  - *Changed (24 November 2026):* frames are drawn by `PixelFrame` from Views (outline with notched corners, fill, highlight, shade, lip) instead of nine-slice PNGs: crisp at every density, no assets, one token table. The 16×16 UI icons became 12×12 grids in `scripts/art/build_ui_icons.py` (16 icons, pre-scaled @1x/@2x/@3x). Still open: a parchment grain/torn-edge texture layer, if phones show the plain frames look flat.
- [ ] **P6-03 — Pixel UI components.** `PixelPanel`, `PixelButton` (drops 1 UI pixel when pressed, with a haptic tap), `Chip`, `SegmentedProgress`, `Badge`, `TabBar`, `CollectibleCard`, `Stamp`. Stepped, frame-like motion that respects reduced-motion settings. No frosted glass, gradients, or soft glows.
  - *v1 built (24 November 2026):* `src/ui/`: `PixelFrame`, `PixelText`, `PixelIcon`, `PixelButton`, `IconButton`, `Panel`, `SectionHeader`, `Divider`, `Ribbon`, `Chip`, `ProgressBar`, `StepDots`, `SegmentedTabs`, `TabBar`, `CollectibleTile`, `Badge`, `QuestRow`. Left: haptic tap (needs `expo-haptics`, P6-06), `Stamp` (P7-05), stepped card/stamp animations.
  - *v1.1 (24 November 2026), the parts Phase 5 needs:* `Screen` and `TopBar` (safe areas, header/footer/overlay slots), `BottomSheet` (controlled, tap or swipe the header, at most half the screen, map stays mounted), `PixelModal` with a stepped pop, `CollectibleCard` (kind, image, checked text, source link, date found, "Found!" ribbon), `ConfirmDialog` with a red `danger` button, `NoticeProvider`/`useNotice` (one notice at a time, Undo action, announced to screen readers), `useSteps` (12 fps stepped frames, reduce motion shows the last), and icons info, undo, settings, chevron (20 in all). Verified in Chrome at 390×844 by clicking through every overlay; that found and fixed Undo clearing its own follow-up notice. Not verified: the sheet swipe and Android back button on phones. The rest of the missing parts are listed by phase in [UI_KIT.md → Roadmap](docs/ui/UI_KIT.md#roadmap-what-the-kit-does-not-have-yet).
- [ ] **P6-04 — `/ui-kit` route.** Every component shown over the globe, the Europe board, and a capital scene; checked on phones, then applied to the Phase 5 screens.
  - *Started (24 November 2026):* `/ui-kit` shows every component in a Portugal/Lisbon country sheet, a challenge, a quest list, buttons, type and icons, with labelled demo data. Verified in Chrome at 390×844. Not yet shown over the globe/board/capital, and not checked on physical phones.
- [ ] **P6-05 — Animate the scenes.** Moved to Phase 4b (L-02 to L-04), which started early.
- [ ] **P6-06 — Sound and haptics.** A short chiptune loop per continent; sounds for finding, stamping, and buttons. CC0 or generated audio with sources recorded; mute setting; follows the phone's silent mode. Check the SDK 58 docs before choosing modules.
- [ ] **P6-07 — Real day and night.** Light the globe from the sun's actual position, and tint capital scenes by their local time, with lit windows at night. Computed on the device, no network.
- [ ] **P6-08 — Accessibility pass.** Large-text mode, colour-blind-safe statuses (symbol and number, never colour alone), screen-reader labels, and the "find it" assist.

## Phase 7 — World progress, Quests, and Passport

**Dependencies:** Phase 5 records and Phase 6 components.

- [ ] **P7-01 — Three-tab navigation.** World (the map), Quests, and Passport through Expo Router. The map stays mounted and the camera is preserved.
- [ ] **P7-02 — Clouds over undiscovered countries.** Pixel clouds or fog cover undiscovered countries and clear with a short stepped animation when discovered. Seeded per country; works on the globe and the board.
- [ ] **P7-03 — Daily featured place.** One country or capital per day with 3–5 discoveries, shown in Quests. Document the selection rule (curated or rotating). No penalty for missed days. Optional, opt-in local reminder with no server.
- [ ] **P7-04 — Travel track.** Dated manual visits with undo, a wishlist, and visited pins on the map, shown differently from discovery. Viewing or discovering never records travel.
- [ ] **P7-05 — Generated passport stamps and the Passport.** Rule-built stamps per country (shape, flag colours, main landmark icon, date) from a build script. The Passport holds stamps by continent, visited places, the wishlist, and the collection.
- [ ] **P7-06 — Home country and My World.** Choose a home country once; the globe opens over it. My World shows only visited countries lit, pixel flight arcs between visits, and counts.
- [ ] **P7-07 — Progress and rewards.** Counts with real denominators, completion badges, and stamps, all derived from records. Undo removes rewards; repeating cannot farm them. Add XP and levels only if P5-08 recommends them.
- [ ] **P7-08 — Verify records.** Visit/undo, discovery, relaunch, corrupt storage, content updates, and no progress changes from browsing.

## Phase 8 — Resume capitals and release validation

- [ ] **P8-01 — Resume Phase 4 with the loop.** Every new capital ships with landmark data, collectibles, and "find it" prompts. Apply playtest-driven pipeline changes to the existing capitals. (Rome, the old second-capital task, is already built.)
- [ ] **P8-02 — Validate platforms and accessibility.** Available Android/iPhone and Chromium/Safari; safe areas, targets, labels, reduced motion, sheet/gesture separation, tiny countries, date-line and polar behavior.
- [ ] **P8-03 — Measure the real scenes.** Frame behavior with animation and audio, decoded/GPU/transient memory, cold loading, pack/app disk cost, and five-minute memory trends.
- [ ] **P8-04 — Verify saved/offline behavior.** Installed native packs in airplane mode, relaunch, write failure/reset, reward undo, and content versions. Browser offline reload needs a separately tested cache strategy.
- [ ] **P8-05 — Prepare the preview.** Retain attribution, regeneration instructions, coverage limits, a recorded walkthrough, and passing project checks.

## Phase 9 — After the first release (backlog)

Roughly in priority order; each item needs its own plan before work starts.

- **Spot the animal** — find or identify wildlife in its habitat on the continent board.
- **Map questions** — answered by tapping the map ("Tap the country whose capital is Lisbon"), not multiple choice.
- **Field guide** — a card per animal with a small map of where it lives, from the sourced anchors; completing a continent's wildlife is a long-term goal.
- **Postcards** — a framed pixel postcard of a capital scene with the player's stamp, shared through the system share sheet. No social features needed.
- **Landmark close-ups** — tapping a found landmark shows its 4× study with two facts.
- **Seasonal events** — e.g. cherry blossom in Tokyo in April, Christmas markets in Vienna in December.
- **Home-screen widget** showing the daily place; check SDK support first.
- **Curated cultural packs** — foods, traditions, history, fact-checked per country.
- **XP and levels** — only if playtesting shows progress feels flat.

**Not planned:** leaderboards, multiplayer, or accounts; live online data such as weather; facts generated by AI at runtime; AR; currencies, energy systems, loot boxes, or shops.

**Montenegro / Podgorica (17 November 2026).** Research before drawing: six defining sites, north up/west left, with the Morača descending through a rocky channel from the north towards the south-west and the smaller Ribnica entering from the east at Sastavci. The cathedral and Kruševac palace are on the west/right bank; St George's and Stara Varoš are east/left. Gorica rises behind St George's northern edge; the church sits at its southern foot, not its summit. Distances and river bends are compressed into isometric-axis segments. Planned anchors: cathedral `(135,150)`, St George `(615,180)`, Millennium `(350,260)`, Ribnica `(380,395)`, palace `(170,410)`, clock tower `(510,465)`. Coordinate sources were read on 17 November 2026; OSM coordinates below are bounding-box centres fetched from the [OSM map API](https://www.openstreetmap.org/api/0.6/map?bbox=19.246,42.434,19.269,42.447), independently compared with the linked other source.

Podgorica fact sheets (longitude, latitude; model verification follows after rendering):

| Site / coordinate selected for SITES | Two coordinate sources | Setting, silhouette, defining feature, front and compass decisions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Resurrection Cathedral; **19.248250, 42.445694** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Resurrection_of_Christ,_Podgorica) 19.248250, 42.445694; [OSM way 175501033](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/175501033) 19.2482427, 42.44570935, about 2 m. | Preko Morače, inland west/right bank. Broad white limestone church, rough cyclopean lower blocks and dressed upper courses, **two square flat-capped western bell towers**, central dark dome on a pierced drum, monumental entrance arch and gold-ground Resurrection mosaic; a two-plus-one bell gable crowns the middle western roofline. Main front faces west, explicitly snapped to the visible south-west face; western towers flank that entrance, not the eastern apse. Dome remains dark metal, not invented gold. [Parish photograph](https://crkvenaopstinapodgorica.com/h/saborni-hram-hristovog-vaskrsenja---podgorica) is the exterior reference. |
| St George's Church; **19.267143, 42.445424** | [OSM way 197800217](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/197800217) 19.26714315, 42.44542365; [independent visitor map](https://travel-4-fun.com/montenegro/podgorica-top-15-places-hotels/) gives approximately 19.2671274, 42.4456074, about 21 m; [OSM-derived coordinate display](https://mapcarta.com/19031668) also identifies this building. | East bank, cemetery garden at Gorica's southern foot. Small low single nave, pale dressed stone, clay gable roof, eastern semicircular apse. **Western bell gable has two lower arched bell openings and one upper**, three bells rather than a tall enclosed tower; arched wooden entrance. West front explicitly snapped to south-west. [Parish exterior](https://www.crkvenaopstinapodgorica.com/h/crkva-svetog-jordja-pod-goricom) and [tourist office](https://podgorica.travel/en/the-church-of-st-george/) establish the silhouette and hillside setting. |
| Millennium Bridge; **19.258100, 42.445200** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Bridge_(Podgorica)) 19.258100, 42.445200; [OSM way 28131312](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/28131312) centre 19.25870875, 42.4450678, about 52 m; Wikipedia's infobox 19.259014, 42.444914 is the eastern end, about 82 m from the selected anchor. | Crosses Morača north of Sastavci; alignment west-north-west to east-south-east. **One tall white pylon at the eastern end**, a long low deck, twelve main stay cables and backstays to eastern counterweights. Road/sidewalk parapets and separate cable planes. Model snaps the deck to +i (south-east) and the river to +j, so it crosses squarely; east pylon remains at +i. [Bridge account](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Bridge_(Podgorica)) supplies 173 m length, 57 m pylon and 12 main/24 backstay cables. |
| Old Ribnica bridge; **19.258785, 42.439474** | [OSM way 28157847](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/28157847) 19.25878475, 42.43947430; [independent mapped photograph](https://topgid.net/6693-staryj-most-adzhi-pashi-cherez-reku-rybnica.html) 19.2588233, 42.4394917, about 4 m. | Spans the Ribnica immediately east of its confluence with Morača, below Stara Varoš's rocky terrace; does not cross Morača. **One low, broad stone arch**, worn humped cobbled deck, low parapet/edges and stepped bank approaches; no invented tower. Real near north–south axis snapped to +j (south-west), crossing the east-entering Ribnica segment squarely. Both side faces and arch intrados remain visible. |
| Petrović Palace; **19.250280, 42.437780** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podgorica_Royal_Palace) 19.250280, 42.437780; [OSM way 175500962](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/175500962) 19.2502572, 42.43797545, about 22 m. | Kruševac park, west/right bank inland above the river terrace; it must not sit on the water. **Long low two-storey peach/ochre palace**, white pilasters, shuttered divided windows, central arched entrance with balcony, curved central attic crest, clay hip roof with small dormers and chimneys. No domes or corner towers. Famous entrance faces west (the article describes the western stair/columns/terrace), explicitly snapped to south-west. [Exterior photograph](https://www.portalanalitika.me/clanak/najveci-porodicni-dogadaj-u-zemlji-u-subotu-u-podgorici-peto-izdanje-festivala-roditeljstva) is the colour/roof reference. |
| Sahat Kula / Clock Tower; **19.260014, 42.435557** | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clock_Tower_(Podgorica)) 19.260014, 42.435557; [OSM way 224779824](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/224779824) 19.26006865, 42.43559665, about 6 m. | Stara Varoš on the east bank, **south of Ribnica**, in Bećir Beg Osmanagić square. One tall square rubble-limestone shaft, sparse slit windows, arched upper bell openings, clock dial, low dark roof and small metal cross; no added minaret, domes or companion tower. Ground entrance and visible clock face drawn towards south-west; the precise compass orientation of the dial is unconfirmed. Height sources disagree (19 vs 25 m), so illustration preserves a tall shaft without claiming a measured elevation. |

Country tier: Natural Earth `MNE`, exact capital spelling `Podgorica`, bounds `[18.4, 41.8, 20.4, 43.6]`; scene/camera centre `19.2628, 42.4414`. Natural Earth's pinned populated-places layer includes only Podgorica in Montenegro (19.266307, 42.465973); retain the universal 75 km city exclusion and do not invent additional dataset entries. Seven country-index-filtered olive/pine trees and offshore boats use the existing generator. **Upper Ostrog Monastery** is the single country landmark at `19.02917, 42.67500` ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ostrog_Monastery), independently [pilgrimage inventory](https://srpskispomenici.rs/en/monument/ostrog/) 19.0294, 42.6747, about 38 m): a white arcaded monastery embedded in a high limestone cliff, clay roofs and a dark cave recess. Its sprite will compress the complex, without claiming every cave chapel.

Podgorica visual/model verification, after regenerating and viewing every isolated 4× study beside the Lisbon, Madrid and Paris benchmarks:

| Landmark | Fact-sheet/model check | Remaining detail limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Resurrection Cathedral | **Matches** the west/right-bank setting; two western square towers with flat projecting dentilled caps and arched bell recesses, one principal dark ribbed dome on a windowed drum, rounded eastern apse/side roofs, rough lower/dressed upper stonework, great gold mosaic arch, three doorways, steps and the central two-plus-one bell gable. Its west front is explicitly snapped to +j (south-west). It dominates the neighbouring low houses. | Fine stone reliefs, inscriptions and individual icon faces are compressed into native-pixel motifs; not a facsimile of the paintings. |
| St George's Church | **Matches** the eastern bank and Gorica's southern foot, raised below the wooded hill; low single nave, clay roof, eastern apse, arched entrance, exactly two lower bell arches and one upper, with three dimensional bells and crosses. West front explicitly snapped to south-west, altar at the opposite end. | Interior frescoes are not shown. The small masonry arch rings are simplified at native size. |
| Millennium Bridge | **Matches** a square crossing of Morača, long low road deck/sidewalks and railings, one tall pylon at the eastern (+i / south-east) end, **12 main stays total (six per cable plane)** and **24 backstays (twelve per eastern plane)**, road markings and lights. Not mirrored, not an invented suspension bridge. | Some backstays merge in projection and small anchor/bolt fittings are omitted; no claim of engineering-scale dimensions. |
| Old Ribnica bridge | **Matches** a crossing of the smaller Ribnica immediately east of the Morača confluence, below the old-town terrace; one open masonry arch with voussoirs and a visible shaded intrados, humped cobbled deck, low stone edges and stepped approaches. Near north–south axis explicitly snapped to south-west (+j); river segment is perpendicular (+i). | Uneven individual historic stones are condensed into courses and wedge joints. |
| Petrović Palace | **Matches** Kruševac park west/right of Morača, above and inland from the river; a long low two-storey peach/ochre block with white pilasters, shutters, divided windows, central arched entrance, balcony/balusters, rounded attic crest, three dormers, clay hip roof and two chimneys. Western garden/entry front explicitly snapped to south-west. No added domes or corner towers. | Delicate plaster roundels and interior galleries are omitted. |
| Clock Tower | **Matches** Stara Varoš east of Morača and south of Ribnica; one tall square rubble-stone shaft, slit windows, arched upper bell recesses, a clock dial, low dark cap and metal cross. Entrance/dial illustrated on the south-west face. | Exact dial compass direction is **unconfirmed**. Photos and OSM disagree over roof terminology and height; retain the restrained low dark hip and tall shaft without claiming a surveyed height. |

The low houses have their own six-colour palette, dressed divided windows/sills, doors/shopfronts, plinths/cornices, shutter strips, textured clay hip roofs, chimneys and occasional iron balconies. Broad clearances keep landmarks visible, and all squares, gardens, quays and two small Morača boats were inspected in the complete scene. No remaining silhouette, tower-handedness, river-bank, hill-setting or placement shortcoming was found against the reference material; fine relief/painting detail remains deliberately compressed at the 720×520 illustration scale. Every study is current, including raised terrain under the elevated buildings. `podgorica-ostrog.png` shows the native 48×60 country sprite at 4×; white two-tier arcades and icon niches project from a dark recess in a limestone crag, with a small bell stage and clay roof sections. It is an illustrative selection of the upper complex rather than a count of all cave chapels.

Montenegro's country tier contains **13 new icons**: three at Natural Earth's Podgorica point, Upper Ostrog, five northern pines, two southern olives and two boats along the Adriatic coast. Every new anchor is inside `[18.4, 41.8, 20.4, 43.6]`. Trees retain the existing native-resolution country-index/land filter; boats retain the water/near-country filter and an explicit coastal box. Natural Earth's dataset limitation (only one city) is stated rather than silently adding invented or unspaced towns.

Chrome at **1000×780** and **390×844** passed Montenegro/Ostrog/capital URLs, scroll-wheel arrival (pixel-identical to direct arrival), all four pan-edge stops at both sizes, and adjacent Sarajevo/Zagreb visits. Served Podgorica/atlas bytes match disk SHA-256, with no console or runtime errors. An initial browser run overlapped a PNG art update and its last screenshot comparison changed; the complete run was repeated after freezing final assets and all checks passed.
- Final diff review: only new country/capital art, generated registries/atlas, one camera-coverage row, focused tests and required documentation. No dependencies, navigation/API changes, native project files or continent-board changes. Existing user edits in the development plan, art contract, README and agent docs were preserved. No commit was created.

Not verified: physical iOS/Android devices, the Clock Tower dial's precise compass orientation, and subpixel architectural relief/individual iconography. Native export is build validation, not device rendering verification. The scene is deliberately compressed geography; its north-up landmark order and correct banks/hills are checked, not street-scale distances.


**Albania / Tirane (17 November 2026).** Research and geographic layout precede drawing. Natural Earth uses `ALB` and the capital spelling **Tirana**; the illustrated scene uses the requested **Tirane**, id `tirane`, centre `19.8189, 41.3275`. Shared country/camera bounds are `[19.2, 39.6, 21.1, 42.7]`. Distances are compressed north up/west left. Skanderbeg Square is inland, with the museum on its northern side, Palace of Culture east/north-east, mosque and clock to the east, and statue on the southern edge. The cathedral is south-west of the square; Rinia Park is south of it. The Lana enters from the north-east, bends west-south-west past the historic bridge, passes north of the Pyramid, then leaves to the west. The city centre is flat: no invented castle hill or central lake. Tanners’ Bridge stands on a dry former channel **north-west of the present Lana**, not over the modern channel. No boats belong in this small urban stream.

Tirane fact sheets (longitude, latitude; sources read 17 November 2026). OSM building centres are independently calculated from the [map API](https://www.openstreetmap.org/api/0.6/map?bbox=19.811,41.320,19.830,41.332), using way/relation geometry. The selected coordinate is that geometry’s centre. Wikipedia values are listed separately so the comparison is auditable.

| Landmark / selected coordinate | Two independent coordinate sources | Setting, silhouette, defining feature and compass decision before drawing |
| --- | --- | --- |

Broad low pale stone modernist wings and flat roof; projecting central mosaic panel above six deep piers and a glazed entrance, broad stairs. No towers/domes. Defining feature: the *Albanians* mosaic, 13 figures under a red banner. South-facing square front snapped to south-west so the panel can be seen; no city rotation. Side walls receive slit windows, never a repeated mosaic. |
| Palace of Culture; **19.819638, 41.328812** | [OSM way 1249637844](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/1249637844); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Culture_of_Tirana) 19.819440, 41.328890, about 19 m. | Eastern edge of square, north of mosque.
