West-facing front explicitly snapped to south-west. Bay count is compressed, not claimed exact. |
| Et’hem Bey Mosque; **19.819329, 41.327802** | [OSM way 175108083](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/175108083); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Et%27hem_Bey_Mosque) 19.819170, 41.327780, about 14 m. | East edge of square, west/north-west of clock. One square cream prayer hall, **one low grey lead dome**, **one slender stone minaret** with balcony and pointed metal cap; clay-tiled L-shaped portico on north/east. Defining feature: frescoed landscape/floral portico and arches. The real north/east porch is hidden in this projection; explicitly turn only this building 180° to show it on south/west (lit SW and shaded SE faces), carrying its corner minaret with it. See [official tourism](https://akt.gov.al/en/ethem-bey-mosque/) and [heritage institute](https://www.iett.al/mosque-of-ethem-bey/?lang=en). |
| Clock Tower; **19.819683, 41.327703** | [OSM way 233519333](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/233519333); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_Clock_Tower) 19.819680, 41.327740, about 4 m. | Immediately east/south-east of mosque, inland. Single square rubble-stone shaft, projecting balustraded balcony, cream upper clock stage, **four dials**, open columned bell loggia, dark pyramidal roof and finial. No companion tower. Faces follow snapped tile axes; both visible clock faces are drawn. Shaft must remain taller than houses, and the loggia must be genuinely open. |
| Skanderbeg Monument; **19.818557, 41.327753** | [OSM way 248349387](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/248349387); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg_Monument) 19.818800, 41.327500, about 35 m. | Southern edge of square, west of mosque. One bronze horse/rider on a broad irregular tiered stone pedestal. Defining features: horse’s raised foreleg, heavy mane/tail, armoured rider, cloak and horned helmet, curved sword held beside the body. No invented rearing pair of forelegs or sword overhead. Southward facing inferred from photos/background geography and snapped to SE; precise bearing unconfirmed. Sculpted limbs/torso rather than sticks. |
| Resurrection Cathedral; **19.817597, 41.326551** | [OSM relation 7753718](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/7753718); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resurrection_Cathedral,_Tirana) 19.817500, 41.326390, about 20 m. | Inland south-west of square, north of Lana. Circular pale marble church, **one broad blue dome** and gold cross, arched blue-glazed west entrance and broad stairs. **One separate bell tower south-west** (OSM way 691470743, 19.817096, 41.326261) consists of four candle-like shafts, two open bell stages, four-sided clock and cap. Defining feature: blue entrance arch plus four-candle tower. West front snapped to SW, detached tower stays on its geographic SW side. [Church’s own architectural account](https://orthodoxalbania.org/2026/en/2014/05/29/the-complex-of-new-cathedral-of-tirana-resurrection-of-christ-the-synodical-and-the-cultural-centre/) confirms rotunda, gold copper details, western arch and bell structure. |
| Tanners’ Bridge; **19.826050, 41.326424** | [OSM way 627129048](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/627129048); [Click Albania](https://clickapp.win/tirana/ura-e-tabakeve) 19.826050, 41.326420, less than 1 m. | East of centre, dry former Lana channel north-west of modern stream. Short narrow stone pedestrian bridge: one humped central arch, smaller flood opening at each approach, stone voussoirs, low solid parapets and cobbled deck. No towers/flags. Historic crossing approximately NE–SW, snapped to +j; the arch is seen from the SE side. **Wikipedia’s infobox 19.82000, 41.33000 is about 640 m wrong** and rejected; [Commons geotagged photograph](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tanners%27_Bridge,_Tirana,_Albania_04.jpg) 19.826071, 41.326418 independently confirms the selected site. |
| Pyramid of Tirana; **19.821536, 41.323040** | [OSM way 174510408](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/174510408); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyramid_of_Tirana) 19.821390, 41.323060, about 12 m. | South bank of Lana, east of the main boulevard, south-east of square. Wide, low radial concrete structure, truncated summit/oculus, stepped white radial ribs, recessed sloping glazing and bright coloured cubic pavilions after 2023 conversion. No spire/tower. Defining feature: climbable stepped ribs and colourful boxes, not an Egyptian pyramid. Approximately radial: no single privileged front; retain north-up site. [Architect’s photographed project](https://www.mvrdv.com/projects/312/the-pyramid-of-tirana?photo=23456) is the exterior reference. |

Planned ground anchors: museum `(135,135)`, palace `(490,155)`, mosque `(410,270)`, statue `(330,285)`, clock `(555,300)`, cathedral `(245,375)`, Tanners `(650,390)`, Pyramid `(580,470)`. These preserve all coordinate ranks, including near neighbours. Broad open square, Rinia garden and bank promenades are established before houses. Landmarks will be rendered alone at 4× and checked against the fact sheets before scene integration.

Country landmark: **Krujë Castle**, `19.794087, 41.507026`, [OSM way 226764501](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/226764501), compared with [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kruj%C3%AB_Castle) `19.793890, 41.506940`, about 19 m. A compressed pale limestone fortress on a rocky hill, selected curtain walls and the round/rectangular stone masses of the modern Skanderbeg Museum inside; not every ruin/tower in the complex. The 75 km city filter and existing country-index/land decoration filters remain intact.


Tirane model/visual review, after regenerating every study and viewing it beside the named Lisbon/Madrid/Paris benchmarks:

| Landmark | Rendered signature, count/handedness, setting and front verification |
| --- | --- |
| National History Museum | Broad low stone wings, slit side windows, six open piers, recessed glazing, corniced flat roof, steps and one gold-ground mosaic band with 13 small figures/red banner with a miniature double-headed eagle on the SW square face. North side of square; matches the planned front snap and zero-tower silhouette. Individual portraits/weapons in the mosaic are simplified; their identities cannot be read at native pixels. |
| Palace of Culture | Long low stone-clad hall, deep open square-pier colonnade, recessed divided glazing, soffit, flat roof/parapet, return windows and broad stairs. Remains east/north-east of square. West entrance explicitly snapped to SW. Eleven illustrated bays compress the real colonnade; no invented tower/dome or mosaic on this building. |
| Et’hem Bey Mosque | One lead-grey dome, one stone minaret with projecting balcony, upper shaft/pointed cap, arched prayer-hall windows, clay L-portico, real extruded arches, iron screens and small painted landscape/floral bands. OSM building part 1572163036 independently locates the real minaret NW of the prayer hall; after the documented 180° turn its SE corner is drawn at the +i end, clear of the dome. Fresco motifs remain miniature rather than exact landscape reproductions. Mosque remains west of clock, north of statue. |
| Clock Tower | One dressed rubble shaft, slits/door, projecting balustraded gallery, cream clock stage, two visible white dials with marks/hands, genuinely open four-column bell loggia with hanging bell and pyramidal dark cap. Two far dials naturally hidden. East/south-east of mosque; tile-aligned faces match the documented snap. No second tower or extra dome. |
| Skanderbeg Monument | Irregular tiered stone pedestal; shaded closed meshes give the horse a solid body, three grounded legs, one bent raised foreleg, arched neck, ears/muzzle, mane and tail. Armoured rider has legs/boots, torso, cape behind the horse’s flank, beard, two helmet horns, reins and curved sword beside the body. Whole bronze group has one material-coloured silhouette outline. Statue remains west of mosque and south of it, with southward direction inferred then snapped to SE. Exact bearing, tiny helmet ornament and sculpted facial likeness are unverified/simplified. |
| Resurrection Cathedral | Circular marble body, arched windows, one broad blue dome with gold cross, gold-copper buttress caps, grand blue-glazed entrance arch, bronze doors/cross/wheat motifs and broad stairs. One detached SW bell tower has four stone candle shafts, two open bell stages, small bells, white clock faces and golden cap/cross. West front explicitly snapped to SW; tower stays SW of rotunda and the complex north of Lana. Fine relief, interior iconography and all 16 bells are not individually resolved. |
| Tanners’ Bridge | Short narrow humped cobbled span with one open main stone arch, two small approach arches, extruded intrados, voussoir joints, abutments and low parapets. NE–SW length snapped to +j, arches seen from SE; it rests in dry gravel north-west of today’s Lana. Main opening remains empty, with no water falsely routed through it. |
| Pyramid | Broad low radial concrete shell, truncated summit/blue oculus, 16 white stair flights with projected treads/risers, recessed glazing/mullions and five representative coloured cubic pavilions with dressed openings. South of Lana and east of boulevard; radial form has no single front. The cubes/steps are compressed, not a claim to show every pavilion or tread. Glazing is painted before stair geometry so it cannot erase neighbouring ribs. |

House review: six colour/height variations show framed divided windows/sills, shutter accents, ground doors/shopfronts, plinths/cornices, textured clay hips, capped chimneys and occasional projecting iron balconies with corbels. No plain box fillers. Square paving, cobbled streets, Rinia garden, planted open quays and small highlighted Lana water were checked in the final north-up scene. Terrain remains flat; no invented hill, central lake, stream boats or modern bridge. Projected silhouette reservations preserve all landmarks.

Krujë’s separate 54×66 country study shows the hill, pale curtain, museum’s round/stepped stone masses, framed arched openings and crenellations without clipping. The full fortress’s many ruins are deliberately compressed. No national flag is invented. The atlas appends one frame; earlier indices and RGBA pixels are unchanged.

Detail limits after comparison: the Museum’s individual mosaic identities, mosque’s precise fresco scenes and the monument’s sculptural likeness remain less resolved than the large Paris/Madrid studies. Their defining silhouettes, materials and structural features are present; the miniature decorative details are the remaining simplifications, not claims of exact reproduction.

Albania’s generated country tier adds **23 icons**: Tirana’s castle/two houses; Shkodër and Vlorë’s church/two houses each; Korçë and Sarandë churches; Kukës’s two houses; four pines, three olives, two coastal boats and Krujë Castle. Elbasan, Durrës and Fier are within Tirana’s unchanged 75 km exclusion. Every new icon is inside the shared bounds. Previous continent/country placements, frame indices and atlas-prefix RGBA bytes are unchanged.

**North Macedonia / Skopje (17 November 2026).** Research/layout before modelling: seven landmarks, id `skopje`, centre `21.433462, 42.000006`, Natural Earth `MKD` and exact capital name `Skopje`; shared country/camera bounds `[20.4,40.8,23.1,42.4]`. The Vardar flows from the north-west, bending south-east through the centre. Kale, Mustafa Pasha, Daut Pasha and the Archaeological Museum remain on its **left/north-east bank**; St Clement and Porta Macedonia on its **right/south-west bank**. Kale occupies a raised limestone promontory; the mosque has a smaller elevated terrace beside it. Macedonia Square is south-west of Stone Bridge. Distances compress without changing compass order. No sea, invented central lake or river sailing boats.

Skopje fact sheets, checked 17 November 2026. Selected coordinates are independently calculated bounding-box centres of OSM building/bridge footprints from the [OSM map API](https://www.openstreetmap.org/api/0.6/map?bbox=21.423,41.993,21.440,42.005); longitude precedes latitude. Coordinates from a second source and discrepancies are explicit.

| Landmark / selected coordinate | Two coordinate sources | Setting, silhouette, defining feature and front decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mustafa Pasha Mosque; **21.4354104,42.0020253** | [OSM way 172178304](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/172178304); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Pasha_Mosque) 21.435417,42.001940, 9 m. | Elevated beside Kale, north-east of fortress, above Old Bazaar. Square stone/brick prayer hall, one broad lead dome; one slender limestone minaret with balcony and pointed lead cap at its northern corner. North-west porch has four marble columns/three arches/three small domes ([architectural study](https://www.nazanazeri.com.tr/dosyalar/1._The_Architectural_Development_of_Skop.pdf)). Defining feature: large dome, slender single minaret and triple-domed portico. NW porch would be hidden: deliberately turn this building 90° counterclockwise to face SW, carrying its northern minaret to the western corner. |
| Kale (Skopje Fortress); **21.4335243,42.0008134** | [OSM way 474873958](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/474873958); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skopje_Fortress) 21.434400,42.000000, 116 m. | Coordinate difference reflects this large site's extent: Wikipedia marks its SE sector, not the enclosure's centre; both lie within/at the fortress. [Independent locator](https://aroundus.com/p/8521636-skopje-fortress) 21.433220,42.000440 is 49 m from selected centre. Raised promontory overlooking left bank, west/south-west of mosque. Long low pale limestone/travertine crenellated curtains, square and rounded defensive towers, open archaeological interior; no central palace or conical fantasy keep. Defining feature: long battlement skyline over rock. Selected surviving tower/curtain forms compress the irregular enclosure; not a claim to all towers. Main eastern gate is hidden on NE-facing side; visible SW wall gets arrow slits, not a fabricated entrance. |
| Daut Pasha Hamam; **21.4357236,41.9987239** | [OSM way 172171547](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/172171547); [Wikimedia Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Category:Daut_Pa%C5%A1a_Amam) 21.435717,41.998694, 3 m. | Left bank, south-east below Kale, entrance to Old Bazaar, inland from river. Long low east-west stone/brick double bathhouse: two large western domes and eleven smaller unequal domes to east, no minaret. Defining feature: thirteen lead domes, with roof lights. [Official gallery](https://nationalgallery.mk/daut-pasha-hamam/?lang=en) and [tourism architectural guide](https://tourismmacedonia.gov.mk/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/Otomanskite-spomenici-ENG.pdf) verify use, setting and western men's/northern women's entrances. West entrance snapped to SW; big domes remain west on screen. |
| St Clement of Ohrid Cathedral; **21.4264089,41.9985276** | [OSM way 175265123](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/175265123); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Clement_of_Ohrid) 21.426390,41.998610, 9 m. | West of centre on right/south bank, inland. Broad modern rotunda: central green metal dome, lower spreading shell with scalloped arched concrete ribs, four small corner lanterns; gold crosses, stone infill and recessed glazing. Separate tall tapered pale shaft with recessed arched panels, open bell stage, broad flat canopy and clock cube **NW** of church (OSM way 175265102, 21.4261404,41.9988704), not attached to a facade. Defining feature: overlapping green shells and white parabolic arch ribs. Western entrance snapped to SW; detached belfry retains NW position. The [architectural axonometry, plan and exterior photographs](https://northmacedonia.architectuul.com/work/cathedral-church-st-clement-of-ohrid/) were inspected and corrected an initially wrong open-frame tower before finalisation. |
| Stone Bridge; **21.4332409,41.9971170** | [OSM way 734159692](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/734159692); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Bridge_(Skopje)) 21.433071,41.996992, 20 m. | Across Vardar, Old Bazaar NE to Macedonia Square SW. Long narrow pale stone deck, **thirteen semicircular arches** (heritage-guide count; Wikipedia says twelve), solid low parapets, pointed cutwaters and central mihrab/watch recess. Defining feature: repeated open stone arches and humped walkway ([HAEMUS](https://haemus.org.mk/stone-bridge/)). HAEMUS explicitly counts thirteen; the model follows that heritage account. The conflicting twelve-arch count remains a source limitation. Length snapped to +j (NE–SW), river +i (NW–SE); SE-facing arches visible. No decorative towers or invented flags. |
| Archaeological Museum; **21.4344487,41.9968040** | [OSM way 229930502](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/229930502); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Museum_of_North_Macedonia) 21.434440,41.996670, 15 m. | Immediately on left/NE riverbank, south-east of Stone Bridge. Long low cream neoclassical frontage, central triangular pediment, continuous giant colonnade, **two rounded end pavilions with low dark domes**, glazed recess and riverfront steps. Defining feature: pedimented colonnade and paired round ends, not a compact temple. Front faces SW across water and stays on visible +j face; no city turn. Bay count compressed. |
| Porta Macedonia; **21.4324328,41.9944512** | [OSM way 239422349](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/239422349); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porta_Macedonia) 21.432220,41.994440, 18 m. | Pella Square, inland on right bank, south-east edge of Macedonia Square and south of bridge. Single broad marble triumphal arch, one barrel-vaulted passage, paired engaged columns/pilasters, deep cornices, carved relief panels and attic/round medallion. No rooftop quadriga/statue or dome. Defining feature: monumental single opening under relief-covered attic. NW/SE passage axis snapped to NE/SW for the visible SW main face; building turn explicitly illustrative. |

Planned scene anchors `(x,y; elevation)`: mosque `(555,145;16)`, Kale `(405,225;30)`, hammam `(630,295;0)`, cathedral `(130,330;0)`, bridge `(370,395;0)`, museum `(535,420;0)`, gate `(235,485;0)`. Terrain and river are established first, then original 3D models and isolated 4× studies, then houses outside protected landmark silhouettes.

Country landmark: **St John at Kaneo**, on the rocky west shore above Lake Ohrid, selected `20.7887653,41.1111244`, [OSM way 233274330](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/233274330), compared with [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._John_at_Kaneo) `20.7886583,41.1110861`, 10 m. Small Byzantine stone/brick cross-in-square church, one polygonal central drum/dome, patterned arches, clay roofs and eastern apse, on its cliff above blue lake water. No bell tower. Country tier uses pinned source cities and unchanged 75 km spacing. Only Skopje and Bitola survive: Tetovo is too close to Skopje. North Macedonia is landlocked; no offshore boats.

Skopje model/visual review (all seven studies regenerated and viewed, then compared with the named Lisbon/Madrid/Paris studies and finished scenes):

| Landmark | Signature details drawn; count, compass and setting checks |
| --- | --- |
| Mustafa Pasha | One broad grey lead dome on an octagonal drum, one slender faceted minaret with corbelled gallery/railing and pointed lead cap, four marble columns and three open porch arches under three small domes, dressed arched windows, stone courses/brick ties, door and stairs. The deliberate 90° counterclockwise front turn places the porch SW and carries the northern minaret west. Remains on its raised NE terrace beside Kale, north of all other sites. Fine calligraphic ornament is omitted rather than invented. |
| Kale | Long low crenellated limestone walls, three selected square towers and a rounded western tower, slit openings, cornices, buttresses, open grass/archaeological foundation court. All stand on the raised rocky promontory above left bank; no fictional palace/central keep. The gate faces its hidden eastern side; no invented SW gate. The compressed enclosure represents selected surviving wall/tower forms, not a surveyed count of every ruin. |
| Daut Pasha | Two larger western lead domes and eleven smaller eastern ones (13 total), varied chamber heights, stone/brick walls with dressed arched openings, roof-light nubs and a western entrance snapped to SW. No minaret. All smaller dome centres stay east of both large dome centres in screen space. Remains below/south-east of Kale and inland on left bank. Individual roof-light positions and every room boundary are compressed. |
| St Clement | Rounded central green metal dome with scalloped white eaves/lunettes, a lower spreading green shell with four white arch feet, recessed glazing and stone infill, four small corner lanterns/crosses, doors and stairs. A detached **NW** tapered pale belfry has six arched panel stages, an open bell stage with three little bells, wide flat canopy, two visible clock dials with marks/hands, clock cube and cross. The initial open-frame tower was rejected after viewing architectural drawings and photographs; the final shaft matches those sources. West entrance is snapped SW. Right-bank inland setting remains correct. Shell curvature, taper and iconographic paintings remain simplified at native resolution. |
| Stone Bridge | Thirteen open semicircular extruded arches, voussoir joints, solid low parapets, cobbled/humped deck, five representative pointed cutwaters and a small central stone mihrab/guard recess. The final mihrab cap is stone, not an unconfirmed red tile roof. It crosses squarely along +j over the +i Vardar, NE Old Bazaar to SW Macedonia Square. The thirteen-arch choice follows HAEMUS; Wikipedia's twelve-arch count conflicts and is explicitly unresolved. Not all smaller rosettes/consoles are resolved. |
| Archaeological Museum | Long low pale frontage with recessed divided glazing, seventeen illustrated giant fluted columns and voluted capitals, deep entablature, central triangular pediment/medallion, riverfront stairs, two round end pavilions and two low lead domes/finials. SW river front stays visible on +j; no front turn. The museum remains directly on left-bank quay, south-east of Stone Bridge. Bay count and sculpted relief are compressed; individual relief identities are unreadable. |
| Porta Macedonia | One broad marble barrel-vaulted passage with solid spandrels above the open intrados, paired engaged columns/capitals, plinths, relief-panel fields, deep cornices and an attic medallion. No dome, rooftop quadriga or flag. Stays south of bridge, inland on right bank/Pella Square. Its real NW/SE passage is snapped to NE/SW for a visible SW main face, explicitly separate from the north-up geographic layout. Historical scenes within the small relief panels are abstracted, not accurately reproduced. |

House study: six wall colours, one/two/three-storey variants, dark timber-supported projecting upper floors, dressed divided windows/sills, ground doors/shopfronts, plinths/cornices, deep clay hips with projected tile seams/ridges, capped chimneys and occasional iron balconies. Trees/gardens, slab-paved squares, warm cobbled lanes and open pale quays frame the teal Vardar. Water has highlights but no unconfirmed urban sailing boats. The raised terrains, bridge axis and every bank were visually checked in the final 720×520 scene. No landmark is hidden or clipped.

Kaneo's isolated 52×62 sprite shows the cliff/lake, stone/brick cross-plan church, one polygonal drum/dome, clay roof sections, arched windows, cross and eastern apse. It has padding on all sides (nontransparent native bounds `3,1–50,60`). It has no invented bell tower. Seven country trees consist of two pines and five oaks. The 14 additions are Skopje's castle/two houses, Bitola's church/two houses, seven trees and Kaneo. Natural Earth has no other eligible city after Tetovo's 75 km exclusion.

Detail limits after benchmark comparison: Porta's historical relief scenes, museum's sculpted relief identities, mosque's fine calligraphy and cathedral's icon paintings are less resolved than the larger Paris/Madrid studies. Cathedral shell curvature and belfry taper are compressed, Kale represents selected walls/towers, and Daut's small roof-light positions are illustrative. Their defining structural silhouettes/materials and the researched compass settings are present; no claim is made to exact subpixel decoration. Bridge arch-count sources disagree (heritage account 13, Wikipedia 12); the model follows the former, and a physical survey was not performed.
